![обложка|156](<../attachments/tattva-sandarbha-bs--%D0%BE%D0%B1%D0%BB%D0%BE%D0%B6%D0%BA%D0%B0--ff1feb49b0ac72a8.jpeg>)

The Sandarbhas are one of Jīva Gosvāmī’s major works. Sandarbha literally means “stringing together.” Baladeva explains, “The wise say a Sandarbha is that which possesses various matters of importance which should be known. Bhāgavatam verses are gathered together (sandṛbhyate).”

In this case, it is a literary composition consisting of a series of prose sections mixed with verse mainly from Bhāgavatam. It is in six parts which explain Gauḍiya epistemology, theology and philosophy. Tattva Sandarbha deals with the epistemology, while Bhagavat, Paramātmā and Kṛṣṇa Sandarbhas deal with object of worship (sambandha). Bhakti Sandarbha deals with the process or abhidheya, bhakti, and Prīti Sandarbha deals with the goal or prayojana, prema.

Tattva Sandarbha first explains the various pramāṇas or methods of proof and concludes that śabda or scripture is the strongest. Using scriptural proofs, finally Bhāgavatam is concluded to be the best among all scriptures. The second part of Tattva Sandarbha explains prameya—what is proved by Bhāgavatam: Kṛṣṇa as the object of worship, bhakti as the method and prema as the goal. These topics are expanded in the other Sandarbhas.

# Tattva Sandarbha \[BS\]

## Prama-prakaraṇa

### Text 1

*kṛṣṇa-varṇaṁ tviṣākṛṣṇaṁ sāṅgopāṅgāstra-pārṣadam*  
*yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi su-medhasaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**Chiefly through chanting the intelligent people worship the Lord, black in color, but shining with brilliance, who is accompanied by his associates, weapons, and major and minor limbs. SB** [**11.5.32**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.32)

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

I offer respects to the moon of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya. Having offered respects to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, I make this commentary on the Sandarbhas, called (that which harmonizes everything).

Beginning this work named Bhāgavata-sandarbha, This designates all the Sandarbhas. 1 the author praises the supreme Lord named Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, the worshipable deity of thousands of sampradāyas following him, who is a thousand Gaṅgās filled with the nectar of rare prema directed to Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, which are non-different from the svarūpa of Bhagavān--prema which spread when he appeared as avatāra, and whose form as Bhagavān was confirmed by the internal and external vision of millions of great devotees.

He offers praise through information contained in a verse from Bhāgavatam, which particularly indicates the avatāra to be worshipped by devotees in this Kali-yuga. This verse concerning the form of the Lord to be worshipped in Kali-yuga appears in the Eleventh Canto. The intelligent people of Kali-yuga worship this person who is golden (*akṛṣṇam)* by his complexion (*tviṣā*). His golden complexion is described elsewhere:

*āsan varṇās trayo hy asya gṛhṇato ’nuyugaṁ tanūḥ*  
*śuklo raktas tathā pīta idānīṁ kṛṣṇatāṁ gataḥ*

**Your son Kṛṣṇa appeared in previous yugas with three different colors—white, red and yellow—and now he has appeared in a blackish color. SB** [**10.8.13**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.13)

Thus the verse in the Eleventh Canto confirms what is stated in the Tenth Canto, since the Tenth Canto verse speaks of the avatāra appearing in Dvāpara-yuga as Kṛṣṇa, and the Eleventh Canto verse states that Śukla appeared in Satya-yuga and Rakta appeared in Tretā-yuga. The yellow avatāra mentioned by Garga is the form of Caitanya who appeared in a previous day of Brahmā (since the past tense is used).

The Eleventh Canto describes Kṛṣṇa, with śyama complexion, with qualities of a great king, and having the catur-vyūha, as the form to be worshipped in Dvāpara-yuga:

*dvāpare bhagavāñ śyāmaḥ pīta-vāsā nijāyudhaḥ*  
*śrīvatsādibhir aṅkaiś ca lakṣaṇair upalakṣitaḥ*

**In Dvāpara-yuga the Supreme Lord, with a dark blue complexion, wearing yellow garments, is marked with Śrīvatsa and other distinctive ornaments and bodily features.**

*taṁ tadā puruṣaṁ martyā mahā-rājopalakṣaṇam*  
*yajanti veda-tantrābhyāṁ paraṁ jijñāsavo nṛpa*

**O King! In Dvāpara-yuga men who desire to know the Supreme Lord worship him in the mood of honoring a great king, following the prescriptions of both the Vedas and Pañcarātra scriptures.**

*namas te vāsudevāya namaḥ saṅkarṣaṇāya ca*  
*pradyumnāyāniruddhāya tubhyaṁ bhagavate namaḥ*

**Obeisances to you, Vāsudeva, and to your forms of Saṅkarṣaṇa, Pradyumna and Aniruddha. SB** [**11.5.27-29**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.27)

In Viṣṇu-dharma it is said that the Lord appears in green color in Dvāpara-yuga and dark blue in Kali-yuga. It should be understood that this description applies to Dvāpara-yugas when Kṛṣṇa himself does not incarnate (as the yugāvatāra). When Kṛṣṇa appears, Gaurāṅga appears in the next Kali-yuga. This is the explanation. This means that, without doubt, Gaurāṅga is a special manifestation of Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa himself.

This is what is described in Viṣṇu-dharmottara:

*pratyakṣa-rūpa-dhṛg-devo dṛśyate na kalau hariḥ \|*  
*kṛtādiṣv eva tenaiva triyugaḥ paripaṭhyate \|\|*  
*kaler ante ca samprāpte kalkinaṁ brahma-vādinām \|*  
*anupraviśya kurute vāsudevo jagat-sthitim \|\|*

**The Lord is not directly visible in Kali-yuga. Thus the Lord is described as appearing in the three other yugas. At the end of Kali-yuga, Vāsudeva enters into Kalki among the brahmavādīs and makes his residence in the world.**

Caitanya supersedes the Kali-yuga form by having the unlimited powers of Kṛṣṇa, since it is shown that he inevitably appears at the beginning of Kali-yuga after Kṛṣṇa’s appearance.

The nature of his appearance is made explicit by the description. He is in the category of Kṛṣṇa (*kṛṣṇa-varṇam*) or can be addressed by the two syllables (*varṇa*) “Kṛṣṇa” to indicate that he is none other than Kṛṣṇa. *Varṇa* is used to indicate syllables, as in the following:

*samāhutā bhīṣmaka-kanyayā ye*  
*śriyaḥ savarṇena bubhūṣayaiṣām*

**The kings had assembled, attracted by the beauty of Rukmiṇī, who can be equated with the syllables of the word Lakṣmī, with a desire to take her as their bride. SB** [**3.3.3**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-3/3.3)

Śrīdhara Svāmī says, “The two syllables (*rukmi*) in Rukmiṇī’s name express the same meaning as Śrī.”

Or *kṛṣṇa-varṇam* can mean that Caitanya describes (*varṇayati)* Kṛṣṇa. He sings about him under the influence of joy on remembering Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes of great bliss and teaches glorification of Kṛṣṇa to all people by his great mercy. Or he teaches about Kṛṣṇa by his excellent beauty of golden color. This means that, by seeing Caitanya of golden complexion, everyone has realization of Kṛṣṇa.

Or though he is golden in complexion to everyone’s vision, he is blackish, Śyāmasundara, to special devotees by a particular manifestation (*tviṣā)*. In other words, Caitanya is a direct avatāra of Kṛṣṇa himself because of manifesting the complete form of Kṛṣṇa within himself.

His nature as Bhagavān is made clear with the rest of the description. He is seen at all times with innumerable great devotees (*sāṅga*) from Gauḍa, Varendra, Suhma (parts of Bengal) and Utkala (Orissa). They are identified as his limbs *(aṅga*) because they are most attractive (like the Lord himself). They are identified as his ornaments (*upaṅgas)* because they have great powers (like gems) and they are identified as his weapons because they are always with him. These are his associates (*pārṣadam*). Or he always exists with his associates (*pārṣadam*) like Advaita who are like his limbs, his ornaments or his weapons, since they are the source of the greatest prema.

How do they worship this form of the Lord? They worship using the ingredients of worship (*yajñaiḥ*).

*na yatra vaikuṇṭha-kathā-sudhāpagā*  
*na sādhavo bhāgavatās tadāśrayāḥ*  
*na yatra yajñeśa-makhā mahotsavāḥ*  
*sureśa-loko 'pi na vai sa sevyatām*

**Even Brahmaloka should not be accepted as a residence if there are no rivers of sweet topics about the Lord, no devotees who take shelter of those topics, and no great festivals or sacrifices. SB** [**5.19.23**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-5/19.23)

The ingredients of worship are described. The worship is predominated by singing about Kṛṣṇa, experiencing joy by that singing, in a huge group of people. The abhidheya is clearly indicated by showing that the devotees constantly worship the Lord mainly by saṅkīrtana.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

May I have *rati* continually for the tattva in the form of Caitanya endowed with eternal bliss non-different from himself, for Caitanya who is named the deliverer of the universe, the supervisor of dharma, and who bestows satisfaction just by having slight devotion to him.

May the sun called Ānanda-tīrtha (Madhvācārya) who thoroughly destroyed the deep darkness of Māyāvāda philosophy by a shower of rays in the form of words from the Veda, by whom devotion to Viṣṇu was revealed to the world, remain ever victorious!

I offer respects to the excellent Rūpa and Sanātana, the most learned persons on earth, who are the sun and moon against the massive darkness created by māyāvāda philosophy and who showed the tattva called Govinda, like a jewel in the hand, and to whose feet Lakṣmī surrenders (or whose feet are the color of a blue lotus).

May Jīva, who clarified by the nectar of his words the position of the great lord Kṛṣṇa, whose glory was hidden by the hole of vivarta-vāda, by the dirt of faulty logic, and by the mud of Sāṅkhya philosophy, be my shelter!

Since people out of laziness will not be inclined to a large work, this commentary on the profound Sandarbha has been made brief. The versions of verses which were corrected in the Sandarbha by Jīva will be explained and not other versions which were disapproved by him.

The supreme Lord Vyāsadeva, having revealed Brahma-sūtras and its commentary, Bhāgavatam, then taught the Bhāgavatam to Śukadeva. Desiring to clarify the meaning, Jīva invokes auspiciousness in this verse which points out his object of worship-- a cloud of treasured nectar, an axe for chopping mountains of opposing arguments. Being asked by King Nimi, Karabhājana yogī spoke about the avatāras for Satya, Tretā and Dvāpara-yugas. Saying “Hear about the avatāra for Kali yuga” he then spoke this verse.

Intelligent people worship the Lord even in Kali-yuga. By what means do they worship? They worship the Lord by saṅkīrtana, which is a type of arcana (*yajñāiḥ*). Who is that Lord? His interior is the form (*varṇa*) of Kṛṣṇa. He is not black however by his effulgence (*tviṣā*). Garga said *śuklo raktas tathā pītaḥ idānīṁ kṛṣṇatāṁ gataḥ*: the Lord has come in white, red and yellow colors, and now has come in black color. (SB [10.8.13](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.13)) The remaining color of these is *pīta* or gold like lightning (since Kṛṣṇa who is black and the white and red avatāras had already appeared). His aṅgas are Nityānanda and Advaita. His upaṅgas are Śrīvāsa and others. His weapons are the names of the Lord since the names cut down ignorance. His associates (*pārṣadam*) are Gadādhara, Govinda and others. In their company the Lord becomes powerful. That is the indication.

When Garga spoke of the avatāras he spoke of a past form which was yellow (rather than a future form). The present form of Caitanya Mahāprabhu appears in a Kali-yuga of the twenty eighth cycle of yugas in Vaivasvata Manvantara of Śveta-varāha-kalpa (present day of Brahmā). The dharma for Lord Caitanya is indicated in that period of time in Garga’s words. In other Kali-yugas (other than the twenty-eighth cycle in Vaivasvata Manvantara) it is said that Lord appears sometimes in blackish color and sometimes green like a parrot feather. Jīva has already mentioned that the Lord in other Dvāpara-yugas with green color. If he appears in Kali yuga in green color this would make two green avatāras in succession. Baladeva also mentions the Kali-yuga avatāra to be black or green in his commentary on Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta.2

*itthaṁ nṛ-tiryag-ṛṣi-deva-jhaṣāvatārair*  
*lokān vibhāvayasi haṁsi jagat pratīpān*  
*dharmaṁ mahā-puruṣa pāsi yugānuvṛttaṁ*  
*channaḥ kalau yad abhavas tri-yugo 'tha sa tvam*

**In this way, by appearing in various incarnations as a human being, an animal, a great saint, a devatā, a fish or a tortoise, you maintain the worlds, kill those who cause disturbance, and protect dharma in all the yugas. Great Lord! Since you will appear covered in Kali-yuga, you are called Tri-yuga. SB** [**7.9.38**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-7/9.38)

*āsan varṇās trayo hy asya gṛhṇato ’nuyugaṁ tanūḥ*  
*śuklo raktas tathā pīta idānīṁ kṛṣṇatāṁ gataḥ*

**Your son Kṛṣṇa appeared in previous yugas (according to the avatāra he worshipped) with three different colors—white, red and yellow—and now he has appeared in a blackish color. SB** [**10.8.13**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-10/8.13)

*iti dvāpara urv-īśa stuvanti jagad-īśvaram*  
*nānā-tantra-vidhānena kalāv api tathā śṛṇu*

**O King! In this way people in Dvāpara-yuga glorified the Lord of the universe. Now kindly hear about the form in Kali-yuga, worshipped through methods of worship explained in various Pañcarātra scriptures. SB** [**11.5.31**](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-11/5.31)

Those who carefully reflect are called “intelligent (*su-medhasaḥ*).” *Channa* means that his black color is covered by the effulgence of Rādhā.

### Text 2

*antaḥkṛṣṇaṁ bahir gauraṁ darśitāṅgādi-vaibhavam \|*  
*kalau saṅkīrtanādyaiḥ smaḥ kṛṣṇa-caitanyam āśritāḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**By means of saṅkīrtana in Kali-yuga the people take shelter of Kṛṣṇa-caitanya, who is internally Kṛṣṇa and externally golden and who showed his powers by means of his limbs and associates.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

After ascertaining all of this, the author praises Caitanya as the most excellent goal. Sarvabhauma, the crest jewel of the learned has said:

*kālān naṣṭaṁ bhakti-yogaṁ nijaṁ yaḥ*  
*prāduṣkartuṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanya-nāmā \|*  
*āvirbhūtas tasya pādāravinde*  
*gāḍhaṁ gāḍhaṁ līyatāṁ citta-bhṛṅgaḥ \|\|*

**May the bees of our minds thoroughly lick the lotus feet of the Lord named Kṛṣṇa-caitanya who appeared in this world to reveal bhakti-yoga which had been lost through time.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Explaining the meaning of the previous verse, he explains the reason why they surrender to him. The meaning is clear.

### Text 3

*jayatāṁ mathurā-bhūmau śrīla-rūpa-sanātanau \|*  
*yau vilekhayatas tattvaṁ jñāpakau pustikām imām \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Glory to Rūpa and Sanātana, who, revealing the Lord and his associates for the people of the future, had this book written in the land of Mathurā.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Rūpa and Sanātana had this book written in order to reveal (*jñāpakau)* tattva (Kṛṣṇa.)

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The author offers respects in the form of giving blessings. May Rūpa and Sanātana, who are endowed with a wealth of knowledge, renunciation and austerity *(śrīla*), who are guru and parama-guru, manifest their excellence *(jayatām*) in the land of Mathurā. This indicates that they are the masters of this land. He blesses them to remain excellent there. The word *jayatām* also indicates that all others at that place have excellence. But because these two are the source of that excellence, by offering respects to the two, respects are offered to all others. Since others are included in them, he offers respects to these two. The two are described. They have inspired me to write this book called the Sandarbha, since the work can only be accomplished by great intelligence. They revealed tattva, the Lord, to Jīva. According to Viśva-koṣa *tattva* mean a type of philosophy, the real nature of an object or Paramātmā. They revealed to him the Supreme Lord along with his associates. The word *jñāpakau* takes the accusative (*tattvam*) instead of the locative according to Pāṇini 2.3.70, when it indicates the future.

### Text 4

*ko’pi tad-bāndhavo bhaṭṭo dakṣiṇa-dvija-vaṁśajaḥ \|*  
*vivicya vyalikhad granthaṁ likhitād vṛddha-vaiṣṇavaiḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Their remarkable friend Gopāla-bhaṭta, born in a south Indian brāhmaṇa family, considering the truth from works written by previous Vaiṣṇava ācāryas, wrote this work.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Information has been taken from the works of previous devotees (*vṛddha-vaiṣṇavaiḥ likhitāt*) like Rāmānujācārya, Madhvācārya and Śrīdhara Svāmī. This statement eliminates the idea that the author has independently imagined things.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

This verse explains the antiquity of the work and that he simply refined a previous work. The friend of Rūpa and Sanātana, Gopāla-bhaṭta, considering the works written (*likhitāt*) by ancient devotees like Madhvācārya, taking the essence of that, wrote this book.

### Text 5

*tasyādyaṁ granthanālekhaṁ krāntam utkrānta-khaṇḍitam \|*  
*paryālocyātha paryāyaṁ kṛtvā likhati jīvakaḥ*

**Перевод:**

**I have repeatedly examined the original work, parts of which were in order, parts of which were out of order, and parts of which were incomplete. Putting it in a systematic order, I have written this work.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Reviewing that previous work (*ādyaṁ granthanālekham*) of Gopāla-bhaṭṭa (*tasya*), this living being (*jīvikaḥ*), Jīva Gosvāmī, has put it in order (*paryāyam kṛtvā*) and written the present work. *Grantha,* referring to the Sandarbhas, is a *caurādika* word. By the rule *ṇyāsa-śrantho yuc* (Pāṇini 3.3.107) the verb takes the form *granthanā* which means *grantha.* *Lekha* means writing. What was the nature of that writing? It was in order (*krāntam*). Parts were out of order. Parts were broken or missing *(khaṇḍitam).* By Jīva’s effort, it became meaningful.

### Text 6

*yaḥ śrī-kṛṣṇa-padāmbhoja-bhajanaikābhilāṣavān \|*  
*tenaiva dṛśyatām etad anyasmai śapatho’rpitaḥ \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The person who desires only worship of Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet should see this work. A curse is given to others.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

This work *(etat*) should be seen by the person whose chief (*ekābhilāṣavān*) desire is worship of the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

This verse explains the confidential nature of the work. If a person does not respect the supreme position of Kṛṣṇa, it is inauspicious for the person to read this work. This work was written to give auspiciousness (to qualified persons). It is not restricted for study because of fear of imperfections in the work (which some would criticize), since it has been examined and found to be without fault by the most knowledgeable scholars.

### Text 7

*atha natvā mantra-gurūn gurūn bhāgavatārthadān \|*  
*śrī-bhāgavata-sandarbhaṁ sandarbhaṁ vaśmi lekhitum \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Paying respects to my mantra-guru and gurus who have given the meaning of Bhāgavatam, I desire to write this** ***sandarbha*** **named Bhāgavata-sandarbha.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

I desire (*vaśmi)* to write a *sandarbha* composition named Bhāgavata-sandarbha.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Since it is the revelation of the secret and the most essential knowledge (*athaḥ*), I desire (*vāśmi*) to write this Sandarbha, named Bhāgavata-sandarbha. The wise say a Sandarbha is that which possesses various matters of importance which should be known. Bhāgavatam verses are gathered together (*sandṛbhyate). Sandṛbh (*to gather) becomes *sandarbha* by the rule *halaś ca.* (Pāṇini 3.3.121)

### Text 8

*yasya brahmeti saṁjñāṁ kvacid api nigame yāti cin-mātra-sattāpy*  
*aṁśo yasyāṁśakaiḥ svair vibhavati vaśayann eva māyāṁ pumāṁś ca \|*  
*ekaṁ yasyaiva rūpaṁ vilasati parama-vyomni nārāyaṇākhyaṁ*  
*sa śrī-kṛṣṇo vidhattāṁ svayam iha bhagavān prema tat-pāda-bhājām \|\|8\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**May Śrī-kṛṣṇa, Bhagavān Svayam, whose existence as pure consciousness is defined in some scriptures as Brahman, whose aṁśa, the puruṣāvatāra, controlling māyā, shines with his expansions, and one of whose forms called Nārāyaṇa plays in Vaikuṇṭha, give prema to those who worship his feet in this world!**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

The author shows the meaning of the whole work in summary with an invocatory verse. The word *api* indicates the main meaning of Brahman, which includes statements like *api satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma:*  Brahman is eternal, infinite knowledge. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad [2.1.2](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.1.2)) The Lord’s aṁśa, the puruṣa *(pumān),* the antaryāmī of all beings called Paramātmā, manifests with the guṇāvatāras and līlāvatāras (*aṁśakaiḥ*). A form other than Kṛṣṇa (*ekam rupam*) called Nārāyaṇa, the lord of Vaikuṇṭha ( the spiritual sky) and lord of Lakṣmī as described in Padma Purāṇa, perform pastimes in Vaikuṇṭha. Nārāyaṇa, though he is also Bhagavān, belongs to (*yasya*) Kṛṣṇa. This indicates that Kṛṣṇa is Svayam Bhagavān. This hints at the verse in Bhāgavatam which proves the point: *kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam*. (SB [1.3.28](https://vedarama.com/en/books/sb-1/3.28)) The word *śrī* in *śrī-kṛṣṇaḥ* indicates his svarūpa-śakti, his constant companion. The word *iha* means “in this world.” May Kṛṣṇa, Svayam Bhagavān, along with his svarūpa-śakti, give or manifest (*vidhattām*) prema or intense affection to those who worship his lotus feet in this world.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In this verse he speaks briefly of the work’s anubandhas such as *viṣaya* (subject) in order to create taste in the listener. Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa should give (*vidhattām*) prema to persons who serve his lotus feet in this world (*iha*). Who is he? Belonging to Kṛṣṇa, who is endowed with vibhūtis, qualities and form related to his svarūpa (*yasya*), is that entity which exists as mere knowledge devoid of various qualities (*cin-mātra-sattā)* and which sometimes becomes defined as Brahman in the scriptures. For example it is said *satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma:* Brahman is eternity and infinite bliss. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad [2.1.1](https://vedarama.com/en/books/taittiriya/2.1.1)) and *astīy evaupalabdhavyaḥ*: it will be realized as “It is.” (Kaṭha Upaniṣad). The meaning is “Kṛṣṇa becomes contemplated and perceived as mere knowledge.” It should be understood that for persons with bhakti he is perceived as a person with various qualities, since after being described as *satyam jñānam* he is described as the *anandamaya-puruṣa*, the person composed of intense bliss. Jitanta Stotra says:

*na te rūpaṁ na cākāro nāyudhāni na cāspadam \|*  
*tathāpi puruṣākāro bhaktānāṁ tvaṁ prakāśate \|\|*

**You have no form, no shape, no weapons and no abode. But you reveal your human form to the devotees. Jitanta Stotra**

And the Lord is not accepted by ancient authorities to be formless Brahman since the Lord himself is not attracted to that impersonal form.

The puruṣa (*pumān),* the thousand headed Saṅkarṣaṇa lying in the Causal Ocean, an aṁśa of Kṛṣṇa and the controller of prakṛti, controls māyā and appears with his aṁśas. Establishing māyā under his control, agitating her by his glance and creating through her the universe, he becomes Pradyumna with a thousand heads in the universes half-filled with water, and appears along with his aṁśas like Matsya. *Vibhavati* means to manifest his various līlāvatāras who are called *vibhavas.* Belonging to Kṛṣṇa (*yasya*), the one chief form called Nārāyaṇa performs pastimes (*vilasati*) in the spiritual sky situated outside the eight coverings of the universe. *Vilasati* also means he is a vilāsa form of Kṛṣṇa. *Vilāsa* is defined as a vibhūti with almost equal qualities as Kṛṣṇa, Svayam Bhagavān, who depends on no one else. appears to be different because of a different form. This will be clarified in Kṛṣṇa Sandarbha.

**Pramāṇa Prakaraṇa (section on means of true knowledge or proof)**

### Text 9

*athaivaṁ sūcitānāṁ śrī-kṛṣṇa-tad-vācya-vācakatā-lakṣaṇa-sambandha-tad-bhajana-lakṣaṇa-vidheya-saparyāyābhidheya-tat-prema-lakṣaṇa-prayojanākhyānām arthānāṁ nirṇayāya tāvat pramāṇaṁ nirṇīyate \| tatra puruṣasya bhramādi-doṣa-catuṣṭaya-duṣṭatvāt sutarām alaukikācintya-svabhāva-vastu-sparśāyogyatvāc ca tat-pratyakṣādīny api sadoṣāṇi*

**Перевод:**

**In order to delineate the meaning of the ;sambandha or relationship between the subject signified, Kṛṣṇa, and the elements signifying it (*****vācya-vācakatā*****); This indicates the works’ ability to express the subject. This is the meaning of** ***sambandha*** **as one of the four anubandhas. The other elements are** ***abhidheya*** **or action recommended*****,*** **the** ***prayojana*** **or result, and the** ***adhikārī*** **or qualified person. 3 abhidheya, the prescribed action of worshipping Kṛṣṇa; and the prayojana or goal called prema-- the means of proof (*****pramāṇa*****) should first be established.**

**Since humans are subject to the four faults (*****bhrama, pramāda, karaṇāpāṭava*****, and** ***vipralipsā*****) and thus are incapable of understanding something which by its nature is inconceivable and astonishing, their sense perception and other means of knowledge are faulty.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

There are ten proofs: *pratyakṣa, anumāna, śabda, arṣa upamāna, arthāpatti, abhāva, sambhava, aitihya* and *ceṣṭā.* But the root *pramāṇa* is *śabda--* words without the faults of *bhrama, pramāda, vipralipsā* and *karaṇāpāṭava.* Since the proof or semblance of proof resulting from the other nine methods gives false perceptions, being subject to the four faults, such proof cannot give true ascertainment of the highest goal to humans.  *Śabda* however is without these faults. The other proofs are dependent on *śabda* just as servants are dependent on a king, whereas *śabda* is independent, just as the king is independent of the servants.

Sometimes, the other proofs can support *śabda* according to their ability, but because *śabda* is independent it can override the other proofs. It is impossible for the other proofs to contradict matters indicated by *śabda.* And in matters that are not subject to their abilities, *śabda* is the only effective means.

Sense perception or *pratyakṣa* is of six types: by the five senses and mind/intelligence. These six have two types each: determinate knowledge and indeterminate knowledge. Savikalpaka or determinate percection means one perceives an object with recognition of its identity. “This is a tree.” Nirvikalpaka or indeterminate perception means one perceives an object without identifying it.4 Thus there are twelve types. As well, these types may be classed as *pratyakṣa* belonging to a learned person or an unlearned person. The *pratyakṣa* of the learned person is without the four faults which create error and is based upon *śabda.* The *pratyakṣa* of the unlearned person is doubtful. Its knowledge is subject to fault. On seeing a shaved head created by illusion the foolish person thinks “This is the shaved head of Devadatta (dead).”

The knowledge gained by *śabda* is not faulty. For example it is said, “There is snow in the Himalayas. There are jewels in the ocean.” Such statements of *śabda* are firmly rooted in truth. If a person has been fooled by a false shaved head, then even when the head is real he may not believe it. However if a voice from the sky (*śabda*) says “This is real” he will believe it. All knowers of nyāya (logic) firmly believe that it is not possible to determine anything as truth without respect for the wisdom of elders.

*Śabda* is however independent of the other proofs. In the statement “You are the tenth person” the correct knowledge “I am the tenth person” removes obscuring māyā just by entering the ear. Ten persons cross a river. After crossing one person counts to see if all arrived safely. He counts only nine. Another also counts only nine. A bystander then says “The tenth person is you.”5 It is clearly independent. *Pratyakṣa* assists *śabda* according to its ability as in the example “Fire is the cure for cold.” (This can be proved by experience of the senses.) This is not like the following statement (which cannot be tested by observation). “You, son, were born in my womb in Mathurā.”Devakī speaks to Balarāma.6

*Śabda* can override *pratyakṣa.* For instance there is a mantra “There is no poison in you, though you were bitten by a snake.”

What is stated by *śabda* cannot be disproved by *pratyakṣa*. For example it is said in Ayurveda, “Ashes made from gold are sticky.” However, *śabda* is the only resort in describing activity within the human body or the movement of the planets.

One may argue that whatever is testified by everyone through *pratyakṣa* must be true. However this is defeated by the fact that everyone cannot be gathered in one place for observation of a particular object or event. If one argues that many persons’ senses operating many times can arrive at the truth, this is defeated by the fact that there is often wrong perception of an object in a particular place or by some man-made scripture (among the many persons operating at many times).

*Anumāna* is composed of five parts—*pratijñā* (proposition), *hetu* (reason), *udāharaṇa,* involving vāpti (example), *upanaya* (application) and *nigamana* (conclusion). *pratijñā* (proposition): there is fire on the mountain. *hetu* (reason): because of smoke. *udāharaṇa,* involving vāpti (example): where there is smoke there is fire, as in the kitchen. *upanaya* (application): the mountain has smoke. *nigamana* (conclusion): there is fire on the mountain.7 But it does not produce certain knowledge. There may be *viṣama-vyāpti* (nonequipollent concomitance or unequal extension). For example there may be smoke rising profusely from a mountain for a long time, though the fire has been extinguished by rain. Smoke and fire have a different range of conditions for being present.8 Still one may conclude, “There is fire on the mountain.” Or in the rainy season, the mountain may have lots of mist that looks like smoke. *Śabda* is not subject to these logical faults. For example it is said, “Fire arises from contact with a sun stone.”

Correct *anumāna* is rooted firmly in *śabda.* “O cold traveler! Do not believe that there is fire because of smoke. We have seen that the fire has been extinguished by rain.” (*śabda*) “But I see fire on the mountain which gives off smoke.” “That is only an image in the smoke. But over there one can obtain fire.” (*śabda*)

One may argue that the cause of lack of truth is because of some false *anumāna;* true *anumāna* does not err since there is a generic form for making inference. For instance, one could understand that what appeared to be smoke was only vapor from a poisonous mountain because a person developed pain in his eyes. (From this it would be concluded that there was no smoke and thus no fire.)

In answer to this the following may be said. Smoke does not arise in all cases from fire. It is possible that vapors arose from smoke which long ago disappeared. One attains correct knowledge of fire only from perceiving real fire which produces real smoke or perceiving no fire which produces fake smoke. (To prove real smoke we must perceive real fire.) By mixing of sādhya (major term) and sādhana (premise) to gain knowledge of smoke, there is a mutual dependence of the terms. (To prove fire we must prove smoke. To prove smoke we must confirm fire.) This is a fault.

Since there is uncertainty in *pratyakṣa* regarding its truth, *anumāna* is unreliable even in the case of equal extension (*sama-vyāpti*). On the other hand, *śabda* is independent as in “You are the tenth person.” According to an individual’s competence, *anumāna* assists *śabda.* For example one may infer that a diamond can be cut by iron because it is made of earth if one has not seen the particular quality of the diamond. But if one hears about the qualities of a diamond *(śabda*), one would not make such a mistaken inference.

Here are examples of *śabda* overriding *anumāna.* “A limb burned by fire can be relieved by the heat of fire. Dry ginger becomes sweet when cooked by the fire of digestion.”

What is proposed by *śabda* cannot be opposed by *anumāna.* For instance it is said, “This medicine destroys the three doṣas.”

*Śabda* is effective in matters that cannot be touched by the power of the senses and mind. An example is the movement of planets. If *pratyakṣa* and *anumāna* are dubious, then the other types of proof can also be disregarded, since *pratyakṣa* and *anumāna* themselves must follow after *śabda* (for correct knowledge).

The other proofs (methods of gaining knowledge) will be explained in order to gain knowledge of the correct method of proof. *Ārṣa* means words of the devatās or sages.  *Upamāna* is knowledge based on similarity. “A gavaya is similar to a cow.” *Arthāpatti* is making a conclusion (*āpatti*) from words and circumstances (*artha*) with the form “It cannot be otherwise.” “Since he is fat, and does not eat during the day, he must eat at night.”  *Abhāva* is not perceiving something. If the senses do not contact an object, it is assumed that it does not exist at that place. *Sambhava* is a probability (*sambhāvana*) using intelligence. “A hundred must exist in a thousand.” *Aitiya* is knowledge gained by tradition from unknown speakers. *Ceṣṭā* is gesturing to indicate something, such as using the fingers to indicate ten jars.

Moreover, knowledge gained through a method like *pratyakṣa* cannot prove anything spiritual. This is the case of all animals. Their senses will accept or reject based on what is desired or detested. They can never accomplish spiritual perfection. And on the other hand it is seen that some small children have all knowledge, caused by the *śabda* gained from their parents. Without that, those who have been kept in isolation are dumb and cannot speak. They cannot even act properly.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In order to delineate the four topics which have been already indicated *(sūcitānām),* the proof is first delineated. These are the topics. Kṛṣṇa is the subject or viṣaya. The sambandha is the relationship between Kṛṣṇa and the means to express this (the text of this work). The abhidheya, defined as “that which should be done (vidheya),” is worship in the form of hearing, chanting etc. The prayojana or puruṣārtha is prema. Since the work is dedicated solely to describing Kṛṣṇa, it fulfills the sambandha. *Saparyāya* means “having the same meaning.” *Samāna* or *saha* is combined with *paryāya* to become *saparyāya*.  *Sa* is a substitute for *saha* according to the rule *vopasarjanasya.* (Pāṇ 6.3.82)

*saha-śabdas tu sākalpa-yaugapadya-samṛddhiṣu \|*  
*sādṛśye vidyamāne ca sambandhe ca saha smṛtaṁ \|\|*

**The word** ***saha*** **means doing simultaneously, increase, similarity, or having a relationship. Śrīdhara**

Since the person in the material world is overcome with faults like bhrama and cannot touch the highest spiritual object, his sources of knowledge like sense perception are faulty. The four faults of the jīva are bhrama, pramāda, vipralipsā and karaṇāpaṭava. Bhrama means thinking something is something else. For instance one thinks a tree stump is a person. Pramāda is having an inattentive mind. For instance some one sings close by but one does not hear the singing because of thinking of something else. Vipralipsā is the desire to cheat. For example, though knowing the meaning, one does not reveal the meaning to a student. Karaṇāpaṭava is the limited nature of the senses. For instance, one may concentrate with the mind but one cannot understand an object as it really is.

The faults of the jīva who tries to prove something spread to the proofs. Among them bhrama, pramāda and karaṇāpaṭava belong to senses perception. Based on that, there is inference. They are all faulty. Vipralipsā is testimony.

There are eight proofs starting with sense perception. Sense perception or pratyakṣa means using senses like the eye to contact an object. *Anumiti-karaṇam anumānam*: inference or anumāna is the instrument of inferential cognition. For instance one infers fire from knoweldge of smoke, the cause.  *Śabda* means trusted testimony. *Upamiti-karaṇam upamānam*: upamāna is knowledge gained through observation of similarity. “A gavaya is like a cow.” *Saṁjñāsaṁjñi-sambandha-jñānam upamitiḥ*: upamiti means knowledge of the relationship between a name and an object. (All the above quotations are from Tarka-saṅgraha.) The instrument *(karaṇam)* is knowledge of similarity.

*Arthapatti* (circumstantial inference) means to infer a cause for a certain result, since other causes are not available. Since a person is fat and one does not see him eat during the daytime, one infers that he must eat at night. *Anupalabdhi* (non-perception) means cognition of non-existence. For instance by not seeing a pot on the earth, one infers that a pot does not exist. *Sambhāvana* (probability ) means to infer that a hundred must exist in a thousand. *Aitihyam* is knowledge handed down from unknown speakers. For instance “There is a Yakṣa in the tree (because others have said this.)” These are the eight types of proof.

### Text 10

*tatas tāni na pramāṇīty anādi-siddha-sarva-puruṣa-paramparāsu sarva-laukikālaukika-jñāna-nidānatvād-aprākṛta-vacana-lakṣaṇo veda evāsmākaṁ sarvātīta-sarvāśraya-sarvācintyāścarya-svabhāvaṁ vastu vividiṣatāṁ pramāṇam \|\|10\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus sense perception and other means of knowledge cannot be used as proof. The Vedas, characterized by non-material words, are the proof for persons desiring knowledge of that entity who by nature is above all beings, is the shelter of all beings, and is most inconceivable and astonishing. This knowledge has been handed down through all devatās and humans who sprung from Brahmā and all the later dynasties, time and time again, with no beginning (*****sarva-paramparāsu)*****, since the Vedas contain all material and spiritual knowledge (karma and jñāna).**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Since *śabda* was concluded to be the proof, one must consider the nature of *śabda.* It cannot end simply with the definition “*Śabda* is words devoid of defects like *bhrama*.” Since everyone will conclude “My knowledge is devoid of fault” it will not be possible to discern the real truth. This type of *śabda* is understood through *pratyakṣa* and follows after someone else’s *pratyakṣa.* Thus it is not acceptable as proof.

That which is replicated by all individual learning, that which, by knowing, all people become completely knowledgeable, that which makes *pratyakṣa* and other methods become pure with the highest knowledge, and that which is self accomplished, without beginning, which is all *mahāvākyas* and the root of all traditional knowledge, should be accepted as *śabda.* That *śabda* is scripture and the Vedas. It is knowledge which is as eternal as the Vedas.

Those statements with no human origin, arising again and again in each creation from the Lord who is the cause of all causes--statements which are present without beginning--are without the faults such as *bhrama.* All people should accept the scriptures for teachings about the highest truth. They are unfailing proof. By the Lord’s mercy anyone can accept the scriptural teachings. Why should fools with coarse logic not accept them and accept instead false statements? One must accept scriptures which teach about the Lord.

One may argue “Scriptures like Ayurveda which accept the Lord should not be accepted, since they are like other works, without solid proof.” That is not so. Other works are also accepted as scripture if they follow after the Vedas.

One should not say that because Buddha is also the Lord that his words are proof. The same scriptures which state that he is the Lord also say that he wrote scriptures to bewilder the demons.

In the Bhāmatī commentary Vācaspati accepts the Vedas as proof.

“It is not proper to say that, because the Vedas are contrary to *pratyakṣa* which is a prior source of knowledge, the Vedas depend on *pratyakṣa* and are therefore are not authoritative or must be interpreted with *lakṣaṇā.* The Vedas do not depend on anything else for producing knowledge as their effect, since the Vedas are self accomplished proof, being without human authorship *(apauruṣeya*)—which means that all doubts about their faults should be removed.

“One may argue that since what is proved should be independent, the Vedas depend on *pratyakṣa* for producing truth and where contrary to *pratyakṣa* they do not produce truth at all. Thus they are not be accepted as proof. That is not so because the producer (the Lord) is beyond all obstacles or faults. The knowledge derived from the Vedas does not contradict the authority of *pratyakṣa* even in affairs related to daily life *(saṁvyavahārikam*). And in subjects beyond the scope of the senses, the Vedas cannot be contrary to perception. Rather the Vedas are the source of truth whereas *pratyakṣa* does not produce such knowledge.

“One sees production of true knowledge from the Vedas even in dealing with material items in comparison to unreliable methods of proofs. For instance, short and long sounds (which are not truth in themselves) on being manifested as words are the cause of producing truth. Material word like *nāga* and *naga* meaning elephant and tree do not create confusion.

“One should not say that statements which are independent of all other things should be interpreted. *Na vidhau paraḥ śabdārthaḥ*: as a rule one should not interpret a word to have another meaning. (Pūrva-mīmāṁsa 1.2.19)

“Previous knowledge is annulled by contrary later knowledge rather than previous knowledge annulling later knowledge. Knowing an object to be silver comes first. Knowing the object is actually shell comes later, and cancels the previous knowledge. Without removing the previous knowledge which should be removed, one cannot arrive at truth. It has thus been shown that truthful *pramāṇa* does not depend on other knowledge (previous *pratyakṣa)*. In this regard it is said *pūrvaṁ daurbalyaṁ prakṛtivat*: the previous knowledge is weaker by nature. (Pūrva-mīmāṁsa 6.5.54)

*paurvāparya-balīyastvaṁ tatra nāma pratīyate \|*  
*anyonya-nirapekṣāṇāṁ yatra janma dhiyāṁ bhavet \|\|*

**The later knowledge is perceived to be stronger than the former where the two cognitions arise without dependence on each other. Tantra-vārtika 3.3.2” (End of Bhāmiti quotation)**

The word *sāṁvyavahārikam* in the above commentary means “completely material.’ Scripture is to be employed since one sees that sometimes knowledge gained by *pratyakṣa* is sublated by later knowledge of scripture. For instance one can see the sun globe through *pratyakṣa* to be small but the knowledge is later sublated by inference and *śabda.* Seeing objects at a distance to be small is annulled by the details given in scripture.

Śrī Vaiṣṇavas say the following concerning this. Material perception such as *pratyakṣa*, full of ignorance, is confined to the area of ignorance related to material objects. The Vedas however are not full of ignorance and are proof even in matters concerning the world since they are without human authorship and are eternal. As well, jīvanmuktas existing in this world until they give up their bodies constantly recite the Vedas and it will be shown that associates of the Lord, ātmārāmas, endowed with the citśakti and situated beyond ignorance, like the Lord himself, by the mercy of the Lord recite the Vedas with the bliss of bhakti which surpasses the bliss of Brahman. And the supreme Lord promotes creation of the universe constantly while adhering to the rules of his own Veda. Those who take the Vedas as material information composed by human knowledge cannot produce true knowledge since they lack nonhuman sources of knowledge and are subject to faults like *bhrama.* They produce only material knowledge like speech in a dream. Such ideas are non-Vedic.

“How can the Vedas be eternal since we see that the Vedas arise as discussions of recent people?”

*ata eva ca nityatvaṁ*

**Therefore the Vedas are eternal. Brahma-sūtra 1.3.29**

Śaṅkara quotes the following śruti in this regard in his commentary on the above sūtra:

*yajñena vācaḥ padavīyam āyan tām anvavindy ṛṣiṣu praviṣṭāṁ*

**Drawing out the words of the Vedas dwelling within the sages by sacrifice, the sages merely revealed the Vedas. Ṛg Veda 10.71.3**

*yugānte 'ntarhitān vedān setihāsān maharṣayaḥ \|*  
*lebhire tapasā pūrvam anujñātāḥ svayambhuvā \|\|*

**Instructed by Brahmā, the sages at the end of the yuga obtained through austerity the Vedas which had previously disappeared, along with the Itihāsas. Mahābhārata 12.203.17**

Thus, the sages are not the producers of the Vedas. Rather the Vedas, which are eternal, entered the sages and then appeared through them. Similarly the names and forms arise in each day of Brahmā according to the descriptions in the eternal Vedas.

*samāna-nāma-rūpatvāc cāvṛttāv apy avirodho darśanāt smṛteś ca*

**There is no contradiction because after** ***pralaya*** **things are created again with the same names and forms. This is confirmed in śruti and smṛti. Brahma-sūtra 1.3.30**

Madhva in his commentary gives the following śruti.

*sūryācandramasau dhātā yathā-pūrvam akalpayat*

**The creator made the sun and moon as previously. Ṛg Veda 10.190**

*tathaiva niyamaḥ kāle svarādi-niyamas tathā \|*  
*tasmān nānīdṛśaṁ kvāpi viśvam etad bhaviṣyati \|\|*

**There is regulation of time and regulation of** ***svāras*** **(intoned Vedic syllables). Therefore there will be no disimiliarity in the universe in the future.**

*anādi nidhanaṁ cāhur akṣaraṁ param eva ca \|*  
*ādau veda-mayī divyā yataḥ sarvāḥ pravṛttayaḥ \|\|*  
*ṛṣīṇāṁ nāmadheyāni yāś ca vedeṣu sṛṣṭayaḥ \|*  
*veda-śabdebhya evādau nirmame sa maheśvaraḥ \|\|*

**In the beginning Brahmā spoke the supreme, attractive words of the Veda without beginning and end, from which all creation took place. The great Lord carried out the creation and created the names of the sages mentioned in the Vedas from the words of the Vedas. Mahābhārata 12.217.49**

A śruti showing the creation through *śabda* is given in Śārīrika-bhāṣya:

*etā iti vai prajāpatir devānusṛjata, asṛgrān iti manuṣyān indava iti pitṝn*

**Saying the word** ***etā*** **Brahmā created the devatās. Saying** ***asṛgrān*** **he created humans. Saying** ***indava*** **he created the Pitṛs. Chāndogya-brāhmaṇa**

*sa bhūr iti vyāharat sa bhūmim asṛjata*

**Saying Bhū, he created earth. Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa**

*vedena nāma-rūpe vyākarot satāsatī prajāpatiḥ*

**Brahmā created names and forms in gross and subtle modes through the Vedas. Taittirīya-brāhmaṇa**

*autpattikas tu śabdenārthasya sambandhaḥ*

**The relation of an object denoted with a specific word is eternal. Pūrva-mīmāṁsa**

Thus, the Vedas are an independent proof.

*śabda iti cen nātaḥ prabhavāt prayakṣānumānābhyām*

**If one argues that the words of the Vedas denoting devatās are not eternal, the answer is no, since beings are created from eternal words. This is shown in śruti and smṛti statements. Brahma-sūtra 1.3.28**

The arrangement for conversations in the Vedas makes it easier for the listener to understand. In this way the Vedas must be considered proper. The scriptures called the Vedas are thus the real proof. Scriptures which are not related to the Vedas by lacking its qualities and making contrary statements are not proof.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Therefore, because they are endowed with the four faults, these are not proofs concerning the Supreme Lord. These methods starting with sense perception are not means of proving the Supreme Lord. Seeing an illusory shave head of a man one thinks it is real. In this way sense perception is erroneous. One may conclude that because of smoke there is a fire on the mountain. However previously the fire was extinguished by rain, and the mountain gave off smoke long after. Thus inference is faulty.

*Apta-vākya* or trusted testimony may be perceived as faulty because the meaning given by one trusted sage can be contradicted by the opinion of another learned sage. *Nāsav ṛṣir yasya mataṁ na bhinnam*: sages cannot show the path because each has a different opinion. (Mahābhārata) Because these are the main methods of proof, the others which depend on these, such as *upamāna,* are also faulty.

However real *āpta-vākya* (*śabda*) is a proof for understanding material matters, in statements such as “There is snow on the Hīmālayas.” It is also independent of sense perception or inference as in “You are the tenth person.” The statement has its effect without using sense perception or inference. Another example is “The planets move in the zodiac signs.” Though one may not believe a certain shaved head is real, if a divine voice says “It is real” then sense perception is confirmed by *āpta-vākya*. Someone may say “O traveler suffering from cold! Do not hope for fire because it has been extinguished by rain. But on the mountain over there from which smoke is pouring there is a fire.” Here *āpta-vākya* confirms inference.

Manu says that sense perception, inference and *śabda* are the three proofs:

*pratyakṣam anumānaṁ ca śāstraṁ ca vividhāgamam \|*  
*trayaṁ suviditaṁ kāryaṁ dharma-śuddhim abhīpsatā \|\|*

**A person desiring purity of dharma should use the well known three proofs: sense perception, inference and various scriptures. Manu 12.105**

This includes our ancient commentators as well. *Sarva-paramparāsu* means devatās and humans who sprung from Brahmā and all the later dynasties. Viśva says that *paramparā* means a succession, offspring or killing. The Vedas are the source of all knowledge of karma *(laukika)* and jñāna *(alaukika).* The Vedas have non-material words (*aprākṛta-vacana*). Śruti says *vācā virūpa nityayā vṛṣne codasva:* O sage Virūpa, offer praise to the Lord using the eternal word. (Ṛg Veda 8.75.6)

*anādi-nidhanā nityā vāg utsṛṣṭā svayambhuvā \|*  
*ādau vedamayī divyā yataḥ sarvāḥ pravṛttayaḥ \|\|*

**In the beginning the self-born Lord produced the beginningless receptacle, the eternal word consisting of the divine Vedas, from which all this came. Mahābhārata 12.231.56-57**

The rest is clear.

### Text 11

*tac cānugataṁ tarkāpratiṣṭhānāt ity ādau, acintyāḥ khalu ye bhāvā na tāṁs tarkeṇa yojayet ity ādau śāstra-yonitvāt ity ādau \| śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt ity ādau \| pitṛ-deva-manuṣyāṇāṁ vedaś cakṣus taveśvara \| śreyas tv anupalabdhe’rthe sādha-sādhanayor api \|\| 11\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This is stated in the following:**

***tarkāpratiṣṭhānād apy anyathānumeyam it iced evam apy animokṣa-prasaṅgaḥ***

**If you think otherwise, when we say that logic is not solid proof. It would lead to the impossibility of liberation. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.11**

***acintyāḥ khalu ye bhāvā na tāṁs tarkeṇa yojayet \|***  
***prakṛtibhyaḥ paraṁ yac ca tad acintyasya lakṣaṇam \|\|***

**One cannot use material reasoning for those things which are inconceivable. Inconceivable refers to those things existing beyond prakṛti. Mahābhārata 6.6.11**

***śāṣtra-yonitvāt***

**The Lord cannot be known by reasoning because the source of understanding the Lord is the Upaniṣads. Brahma-sūtra 1.1.3**

***śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt***

**The defects of the agent do not apply to Brahman because of scriptural statements, which are the only proof concerning inconceivable subjects. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.27**

***pitṛ-deva-manuṣyānāṁ vedaś cakṣus taveśvara***  
***śreyas tv anupalabdhe ’rthe sādhya-sādhanayor api***

**O Lord! Your Vedas are the best cause of knowledge for Pitṛs, devatās and humans. The Vedas are the means and goal for attaining liberation and Svarga. SB 11.20.4**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Those who not believe in the Lord think that the scriptures have arisen recently through some author since they are well known to be created by a recent person. With the intention of destroying the eternal, continuous Veda by acts which impair the eternal varṇāśrama system, destroying the varṇas by engagement in occupations only to get enough food, gathering groups of like-minded persons for discussion, they conclude that the Vedas have been created recently.

“In the Vedas it is said that stones float, earth speaks and water speaks. Therefore they are not reliable as authority.”

The praise of stones is used in particular rituals in order to increase the power of stones, to show results for performing certain karmas. There is no fault, since it is well known that Rāma made stones float in building the bridge to Laṅkā. When it is said that earth and water speak, it refers to the devatās of the earth and water speaking. Thus in all cases the Vedas are trustworthy. But because they are difficult for ignorant jīvas to comprehend, being words of the omniscient Lord, it is only possible that persons having special *pratyakṣa* gained through the influence of the Vedas can understand what is stated in the Vedas, and not persons fixed in logic.

It is said:

*śāstrārtha-yukto'nubhavaḥ pramāṇaṁ tūttamaṁ matam \|*  
*anumādyā na svatantrāḥ pramāṇa-padavīṁ yayuḥ \|\|*

**The best proof is experience combined with the meaning of scripture. Independent** ***anumāna*** **does not produce proof. Puruṣottama-tantra**

*tarkāpratiṣṭhānāt*

**If you think otherwise, when we say that logic is not solid proof. It would lead to the impossibility of liberation. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.11**

*śrutes tu śabda-mūlatvāt*

**The defects of the agent do not apply to Brahman because of scriptural statements, which are the only proof concerning inconceivable subjects. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.27**

*naiṣā tarkeṇa matir āpaneyā proktā'nyenaiva sujñānāya preṣṭha*

**This knowledge cannot be achieved by logic. O dear one! It should be taught by another person in order to be understood properly. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.2.9**

*na taṁ vidātha ya imā jajānānyad yuṣmākam antaraṁ babhūva \|*  
*nīhāreṇa prāvṛtā jalpyā cāsu-tṛpa uktha-śāsaś caranti \|\|*

**You do not know the person who produced these creatures, the person who dwells within you. Surrounded by ignorance, engaged in material logic, uttering mantras to maintain themselves, the priests carry out their actions. Ṛg Veda 10.83.7**

*Jalpyāḥ pravṛtāḥ* means persons engaged in logical debate. Therefore it is said:

*sarvatra śakyate kartum āgamaṁ hi vinānumā \|*  
*tasmān na sā śaktimatī vināgamam udīkṣitum \|\|*

**In all cases, scripture can be used without** ***anumāna*****. Without scripture,** ***anumāna*** **cannot be given regard. Varāha Purāṇa**

The Advaita-vādīs say:

*yatnenopādito'py arthaḥ kuśalair anumātṛbhiḥ \|*  
*abhiyuktatarair anyair anyathaivopapādyate \|\|*

**A meaning produced with great effort by a skilful logician can be refuted by other more skilful logicians. Vāpuruṣārtha 1.34**

Śaṅkara says, “The logicians from the past, present and future cannot be gathered together at one time and place in the present so that we can produce the true meaning of the text. Since the Vedas are eternally the cause of producing knowledge, they produce a conclusive meaning for a particular topic. The knowledge generated is complete. It cannot be destroyed by any logician of the past, present or future.” Commentary on Brahma-sūtra 2.1.11

The teachings seen in the scriptures which sometimes use logic are the glory of the scripture since that logic is the form of the scripture and has been used only to make the meaning easily understood. If one argues that the words of the Veda act as proof only if proved by logic, then that is only logic. What is the use of the Vedas at all? Such types of persons who think themselves followers of the Veda are excluded from understanding the Vedas. That is the meaning given everywhere. Their destination is that of jackals. That is described in Mahābhārata. A jackal speaks to Kaśyapa. He relates that in his previous life he was a logician who argued with followers of the Vedas. As a result he became a jackal.9

Use of logic is mentioned in statements like *śrotavyo mantavyaḥ* (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.4.5) where *mantavyaḥ* refers to a logical thought process. But Kūrma Purāṇa explains this:

*pūrvāparāvirodhena ko nvartho'bhimato bhavet \|*  
*ity-ādyam ūhanaṁ tarkaḥ śuṣka-tarkaṁ ca varjayet \|\|*

**That logic is approved which produces a meaning not contrary to previous and later parts of a scripture. One should avoid dry logic. Kūrma Purāṇa**

Pūrva-mīmāṁsa refutation

Though the statements of the Veda have been established as proof, some people say that what is established in the Vedas is action, rather than the literal meaning, since action is understood from the power (*śākti*) of and purport (*tātparya*) of words. An elderly man tells a middle aged man “Bring the cow.” A child seeing the person engaged in bringing the cow understands the word “cow” to mean a bodily mass with a dewlap. By hearing “Herd the cow, bring the horse,” the child understands the meaning of the word “bring.” Thus he understands “cow” to mean an animal with a dewlap and “bring” means “to fetch.” There is a perception of the meaning of a word from the beginning by the cognition of action implicit in the word. Words develop meaning in sentences and sentences have meaning because of an active verb.10 The purport (intended meaning) also arises in a statement in the same way.

Concerning this matter the following should be said. How can a statement in itself (without imposing action on it) be without śakti (power to convey meaning)? Does it fail to produce suitable response to the situation by the words themselves? Or does its meaning arise only by performing action? It cannot be the first option, for a statement “Your son is here” (without additionally imposing some action) produces the response of smiling in the father. It cannot be the second option, for a statement “A son has been born” does not include action on the part of the hearer (to fulfill the meaning). This does not mean “See the son who has been born” since that action of seeing was not stated in the sentence.

If one argues that action should be imposed on the statement since initially the meaning to produce action did not appear, that is not so, for even imposing action on a statement should not give it meaning, since the word “action” itself lacks an action imposed on it in this theory. (To deny imposing action on action would limit the principle of imposing action on every word.) Descriptive elements would be rendered useless to grasp the situation since they would always depend on other elements to complete the meaning.

One should not say that the action is endowed with another action, since it is not possible to impose action on action (and still have meaning) and there would be infinite regress (another action must be imposed on the imposed action ad infinitum). There is also no necessity for imposing action on words to comprehend meaning. Even children designate meaning to words themselves without imposing action. “This is a piece of cloth” is easily understood by everyone.

The intent of the speaker is achieved without contrary perception in the listener, because a word accomplishes its meaning by its inherent śakti (rather than imposed śakti of action). Thus the Upaniṣads and other scriptures, designated as perfect, are proof by their direct meaning.

It is said:

Even if the explanation and meaning of a mantra are construed wrongly by others, the scriptures still remain perfect with their real meaning. If knowledge of an object which is not at first understood becomes understood from words which do not go beyond their natural meaning, and this results in discerning the purport after some time through *śabda*, is this not proof of the value of *śabda*? Since they are the final conclusion of the Vedas, if the Upaniṣads, which confirm some scriptures by agreement and refute others by taking them non-literally, give knowledge of ātmā, full of unlimited bliss, which was previously not understood, for the purpose of overcoming all suffering, they are proof by dint of their innate meaning even if this is contrary to other sources of knowledge and even if it makes *pratyakṣa* useless as a source of knowledge.” Bhagavan-nāma-kaumudī 1.13

Sphoṭa-vāda refutation:

If all sound in the form of the Vedas acts as proof by its own meaning, how does it produce the meaning? There is no śakti to produce meaning because the syllables quickly disappear. Some claim that the perception of meaning of the last uttered syllables arises from impressions generated from previous syllables. These impressions are perceived only by their effects, since they cannot be directly perceived. Because there is an order in remembrance, which is the effect of impressions, one perceives meaning of the last uttered syllables without having to ascertain the whole situation. In this way some speak of perception of meaning in words to be a sudden manifestation (*sphoṭa*).

*sa ca varṇānām anekatvenaika-pratyayānupapatter ekaika-varṇa-pratyayāhita-saṁskāra-bīje'ntya-varṇa-pratyaya-janita-paripāke pratyayiny eka-pratyaya-viṣayatayā jhaṭiti pratyav abhāsate*

**The meaning of a word manifests suddenly as the object of one perception in the perceiver as a product of the perception of the last syllables uttered, containing the seed of an impression gathered from a series of syllables, since one perception cannot be accomplished suddenly by many syllables. Śārīrika-bhāṣya on Brahma-sūtra 1.3.28**

These persons maintain that the Vedas are eternal because of the presence of *sphoṭa*. The Vedas are repeated for recognition (of syllables). Vedāntists however say *varṇā eva tu śabda iti bhagavān upavarṣaḥ:*  the syllables themselves are *śabda*; this is the opinion of Upavarṣa. (Śabara’s commentary on Dvādaśa-lakṣaṇā)

According to this, when the word “cow” is pronounced twice one accepts the eternity of words made of syllables since everyone recognizes at once that this does not mean two different words (arising by different *sphoṭas*) but rather one.

The syllables, like a line of ants, are combined with particular meaning in an accepted order. The syllables manifesting in this way in the intellect show the meaning of all the syllables after receiving each syllable. They then produce understanding of the respective meanings without fail.

In this way the varṇavādīs do not have to rely on excessive fabrication. The sphotāvādīs however have the fault of destroying what is seen and then constructing something unseen (imaginary). They maintain that the syllables accepted in a specific order reveal *sphoṭa* and that *sphoṭa* reveals the meaning. This explanation requires greater fabrication. Thus the words of the Veda, made of syllables only, causing revelation of meaning, are accepted as eternal (without resort to the principle of *sphoṭa*).

Meaning of words (*śabda-vṛtti*)

There are three functions of words: *mukhya* (literal), *lakṣaṇā* (indirect) and *gauṇa* (relating to quality). Mukhya or literal meaning has two types: conventional (*rūḍhi*) and etymological *(yoga*). *Rūḍhi* operates by assigning (*saṅketa*) a particular name (*saṁjñā*) connected to a class of objects (*saṁjñī)* to a specific object in question, according to special quality (*guṇa*), class characteristics (*jāti*) or individuality (*svarūpa*). “The cow Diṭtha is white.” (Diṭṭha is a name by individual convention, white is its quality and cow is the class.)

*Lakṣaṇā:*

This is an interpretive meaning understood by conventional usage. An example is “There is a village of cowherds on the Gaṅgā.” This has three types. The first is *ajahat-svārthā* (having previous reference, not giving up the original meaning). An example is “The white one (horse) runs.” (“Horse” was previously mentioned in a conversation.) The second type is *jahat-svārthā* (giving up the original meaning). An example is “There is a village of cowherds on the Gaṅgā.” (“On the Gaṅgā” means “on the bank of the Gaṅgā.”) The third type is *jahat-ajahat-svārthā* (part of the meaning remains and part is destroyed). An example is “This is that person Devadatta.” The part not given up is Devadatta. “That” and “this” indicate different persons, past and present. Śaṅkara uses this concept to explain *tat tvam asi.* Jīva and Brahman have the common attribute of consciousness. This quality of the jīva is not given up, but the jīva’s attribute of ignorance is given up in order to equate jīva with Brahman. 11 Rāmānujācārya does not accept the third category. One should consult his works.  *Sa* refers to past time and *ayam* refers to the present experience. There is no contradiction in combining the two words. How can it be *lakṣaṇā*? Lakṣaṇā should be used only if there is contradiction of terms.12 That is his argument in summary.

*Gauṇa:*

This refers to a meaning of words because of having similar qualities with an object mentioned. “Devadatta is a lion.” (Devadatta is similar to a lion in courage.)

*abhidheyāvinā-bhūta-pravṛttir lakṣaṇeṣyate \|*  
*lakṣyamāṇa-guṇair yogād vṛtter iṣṭā tu gauṇatā \|\|*

**The action of going beyond the direct meaning of words is called** ***lakṣaṇā. Gauṇa*** **is produced by employing words with qualities similar to those of the referent object. Tantra-vārttika. 1.4.22**

*Lakṣaṇā* depends on conventional meaning (*rūḍhi*) and intention. An example of dependence on conventional meaning is “The (person from) Kaliṅga is bold.” An example of dependence on the intention is “The cowherds on the Gaṅgā.” The intention to be understood is that they live on bank because it is cool and breezy.

*Gauṇa-vṛtti* depends only on intention. “The carrier is a cow.” The intention to be understood is that the person is extremely ignorant and is therefore called a cow.

Yoga

*Yoga* is produced by combination of elemental words (roots) with affixes, making words and meanings produced by *mukhya, lakṣaṇā* and *gauṇa-vṛtti.* Example of *yoga* with *mukhya* meaning is *paṅkaja,* “born from the mud,” which means a lotus. An example of *yoga* using *lakṣaṇā* is the word *aupagava* (literally a descendent of Upaga), meaning by extension, any brāhmaṇa’s son. An example of *yoga* using *gauṇa-vṛtti* is the word *pācaka* (a digestive medicine, which “cooks or digests” food like a fire).

Vyañjana

The function called *vyañjana* (figurative expression) should be considered. “The cowherds on the Gaṅgā”refers to a cool, breezy bank where the cowherds live. It is said:

*śabda-buddhi-karmaṇāṁ viramya vyāpārābhāva iti nayenābhidhā-lakṣaṇā-tātparyākhyāsu tisṛṣu vṛttiṣu svaṁ svam arthaṁ bodhayitvopakṣīṇāsu yayānyo'rtho bodhyate, sā śabdasyārthasya prakṛti-pratyayādeś ca śaktir vyañjana-nigamana-dhvanana-pratyāyana-bhāvābhiprāyādi-vyapadeśa-viṣayā vyañjanā nama*

**There is a principle that the agency of words, thought and action cease after completing their functions. By that principle, when direct meaning (*****abhidhā),*** **indirect meaning (*****lakṣaṇā*****) and general import (*****tātparya*****) having revealed their respective meanings then cease to function, the power of a word, its meaning, its root and affixes by which another meaning is understood, is called** ***vyañjana*** **(suggestive meaning). It is also called** ***nigamana, dhvani, pratyāya, bhāva*** **and** ***abhipraya.*** **Sāhitya-darpaṇa 2.23**

Words in Sentences

These usages of words produce an understanding of the meanings of particular words in the context of a sentence. The meaningful word is produced by a meaning produced through declension. Making the words into a sentence produces specific meaning.

*Syād yogyatākāṅkṣa-yuktaḥ padoccayaḥ*: a number of words combined suitably (*yogyatā*), with expectancy (*ākāṅkṣā)* and with proximate connection *(āsatti*) make a sentence. (Sāhitya-darpaṇa 2.1)

“Suitability means absence of contradiction in the mutual relationship of the meaning of words. For instance ‘He sprinkled the plant with fire’ is not sensible.” (Sāhitya-darpana)

*Prajāpatir ātmano vapām upākhidat*: Prajāpati destroyed his own intestines. (Taittirīya-saṁhitā 2.5.1) This statement has suitability because of the inconceivable power of Prajāpati’s actions.

“*Ākāṅkṣā* means that in the absence of words necessary for complete comprehension, the listener would ask questions. Thus a sentence like “The cow, horse, man, elephant” could arise (because of missing words). *Āsatti* means proximate connection of the words. If this were not so, uttering “Devadatta” could be connected with “goes” uttered on the next day. Since *ākāṅkṣā* and *yogyatā* are actually properties of the self, to say these are qualities of the group of words is meant figuratively.” Sāhitya-darpaṇa 2.1

Larger Statements

The statements produce a larger statement (*mahāvākya*). *Mahāvākya* means “a collection of statements.” The meaning of the whole (*mahāvākya*) is understood by the usage of upakrama, upasaṁhāra etc.:

*upakramopasaṁhārāv abhyāso'pūrvatā phalam \|*  
*arthavādopapattī ca liṅgaṁ tātparya-nirṇaye \|\|*

**In understanding the meaning of a particular passage, the means are** ***upakrama, upasaṁhāra, abhyāsa, apūravatā, phalam, artha-vāda*** **and** ***upapatti.*** **Bṛhat-saṁhitā quoted by Madhva in commentary on Brahma-sūtra 1.1.47**

*Upakrama* and *upasaṁhāra* are the same statement. *Abhyāsa* means repetition. *Apūrvatā* means that which was not previously understood.  *Phalam* means the goal. *Arthavāda* means praise and *upapatti* means use of reasoning. These are the six ways sentences are used together to make a point. The meaning of the *mahāvākya* (total statement) is understood by the general intent employing positive and negative statements. Using reasoning (*upapatti*) does not mean dry logic but assembling reasons which are stated in scripture. Using dry logic would render scripture meaningless.

Strength of Statements

Where there is contradiction of one scriptural statement with another, one must consider which is stronger. Strength is based on the scriptural source and the source of the statement. Strength based on scriptural source is judged by the following:

*śruti-smṛti-virodhe tu śrutir eva balīyasī*

**If there is contradiction of śruti and smṛti, śruti is the stronger authority.**

Strength based on the type of statement is as follows:

*śruti-liṅga-vākya-prakaraṇa-sthāna-samākhyānāṁ samavāye pāra-daurbalyam artha-viprakarṣāt*

**Where there is a combination of literal meaning, interpretive meaning, altering the syntax, reference to other texts, philosophical stance, and etymological meaning, the later statements are considered progressively weaker in authority, because of the possibility of their producing contrary meaning. Jaimini-sūtra 3.3.14**

These terms are defined as follows:

*śrutiś ca śabdaḥ kṣamatā liṅgam*  
*vākyaṁ padāny eva tu saṁhitāni \|*  
*sā prakriyā yat karaṇaṁ sakṣāṅkṣam,*  
*sthānaṁ kramo yoga-balaṁ samākhyā \|\|*

***Śruti*** **means direct meaning,** ***liṅga*** **means suggested meaning,** ***vākya*** **means interpretation through taking the phrases as a whole,** ***prakriyā*** **or** ***prakaraṇa*** **means intentionally taking a word out of context.** ***Sthānam*** **means interpretation according to a philosophical stance and** ***samākhyā*** **means etymology.**

Where there is contradiction due to apparent ambiguity, one should find the meaning according to the strength of the various statements.

In regards to the inconceivable, it is explained that it is not subject to logic.  *Acintyāḥ khalu ye bhāvā na tāṁs tarkeṇa yojayet:* that which is inconceivable is not subject to logic. (Skanda Purāṇa) One could then say “If it is not inconceivable, then there is opportunity for using logic.” But that is not my inclination. In all circumstances the Vedas are the proof. It is said in Śaṅkara’s Śārīraka:

*āgama-balena brahma-vādī kāraṇādi-svarūpaṁ nirūpayati, nāvaśyaṁ tasya \[anumānasya\] yathā-dṛṣṭaṁ sarvam abhyupagataṁ mantavyam*

**The brahmavadis define the nature of the ultimate cause on the strength of scriptures. One should not conclude that everything seen should be understood according to inference. (commentary on Brahma-sūtra 2.2.38)**

The Veda is testimony beyond the material world. What is described as supreme in the Vedas will be inconceivable because it is beyond the material world. When examining the Vedas, what is indicated to be above everything else by the six types of statements (*upakrama* etc.) should become the object of worship.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

“What is this insistence on the Vedas being the only proof.” In answer to this Jīva says that statements by Vedavyāsa agree with his conclusion. He cites six statements of Vyāsa. The first is from Brahma-sūtras. *Tarkāpratiṣṭhānāt.* Defining the Lord by logic (*tarkeṇa*) is not possible since its conclusions are destroyed by varieties of human intelligence (one defeating the other). Thus śruti says:

*naiṣā tarkeṇa matir āpaneyā proktānyenaiva sujñānāya preṣṭha*

**This knowledge cannot be achieved by logic. O dear one! It should be taught by another person in order to be understood properly. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 1.2.9**

*vyāpyāropeṇa vyāpakāropas tarkaḥ*

***Tarka*** **means by accepting hypothetical** ***vyāpya*** **(invariable concomitant), one must accept a** ***vyāpaka*** **(pervasive concomitant). Tarka-saṅgraha**

It takes the form “If there is no fire, there is no smoke.” By destroying doubt about the pervasion, it becomes a type of inference or *anumāna.* Thus *tarka* means *anumāna.*

The verse from Mahābhārata is found in the Udyama-parva. *Śāstra-yonitvāt* is from Brahma-sūtras. Is the Lord to be known by inference or by scriptures? Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad says *mantavyaḥ*: he is known by inference. No, he is not known by inference. (The word *na* should be added to the sūtra.) Why? Because the cause of knowing him is scriptures (*śāstra-yonitvāt*). *Aupaniṣadaṁ puruṣaṁ pṛcchāmi*: I ask about the Lord who is known through the Upaniṣads. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad)

*Na* should also be added to *śrutes tu.* The faults seen in matter should not apply to Brahman as the agent of creation since it is said in scripture that he creates by his will alone*. So’kāmayata bahu syāṁ prajāyeya:* he desired “Let me become many for increasing the population. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.6.1)

“Why should śruti say something contrary to logic?” *Śabda-mūlatvāt:* scripture is the only proof for matters which are inconceivable. Inconceivable effects are also seen in gems and mantras. Uddhava speaks the verse from the Eleventh Canto. O Lord! Your Vedas are the best eye for humans, devatās and pitas. Why? They can be used for attaining Svarga and liberation. Thus the Vedas are the proof. This means “My statement is supported by all the Vedas. I do not say anything unprecedented.”

### Text 12

*tatra ca veda-śabdasya samprati duṣpāratvād duradhigamārthatvāc ca tad-artha-nirṇāyakānāṁ munīnām api paraspara-virodhād veda-rūpo vedārtha-nirṇāyakaś cetihāsa-purāṇātmakaḥ śabda eva vicāraṇīyaḥ \| tatra ca yo vā veda-śabdo nātma-viditaḥ so’pi tad-dṛṣṭyānumeya eveti samprati tasyaiva pramotpādakatvaṁ sthitam \| tathā hi mahābhārate mānavīye ca – itihāsa-purāṇābhyāṁ vedaṁ samupabṛṁhayet iti \| pūraṇāt purāṇam iti cānyatra \| na cāvedena vedasya bṛṁhaṇaṁ sambhavati \| na hy aparipūrṇasya kanaka-valayasya trapuṇā pūraṇaṁ yujyate \|*

*nanu yadi veda-śabdaḥ purāṇam itihāsaṁ copādatte \| tarhi purāṇam anyad anveṣaṇīyam \| yadi tu na, na tarhītihāsa-purāṇayor abhedo vedena \| ucyate – viśiṣṭaikārtha-pratipādaka-pada-kadambasyāpauruṣeyatvād abhede’pi svarakramabhedād bheda-nirdeśo’py upapadyate \| ṛg-ādibhiḥ samam anayor apauruṣeyatvenābhedo mādhyandina-śrutāv eva vyajyate evaṁ vā are’sya mahato bhūtasya niśvasitam etad yad ṛg-vedo yajur-vedaḥ sāma-vedo’tharvāṅgirasa itihāsaḥ purāṇam ity ādinā \|\|12\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since the words of the Vedas are now difficult to access and difficult to understand, and since sages who explain the texts mutually disagree on the meaning, the words of Itihāsas and Purāṇas, which explain the meaning of the Vedas and which are another form of the Vedas, should be discussed. Those statements in the Vedas which cannot be understood can be understood by seeing the Purāṇas and Itihāsas. That fact establishes these scriptures as proof in Kali-yuga.**

***itihāsa-purāṇābhyāṁ vedaṁ samupabṛṁhayet***

**One should clarify the meaning of the Vedas by the Itihāsas and Purāṇas. Mahābhārata 1.1.267**

**It is also said that a Purāṇa is so-called because it completes** ***(pūraṇa*****) the meaning of Vedas. It is not possible to complete the Vedas with something which is not equal to the Vedas. It is not possible to complete a gold bracelet with a piece of lead.**

**“If the word** ***veda*** **means Purāṇas and Itihāsas, then one should look for other works to be called Purāṇas. If one does not do so, the Itihāsas and Purāṇas would simply be the Vedas.” Though there is non-difference because they are all beyond human production (*****apauruṣeya*****) with words indicating one meaning, there is also difference, since the Vedas have intonation of the words (*****svaras*****) and Purāṇas do not. The Madhyandina-śruti indicates that the Purāṇas and Itihāsas are equal to the Vedas since they are also nonhuman in origin like the Vedas.**

***evaṁ vā are’sya mahato bhūtasya niśvasitam etad yad ṛg-vedo yajur-vedaḥ sāma-vedo’tharvāṅgirasa itihāsaḥ purāṇam***

**The breathing of the Lord is the Ṛg, Yajur, Sāma and Atharva Vedas, the Itihāsas and Purāṇas. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.4.10**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Though proof has been defined, another doubt arises, and the conclusion is shown in this section.

The Vedas are difficult to access in Kali-yuga. They are difficult to understand because they are not widely used at this time.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

If this is so, then by the Vedas one should understand about the highest goal. This anuccheda replies to that statement. Someone may argue, “Though the meaning of the Vedas may be difficult, sages have explained the meaning by using nyāya and other scriptures.” In answer to this it is said here that the explanations of the meaning of one sage contract the explanations of other sages.

Since what cannot be understood in the Vedas can be understood from the Purāṇas and Itihāsas, the Itihāsas and Purāṇas which are forms of the Vedas are established as proof. *Itihāsa-purāṇābhyāṁ vedaṁ samupabṛṁhayet*: one should clarify the meaning of the Vedas by the Itihāsas and Purāṇas. It is called Purāṇa because it completes the meaning of the Vedas (*pūranāt*). One cannot complete a gold bracelet with a link of lead.

Someone may worry that the Purāṇas and Itihāsas are actually the Vedas. This doubt is clarified by explaining the difference. Though the Vedas and the Purāṇas indicate only one meaning--Bhagavān endowed with all his śaktis--and are both without human authors, the Vedas have particular *svaras* or intonations of pitch and the Purāṇas and Itihāsas do not. This element is different. In the quote from Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad Yājñavalkya speaks to Maitreyī. “O Maitreyī! The breathing of the powerful or worshipable (*mahataḥ*) Lord (*asya*), eternally perfect (*bhūtasya)*, is the Vedas, Itihāsas and Purāṇas.” The meaning of the rest of the words is clear.

### Text 13

*ataeva skānda-prabhāsa-khaṇḍe –*  
*purā tapaś cacārogram amarāṇāṁ pitāmahaḥ \|*  
*āvirbhūtās tato vedāḥ saṣaḍ-aṅga-pada-kramāḥ \|\|*  
*tataḥ purāṇam akhilaṁ sarva-śāstra-mayaṁ dhruvam \|*  
*nitya-śabda-mayaṁ puṇyaṁ śata-koṭi-pravistaram \|\|*  
*nirgataṁ brahmaṇo vaktrāt tasya bhedān nibodhata \|*
