*brāhmyaṁ purāṇaṁ prathamam ity ādi \|*  
*atra śata-koṭi-saṅkhyā brahma-loke prasiddheti tathoktam \| tṛtīya-skandhe ca ṛg-yajuḥ-sāmātharvākhyān vedān pūrvādibhir mukhaiḥ ity ādi-prakaraṇe,*  
*itihāsa-purāṇāni pañcamaṁ vedam īśvaraḥ \|*  
*sarvebhya eva vaktrebhyaḥ sasṛje sarva-darśanaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*api cātra sākṣād eva veda-śabdaḥ prayuktaḥ purāṇetihāsayoḥ \| anyatra ca -- purāṇaṁ pañcamo vedaḥ \| itihāsaḥ purāṇaṁ ca pañcamo veda ucyate \| vedān adhyāpayāmāsa mahābhārata-pañcamān ity ādau \| anyathā vedān ityādāv api pañcamatvaṁ nāvakalpeta samāna-jātīya-niveśitatvāt saṅkhyāyāḥ \| bhaviṣya-purāṇe kārṣṇaṁ ca pañcamaṁ vedaṁ yan mahābhārataṁ smṛtam iti \| tathā ca sāma-kauthumīya-śākhāyāṁ chāndogyopaniṣadi ca -- ṛg-vedaṁ bhagavo’dhyemi yajur-vedaṁ sāma-vedam ātharvaṇaṁ caturtham itihāsaṁ purāṇaṁ pañcamaṁ vedānāṁ vedam ity ādi \| ataeva asya mahato bhūtasya ity ādāv itihāsa-purāṇayoś caturṇām evāntarbhūtatva-kalpanayā prasiddha-pratyākhyānaṁ nirastam \| tad uktaṁ brāhmyaṁ purāṇaṁ prathamam ity ādi \|\|13\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Therefore it is said in Skanda Purāṇa:**

***purā tapaś cacārogram amarāṇāṁ pitāmahaḥ \|***  
***āvirbhūtās tato vedāḥ saṣaḍ-aṅga-pada-kramāḥ \|\|***  
***tataḥ purāṇam akhilaṁ sarva-śāstra-mayaṁ dhruvam \|***  
***nitya-śabda-mayaṁ puṇyaṁ śata-koṭi-pravistaram \|\|***  
***nirgataṁ brahmaṇo vaktrāt tasya bhedān nibodhata \|***  
***brāhmyaṁ purāṇaṁ prathamam***

**Long ago the grandfather of the devatās performed intense austerities. The Vedas with their six aṅgas appeared with their verses in particular order. Then all the eternal, pure Purāṇas, embodiment of all scriptures, with eternal words, of a billion verses, emerged from Brahmā’s mouth. Please hear about the different Purāṇas starting with the Brahmā Purāṇa. Skanda Purāṇa 2.3-5**

**The billion verses are known in Brahmaloka.**

***ṛg-yajuḥ-sāmātharvākhyān vedān pūrvādibhir mukhaiḥ***  
***śāstram ijyāṁ stuti-stomaṁ prāyaścittaṁ vyadhāt kramāt***

**From Brahmā’s eastern mouth, the Ṛg Veda and activities of the** ***hotā*** **were established. From his southern mouth the Yajur Veda and activities of the** ***adhvaryu*** **were established. From his western mouth the Sāma Veda and the activities of the** ***udgātā*** **were established. From his northern mouth the Atharva Veda and the activities of the** ***brahma*** **were established. SB 3.12.37**

***itihāsa-purāṇāni pañcamaṁ vedam īśvaraḥ***  
***sarvebhya eva vaktrebhyaḥ sasṛje sarva-darśanaḥ***

**Brahmā, who has faces in all directions, created the fifth Veda composed of the Purāṇas and Itihāsas from all of his mouths. SB 3.12.39**

**The word** ***veda*** **is directly used here to indicate the Purāṇas and Itihāsas. Elsewhere it is said:**

***ṛg-yajuḥ-sāmātharvākhyā vedāś catvāra uddhṛtāḥ \|***  
***itihāsa-purāṇaṁ ca pañcamo veda ucyate \|\|***

**He divided the Veda into the Ṛg, Yajus, Sāma and Atharvā. Histories and Purāṇas are called the fifth Veda. SB 1.4.20**

***vedān adhyāpayāmāsa mahābhārata-pañcamān***

**He studied the Vedas and the Mahābhārata, the fifth Veda. Mahābhārata 12.340.11**

**Mahābhārata cannot be considered the fifth Vedas unless it is equal in quality to the Vedas.**

***kārṣṇaṁ ca pañcamaṁ vedaṁ yan mahābhārataṁ smṛtam***

**The Mahābhārata of Vedavyāsa is considered the fifth Veda. Bhaviṣya Purāṇa**

***ṛg-vedaṁ bhagavo’dhyemi yajur-vedaṁ sāma-vedam ātharvaṇaṁ caturtham itihāsaṁ purāṇaṁ pañcamaṁ vedānāṁ vedam***

**I studied the Ṛg Veda, Yajur, Veda, Sāma Veda, Atharva Veda, the Itihāsas and Purāṇas, the fifth of Veda. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 7.1.2, Sāma-kautumīya-śākhā**

**Since the above statements state that the Purāṇas and Itihāsas as part of the Vedas, well known objections that Purāṇas and Itihāsas are simply names of parts of the four Vedas in explaining the quotation from Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.4.10 are hereby refuted. The Purāṇas are even specifically mentioned. (*****brāhmyaṁ purāṇaṁ prathamam*** **mentioned above)**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

These verses explain the appearance of the Vedas and Purāṇas. The word *sasṛje* in SB 3.12.39 means “Brahmā manifested the fifth Veda.” It was not created. The Mahābhārata says that Mahābhārata is the fifth Veda. The Mahābhārata could not be considered the fifth Veda unless it was equal in quality to the Vedas. It is like saying “Call the brāhmaṇas including the fifth one called Yajña-datta.” *Kārṣṇam* means “spoke by Vyāsa.” (quote from Bhaviṣya Purāṇa)

Because it is stated that the Purāṇas and Itihāsas are the fifth Veda, objections are refuted. There is an argument that the words Purāṇa and Itihāsa mentioned in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka quotation are merely parts of the Veda which narrate previous events and stories with the five characteristics (sarga, pratisarga, vaṁśa, manvantaras and vaṁśanucaritam). See anuccheda 6113 Those stories are accepted as parts of the Veda. The idea concocted by persons absorbed in karma that the statement in the Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad does not refer to the Purāṇas and Mahābhārata written by Vyāsa for śūdras and others is refuted by the above quotations.

### Text 14

*pañcamatve kāraṇaṁ ca vāyu-purāṇe sūta-vākyam –*  
*itihāsa-purāṇānāṁ vaktāraṁ samyag eva hi \|*  
*māṁ caiva pratijagrāha bhagavān īśvaraḥ prabhuḥ \|\|*  
*eka āsīd yajur vedas taṁ caturdhā vyakalpayat \|*  
*cāturhotram abhūt tasmiṁs tena yajñam akalpayat \|\|*  
*ādhvaryavaṁ yajurbhis tu ṛgbhir hotraṁ tathaiva ca \|*  
*audgātraṁ sāmabhiś caiva brahmatvaṁ cāy atharvabhiḥ \|\|*  
*ākhyānaiś cāpy upākhyānair gāthābhir dvija-sattamāḥ \|*  
*purāṇa-saṁhitāś cakre purāṇārtha-viśāradaḥ \|\|*  
*yac chiṣṭaṁ tu yajurveda iti śāstrārtha-nirṇayaḥ \|*  
*iti brahma-yajñādhyayane ca viniyogo dṛśyate’mīṣāṁ yad-brāhmaṇānītihāsa-purāṇāni iti \| so’pi nāvedatve sambhavati \| ato yad āha bhagavān mātsye --*  
*kālenāgrahaṇaṁ matvā purāṇasya dvijottamāḥ \|*  
*vyāsa-rūpam ahaṁ kṛtvā saṁharāmi yuge yuge \|\| iti*  
*pūrva-siddham eva purāṇaṁ sukha-saṅgrahaṇāya saṅkalpayāmīti tatrārthaḥ \| tad-anantaraṁ hy uktam –*  
*caturlakṣa-pramāṇena dvāpare dvāpare sadā \|*  
*tad-aṣṭādaśadhā kṛtvā kṛtvā bhūr-loke’smin prabhāṣyate \|*  
*adyāpy amartya-loke tu śata-koṭi-pravistaram \|*  
*tad-artho’tra catur-lakṣaḥ saṅkṣepeṇa niveśitaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*atra tu yac chiṣṭaṁ tu yajur vede ity uktatvāt tasyābhidheya-bhāgaś caturlakṣas tv atra martya-loke saṅkṣepeṇa sāra-saṅgraheṇa niveśitaḥ \| na tu racanāntareṇa \|\|14\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The description of the five parts of the Veda is found in Vāyu Purāṇa:**

***itihāsa-purāṇānāṁ vaktāraṁ samyag eva hi \|***  
***māṁ caiva pratijagrāha bhagavān īśvaraḥ prabhuḥ \|\|***  
***eka āsīd yajur vedas taṁ caturdhā vyakalpayat \|***  
***cāturhotram abhūt tasmiṁs tena yajñam akalpayat \|\|***  
***ādhvaryavaṁ yajurbhis tu ṛgbhir hotraṁ tathaiva ca \|***  
***audgātraṁ sāmabhiś caiva brahmatvaṁ cāy atharvabhiḥ \|\|***

**The supreme Lord (Veda-vyāsa) accepted me as the speaker of the Itihāsas and Purāṇas.**

**There was one Veda, called Yajur. He made it into four. Then cātur-hotras appeared in the Vedas. By them he made sacrifice:** ***adhvaryu*** **by the Yajur Veda,** ***hotā*** **by the Ṛg Veda,** ***udgātā*** **by the Sāma Veda and** ***brahma*** **by the Atharva Veda. Vāyu Purāṇa 60.16-18**

***ākhyānaiś cāpy upākhyānair gāthābhir dvija-sattamāḥ \|***  
***purāṇa-saṁhitāś cakre purāṇārtha-viśāradaḥ \|\|***  
***yac chiṣṭaṁ tu yajurveda iti śāstrārtha-nirṇayaḥ \|***

**O best of the brāhmaṇas! Knowledgeable of the meaning of the Purāṇas, Vyāsa created all the Purāṇas and Mahābhārata, by describing the five elements, ancient events and songs. This is the remnant of the Yajur Veda. This defines the meaning of scripture. Vāyu Purāṇa 60.21-22**

**The Purāṇas are mentioned in the recitation of Brahma-yajña (daily recitation of the Vedas). The following is recited during that ritual. yad-brāhmaṇānītihāsa-purāṇāni: the Brāhmaṇas, Itihāsas and Purāṇas. (Yajur Veda Āraṇyaka) If they were not the Vedas they could not be recited in Brahma-yajña. Some argue that the words** ***itihāsa*** **and** ***purāṇa*** **refer to parts of the Vedas which tell histories and stories and not to the Mahābhārata and Purāṇas written by Vyāsa.14 The Lord says:**

***kālenāgrahaṇaṁ matvā purāṇasya dvijottamāḥ \|***  
***vyāsa-rūpam ahaṁ kṛtvā saṁharāmi yuge yuge \|\|***

**Thinking that in the passing of time people cannot receive the Purāṇas, I appear in form of Vyāsa and collate them yuga after yuga. Matsya Purāṇa 53.8-9**

**The meaning is “I bring together the Purāṇas which were previously there, so that people can easily access them.” After that it is said in the same Purāṇa:**

***caturlakṣa-pramāṇena dvāpare dvāpare sadā \|***  
***tad-aṣṭādaśadhā kṛtvā kṛtvā bhūr-loke’smin prabhāṣyate \|***  
***adyāpy amartya-loke tu śata-koṭi-pravistaram \|***  
***tad-artho’tra catur-lakṣaḥ saṅkṣepeṇa niveśitaḥ \|\|***

**The Purāṇas are divided into eighteen with four hundred thousand verses during each Dvāpara-yuga on earth. Even today in heavenly planets the verses number a billion. Their meaning has been condensed into four hundred thousand verses. Matsya Purāṇa 53.9-11**

**What remains of the Yajur Veda (mentioned above) is the four hundred thousand verses on earth, which contain in summary, as the essence, the meaning of the billion verses in the heavenly planets. They are not a different creation.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

By the Ṛg and other Vedas the *cāturhotram* was created with four priests and thus karma arose. It was not created by the Itihāsas and Purāṇas. The Itihāsas and Purāṇas became the fifth Veda Vyāsa created the Purāṇas and Mahābhārata (*saṁhitā)* with the five characteristics (*ākhyanaiḥ*), ancient events (*upākhyānaiḥ),* and specific hymns (*gāthābhiḥ*). This Mahābhārata was the remainder of the Yajur Veda.

The use of Itihāsas and Purāṇas is seen in the recitation of Brahma-yajña. They could not be used if they were not the Vedas. Appearing in the form of Vyāsa *(vyāsa-rūpam kṛtvā*), I collate (*saṁharāmi*) the Itihāsas and Purāṇas yuga after yuga. Since it was stated that it was the remainder of the Yajur Veda, the meaning is that its most important part (*tasyābhidheya-bhāgaḥ*) was condensed into four hundred thousand verses on earth planet.

### Text 15

*tathaiva darśitaṁ veda-sahabhāvena śiva-purāṇasya vāyavīya-saṁhitāyām –*  
*saṅkṣipya caturo vedāṁś caturdhā vyabhajat prabhuḥ \|*  
*vyasta-vedatayā khyāto veda-vyāsa iti smṛtaḥ \|\|*  
*purāṇam api saṅkṣiptaṁ caturlakṣa-pramāṇataḥ \|*  
*adyāpy amartya-loke tu śata-koṭi-pravistaram \|\|*  
*saṅkṣiptam ity atra teneti śeṣaḥ \| skāndam āgneyam ity ādi samākhyās tu pravacana-nibandhanāḥ kāṭhakādivat \| ānupūrvīr nirmāṇa-nibandhanā vā \| tasmāt kvacid anityatva-śravaṇaṁ tv āvirbhāva-tirobhāvāpekṣayā \| tad evam itihāsa-purāṇayor vedatvaṁ siddham \| tathāpi sūtādīnām adhikāraḥ \| sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phala-śrī-kṛṣṇa-nāmavat \| yathoktam – prabhāsa-khaṇḍe –*  
*madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ*  
*sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam \|*  
*sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā*  
*bhṛgu-vara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma \|\| iti \|\|*  
*yathā coktaṁ viṣṇu-dharme –*  
*ṛg-vedo’tha yajur-vedaḥ sāma-vedo’py atharvaṇaḥ \|*  
*adhītās tena yenoktaṁ harir ity akṣara-dvayam \|\| iti \|*  
*atha vedārtha-nirṇāyakatvaṁ ca vaiṣṇave –*  
*bhārata-vyapadeśena hy āmnāyārthaḥ pradarśitaḥ \|*  
*vedāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ sarve purāṇe nātra saṁśayaḥ \|\| ity ādau \|*  
*kiṁ ca vedārtha-dīpakānāṁ śāstrāṇāṁ madhya-pātitābhyupagame’py āvirbhāvaka-vaiśiṣṭyāt tayor eva vaiśiṣṭyam \| yathā pādme --*  
*dvaipāyanena yad buddhaṁ*  
*brahmādyais tan na budhyate \|*  
*sarva-buddhaṁ sa vai veda*  
*tad buddhaṁ nānya-gocaraḥ \|\|15\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Skanda Purāṇa, in Vāyavīya-saṁhita, shows that the Purāṇas accompany the Vedas:**

***saṅkṣipya caturo vedāṁś caturdhā vyabhajat prabhuḥ \|***  
***vyasta-vedatayā khyāto veda-vyāsa iti smṛtaḥ \|\|***  
***purāṇam api saṅkṣiptaṁ caturlakṣa-pramāṇataḥ \|***  
***adyāpy amartya-loke tu śata-koṭi-pravistaram \|\|***

**The Lord, condensing the four Vedas, divided them into four. He is known as Veda-vyāsa because he divided** ***(vyasta*****) the Vedas. Being only four hundred thousand verses, the Purāṇas were condensed by him. Even today in heavenly planets there are a billion verses. Śiva Purāṇa 1.33-34**

**The Purāṇas were condensed by Vyāsa. Skanda and Agni Purāṇas are so called because they were spoken by Skanda and Agni, but were written by Vyāsa, just as the Kāṭhaka refers to parts of the Veda studied by Kaṭha. Or the names indicate the successive causes of production. For instance Skanda Purāṇa is written to glorify Skanda.15 Therefore though it is said that the Purāṇas are not eternal, that is only because they appear and disappear repeatedly. Thus it is proved that the Itihāsas and Purāṇas are also Vedas. Though the Purāṇas are Vedas, Sūta and others (who were śūdras) were qualified to study them. The Purāṇas are like the name of Kṛṣṇa, which is the real fruit of the creeper of all the Vedas:**

***madhura-madhuram etan maṅgalaṁ maṅgalānāṁ***  
***sakala-nigama-vallī-sat-phalaṁ cit-svarūpam \|***  
***sakṛd api parigītaṁ śraddhayā helayā vā***  
***bhṛgu-vara nara-mātraṁ tārayet kṛṣṇa-nāma \|\|***

**The name of Kṛṣṇa is sweeter than the sweetest, the most auspicious of all things auspicious. It is the highest fruit in the tree of all the Vedas, and is composed entirely of pure consciousness. O best of Bhṛgu’s dynasty! Heard once with faith or in negligence, it can deliver any human being. Prabhāsa-khaṇda, Skanda Purāṇa**

**It is also said in Viṣṇu-dharma:**

***ṛg-vedo’tha yajur-vedaḥ sāma-vedo’py atharvaṇaḥ \|***  
***adhītās tena yenoktaṁ hair ity akṣara-dvayam \|\|***

**He who recites the two syllables “Hari” has studied the Ṛg, Yajur, Sāma and Atharva Vedas. Viṣṇu-dharma**

***bhārata-vyapadeśena hy āmnāyārthaḥ pradarśitaḥ \|***  
***vedāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ sarve purāṇe nātra saṁśayaḥ \|\|***

**By the teachings of Mahābhārata I have shown the meaning of the Vedas. The Vedas are established in all the Purāṇas without doubt. Viṣṇu Purāṇa**

**Though the Purāṇas are included in works that reveal the meaning of the Vedas, the Purāṇas and Mahābhārata are superior to other works because of the revealer’s excellence. It is said in Padma Purāṇa:**

***dvaipāyanena yad buddhaṁ brahmādyais tan na budhyate \|***  
***sarva-buddhaṁ sa vai veda tad buddhaṁ nānya-gocaraḥ \|\|***

**What is understood by Vyāsa is not understood by Brahma and others. He knows everything. His understanding cannot be approached by anyone. Padma Purāṇa**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

The author summarizes the topic in this section. Purāṇas and Itihāsas are also Veda. This does not include other works resembling smṛtis which have conclusions contrary to the other smṛtis.

*smṛty-anavakāśa-doṣa-prasaṅga iti cen nānya-smṛty-anavakāśa-doṣa-prasaṅgāt*

**If one argues that refusing the conclusions of Sāṅkhya smṛti would make that scripture useless, the answer is no, for accepting it would render the conclusions of other smṛtis useless. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.1**

There is the following doubt:

*“na ca smārtam atad-dharmābhilāpāt*

**The antaryāmī is not** ***pradhāna*** **because qualities are mentioned that do not belong to** ***pradhāna.***  **Brahma-sūtra 1.2.19**

In the above sūtra, *smṛti* means *pradhāna.* Did not Vyāsa who arranged the śrutis call the Purāṇas *smṛti* because they deal mainly with *pradhāna* as the final cause (as propounded by the followers of Sāṅkhya) whereas the Vedas do not discuss it?”

That is not so. Vyāsa does not accept *pradhāna* as independent. He does not consider Sāṅkhya philosophy as *smṛti* because it propounds independent *pradhāna.* In the following sūtra he states that *pradhāna* is dependent on the Supreme Lord.

*tad-adhīnatvād arthavad*

***Pradhāna*** **is useful, since it produces the world under the Lord's direction. Brahma-sūtra 1.4.3**

*Pradhāna* is also known as *avyākṛta.* That *pradhāna* is not independent is also seen in the Purāṇas. The Sāṅkhya texts are not regular smṛtis.

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

He is known as Veda-vyāsa because he divided (*vyasta*) the Vedas. Skanda Purāṇa is so-called not because it was written by Skanda, but it was spoken by him, just as a composition is called Kāṭhaka because it was learned by the person named Kaṭha. The parts of the Veda studied by Kaṭha are called Kāṭhaka. According to *gotra-varaṇād vuñ* (Pāṇ 4.3.126), *kaṭhaka* means the dharma and tradition of the school of Kaṭha. Thus the meaning of Kaṭhaka is the works studied by Kaṭha. Similarly the Purāṇas were spoken by Skanda and others but not made by them. Otherwise they would not be eternal. Or the names indicate the successive causes of production (*ānupūrvī*) of the Purāṇas. Though the Purāṇas and Itihāsas are Vedas, śūdras are also entitled to recite them. That is on the strength of the following statement:

*strī-śūdra-dvijabandhūnāṁ trayī na śruti-gocarā \|*  
*karma-śreyasi mūḍhānāṁ śreya evaṁ bhaved iha \|*  
*iti bhāratam ākhyānaṁ kṛpayā muninā kṛtam \|\|*

**The women, śudras and dvija-bandhus were not qualified to hear the Vedas. Thinking what would be the best for the foolish in terms of the best sādhana, Vyāsa by his mercy wrote the** ***Mahābhārata. SB 1.4.25***

Because the Purāṇas explain the parts of the Vedas difficult to understand and because they complete the fragmented parts of the Vedas, the Vedas are firmly established in the Purāṇas. (This explains the quotation from Viṣṇu Purāṇa)

Though the Purāṇas are included as smṛtis among the works like Manu-smṛti which explain the Vedas, because they were manifested from the Supreme Lord in the form of Vyāsa they are superior. Proof for this is given in Padma Purāṇa.

### Text 16

*skānde –*  
*vyāsa-citta-sthitākāśād avacchinnāni kānicit \|*  
*anye vyavaharanty etāny urīkṛtya gṛhād iva \|\| iti \|*  
*tathaiva dṛṣṭaṁ śrī-viṣṇu-purāṇe parāśara-vākyam –*  
*tato’tra mat-suto vyāsa aṣṭāviṁśatime’ntare \|*  
*vedam ekaṁ catuṣpādaṁ caturdhā vyabhajat prabhuḥ \|\|*  
*yathātra tena vai vyastā veda-vyāsena dhīmatā \|*  
*vedas tathā samastais tair vyāsair anyais tathā mayā \|\|*  
*tad anenaiva vyāsānāṁ śākhābhedān dvijottama \|*  
*caturyugeṣu racitān samasteṣv avadhāraya \|\|*  
*kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyanaṁ vyāsaṁ viddhi nārāyaṇaṁ prabhum \|*  
*ko’nyo hi bhuvi maitreya mahābhārata-kṛd bhavet \|\| \[ViP 3.4.2-5\] iti \|*  
*skānda eva –*  
*nārāyaṇād viniṣpannaṁ jñānaṁ kṛta-yuge sthitam \|*  
*kiñcit tad anyathā jātaṁ tretāyāṁ dvāpare’khilam \|\|*  
*gautamasya ṛṣeḥ śāpāj jñāne tv ajñānatāṁ gate \|*  
*saṅkīrṇa-buddhayo devā brahma-rudra-puraḥsarāḥ \|\|*  
*śaraṇyaṁ śaraṇaṁ jagmur nārāyaṇam anāmayam \|*  
*tair vijñāpita-kāryas tu bhagavān puruṣottamaḥ \|\|*  
*avatīrṇo mahāyogī satyavatyāṁ parāśarāt \|*  
*utsannān bhagavān vedān ujjahāra hariḥ svayam \|\| iti \|*  
*veda-śabdenātra purāṇādi-dvayam api gṛhyate \| tad evam itihāsa-purāṇa-vicāra eva śreyān iti siddham \| tatrāpi purāṇasyaiva garimā dṛśyate \| uktaṁ hi nāradīye –*  
*vedārthād adhikaṁ manye purāṇārthaṁ varānane \|*  
*vedāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ sarve purāṇe nātra saṁśayaḥ \|\|*  
*purāṇam anyathā kṛtvā tiryag-yonim avāpnuyāt \|*  
*sudānto’pi suśānto’pi na gatiṁ kvacid āpnuyāt \|\| iti \|16\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In Skanda Purānā it is said:**

***vyāsa-citta-sthitākāśād avacchinnāni kānicit \|***  
***anye vyavaharanty etāny urīkṛtya gṛhād iva \|\|***

**Other sages accept and use some of Vyāsa's conclusions from the vast sky situated in Vyāsa's heart as if taking articles from a house. Skanda Purāṇa**

**Parāśara says:**

***tato’tra mat-suto vyāsa aṣṭāviṁśatime’ntare \|***  
***vedam ekaṁ catuṣpādaṁ caturdhā vyabhajat prabhuḥ \|\|***  
***yathātra tena vai vyastā veda-vyāsena dhīmatā \|***  
***vedas tathā samastais tair vyāsair anyais tathā mayā \|\|***  
***tad anenaiva vyāsānāṁ śākhābhedān dvijottama \|***  
***caturyugeṣu racitān samasteṣv avadhāraya \|\|***  
***kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyanaṁ vyāsaṁ viddhi nārāyaṇaṁ prabhum \|***  
***ko’nyo hi bhuvi maitreya mahābhārata-kṛd bhavet \|\|***

**In the twenty-eighth Manvantara, my son Vyāsa divided the one Veda of four parts into four Vedas. Just as the Vedas were compiled by intelligent Vyāsa, I have had this done by other Vyāsas as well. O best of the brāhmaṇas! Understand that in all cycles of four yugas there are different branches of the Vedas produced by other Vyāsas. Know that Kṛṣṇa-dvaipāyana Vyāsa is Lord Nārāyaṇa. O Maitreya! Who else could produce Mahābhārata on earth? Viṣṇu Purāṇa 3.4.2-5**

**In Skanda Purāṇa it is said:**

***nārāyaṇād viniṣpannaṁ jñānaṁ kṛta-yuge sthitam \|***  
***kiñcit tad anyathā jātaṁ tretāyāṁ dvāpare’khilam \|\|***  
***gautamasya ṛṣeḥ śāpāj jñāne tv ajñānatāṁ gate \|***  
***saṅkīrṇa-buddhayo devā brahma-rudra-puraḥsarāḥ \|\|***  
***śaraṇyaṁ śaraṇaṁ jagmur nārāyaṇam anāmayam \|***  
***tair vijñāpita-kāryas tu bhagavān puruṣottamaḥ \|\|***  
***avatīrṇo mahāyogī satyavatyāṁ parāśarāt \|***  
***utsannān bhagavān vedān ujjahāra hariḥ svayam \|\|***

**In Satya-yuga knowledge was produced and fixed by Nārāyaṇa. All this was produced in Tretā and Dvāpara- yugas with slight change. When, because of a curse of Gautama, the knowledge was lost, Brahmā, Śiva and other devatās developed limited intelligence. They approached pure Nārāyaṇa, the shelter of the jīvas. Informed of the situation, the Supreme Lord, the great yogī, appeared through Parāśara in the womb of Satyavatī and recovered the Vedas (Itihāsas and Purāṇas) which were lost.**

**Here the word** ***vedān*** **means the Itihāsas and Purāṇas. Thus it is proved that the conclusion of the Itihāsas and Purāṇas is best. From this one sees the greatness of the Purāṇas. It is said in Nāradīya Purāṇa:**

***vedārthād adhikaṁ manye purāṇārthaṁ varānane \|***  
***vedāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ sarve purāṇe nātra saṁśayaḥ \|\|***  
***purāṇam anyathā kṛtvā tiryag-yonim avāpnuyāt \|***  
***sudānto’pi suśānto’pi na gatiṁ kvacid āpnuyāt \|\|***

**O beautiful faced woman! I consider the meaning of the Purāṇas greater than the meaning of the Vedas. The Vedas are established in all the Purāṇas without doubt. If one disrespects the Purāṇas one attains an animal birth. Though controlling the senses and mind, one never attains the spiritual goal. Naradīya Purāṇa**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The knowledge of Vyāsa is a great sky. Knowledge belonging to others is a small part of his knowledge, like small pieces of sky. This means that because he is the Supreme Lord, he is omniscient. The son of Parāśara is greater than the other Vyāsas because he is the Supreme Lord. The Skanda Purāṇa quote shows clearly that Parāśara’s son is the Supreme Lord.

Gautama had the blessing of having continual piles of grain. He fed brāhmaṇas during a great famine. When the famine was over, they desired to leave but he forcibly stopped them. They made a cow by magic and by the touch of Gautama it died. Because he had killed a cow and was impure, they left. After doing atonement, Gautama discovered they had tricked him and he cursed them. They thus lost their knowledge. This is related in Varāha Purāṇa.

The meaning of the statement in Nāradīya Purāṇa is “I consider the meaning in the Purāṇas greater than the meaning of the Vedas, because there is no doubt about the meaning in the Purāṇas.” *Anyathā kṛtvā* in the verse from Nāradīya Purāṇa means “disrespecting.”

### Text 17

*skānda-prabhāsa-khaṇḍe --*  
*vedavan niścalaṁ manye purāṇārthaṁ dvijottamāḥ \|*  
*vedāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ sarve purāṇe nātra saṁśayaḥ \|\|*  
*bibhety alpa-śrutād vedī mām ayaṁ cālayiṣyati \|*  
*itihāsa-purāṇais tu niścalo’yaṁ kutaḥ purā \|\|*  
*yan na dṛṣṭaṁ hi vedeṣu tad dṛṣṭaṁ smṛtiṣu dvijāḥ \|*  
*ubhayor yan na dṛṣṭaṁ hi tat purāṇaiḥ pragīyate \|\|*  
*yo veda caturo vedān sāṅgopaniṣado dvijāḥ \|*  
*purāṇaṁ naiva jānāti na ca sa syād vicakṣaṇaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*atha purāṇānām evaṁ prāmāṇye sthite’pi teṣām api sāmastyenāpracarad-rūpatvāt nānādevatāpratipādaka-prāyatvād arvācīnaiḥ kṣudrar-buddhibhir artho duradhigama iti tad-avastha eva saṁśayaḥ \| yad uktaṁ mātsye –*  
*pañcāṅgaṁ ca purāṇaṁ syād ākhyānam itarat smṛtam \|*  
*sāttvikeṣu ca kalpeṣu māhātmyam adhikaṁ hareḥ \|\|*  
*rājaseṣu ca māhātmyam adhikaṁ brahmaṇo viduḥ \|*  
*tadvad agneś ca māhātmyaṁ tāmaseṣu śivasya ca \|*  
*saṅkīrṇeṣu sarasvatyāḥ pitṝṇāṁ ca nigadyate \|\| iti \|*  
*atrāgnes tat-tad-aganu pratipādyasya tat-tad-yajñasyety arthaḥ \| śivasya ceti cakārāch chivāyāś ca \| saṅkīrṇeṣu sattva-rajas-tamomayeṣu kalpeṣu bahuṣu \| sarasvatyāḥ nānāvāṇyātmaka-tad-upalakṣitāyā nānādevatāyā ity arthaḥ \| pitṝṇāṁ karmaṇā pitṛlokaḥ \[BAU 1.5.16\] iti \| śrutes tat prāpaka-karmaṇām ity arthaḥ \|\|17\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is said in Skanda Purāṇa:**

***vedavan niścalaṁ manye purāṇārthaṁ dvijottamāḥ \|***  
***vedāḥ pratiṣṭhitāḥ sarve purāṇe nātra saṁśayaḥ \|\|***  
***bibhety alpa-śrutād vedo mām ayaṁ cālayiṣyati \|***  
***itihāsa-purāṇais tu niścalo’yaṁ kṛtaḥ purā \|\|***  
***yan na dṛṣṭaṁ hi vedeṣu tad dṛṣṭaṁ smṛtiṣu dvijāḥ \|***  
***ubhayor yan na dṛṣṭaṁ hi tat purāṇaiḥ pragīyate \|\|***  
***yo veda caturo vedān sāṅgopaniṣado dvijāḥ \|***  
***purāṇaṁ naiva jānāti na ca sa syād vicakṣaṇaḥ \|\|***

**O best of the brāhmaṇas! I consider the meaning of the Purāṇas to be fixed like the Vedas. The Vedas are established in all the Purāṇas without doubt. The Vedas were afraid of people with little knowledge, thinking they would distort the meaning. The Vedas have been established by the Itihāsas and Purāṇas in ancient times. What is not seen in the Vedas is seen in the smṛtis. What is not seen in the smṛtis is described in the Purāṇas. The clever person who knows the Vedas and Upaniṣads but does not know the Purāṇas is not knowledgeable. Skanda Purāṇa, Prabhāsa-khaṇda 2.90-93**

**Though the Purāṇas are established as authority because they do not leave the meaning unsettled, those who are less intelligent still find the meaning of the Purāṇas difficult to grasp, since the Purāṇas are unavailable in complete form and since they instill faith in worshipping various devatās. Matsya Purāṇa says:**

***pañcāṅgaṁ ca purāṇaṁ syād ākhyānam itarat smṛtam \|***  
***sāttvikeṣu ca kalpeṣu māhātmyam adhikaṁ hareḥ \|\|***  
***rājaseṣu ca māhātmyam adhikaṁ brahmaṇo viduḥ \|***  
***tadvad agneś ca māhātmyaṁ tāmaseṣu śivasya ca \|***  
***saṅkīrṇeṣu sarasvatyāḥ pitṝṇāṁ ca nigadyate \|\|***

**It is said that the Purāṇas have five topics. Otherwise it is called ākhyāna. In sattvika works the glory of Viṣṇu predominates. In rājasic works the glory of Brahmā is greater. In tamasic works the glories of Agni and Śiva are greater. In mixed works the glory of Sarasvatī (as well as other devatās) and Pitṛs is greater.**

**Prominence of Agni means fire sacrifices. The word** ***ca*** **after Śiva indicates worship of Durgā. In works with a mixture of sattva, rajas and tamas, Sarasvatī and various devatās are worshipped.** ***Sarasvatyāḥ*** **(literally meaning “power of speech”) means many devatās or personalities having various messages. That is the meaning. The śrutis also mention actions for attaining the Pitṛs.** ***Karmaṇā pitṛlokaḥ:*** **one goes to Pitṛloka by performing karmas. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 1.5.16)**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The meaning of the Purāṇas is certain and like the Vedas is approved by all. “One can understand the meaning of the Vedas from the commentaries on the Vedas written by the wise sages instead of consulting the Purāṇas.” The Vedas are afraid of commentaries written with scant knowledge (*bibhety alpa-śrutād vedaḥ*). What is the use of manufactured works when there is already a commentary without human authorship?

Though the Purāṇas are the cause of accurate knowledge because of no confusion of meaning, the meaning—that the Lord endowed with his vibhūtis is supreme-- is not understood by recent persons of meager intelligence. The same meaning is stated in the following:

*veda rāmāyaṇe caiva purāṇe bhārate tathā \|*  
*ādāv ante ca madhye ca hariḥ sarvatra gīyate \|\|*

**In the Vedas, Rāmāyaṇa, Purāṇas and Mahābhārata, in the beginning, middle and end, the Lord is glorified everywhere. Hari-vaṁśa 132.95**

The ignorant are those who do not understand this point.

### Text 18

*tad evaṁ sati tat-tat-kalpa-kathāmayatvenaiva mātsya eva prasiddhānāṁ tat-tat-purāṇānāṁ vyavasthā jñāpitā \| tāratamyaṁ tu kathaṁ syāt yenetara-nirṇayaḥ kriyeta \| sattvādi-tāratamyenaiveti cet, sattvāt sañjāyate jñānam iti sattvaṁ yad brahma-darśanam iti ca nyāyāt sāttvikam eva purāṇādika paramārtha-jñānāya prablama ity āeyātam \| tathāpi paramārthe’pi nānā-bhaṅgyā vipratipadyamānānāṁ samādhānāya kiṁ syāt \| yadi sarvasyāpi vedasya purāṇasya cārtha-nirṇayāya tenaiva śrī-bhagavatā vyāsena brahma-sūtraṁ kṛtaṁ tad-avalokanenaiva sarvo’rtho nirṇaye ity ucyate \| tarhi nānya-sūtrakāra-muny-anugatair manyeta \| kiṁ cātyanta-gūḍhārthānām alpākṣarāṇāṁ tat-sūtrāṇām anyārthatvaṁ kaścid ācakṣīta \| tataḥ katarad ivātra samādhānam \| tad eva samādheyam yady ekatamam eva purāṇa-lakṣaṇam apauruṣeyaṁ śāstraṁ sarva-vedetihāsa-purāṇānām artha-sāraṁ brahma-sūtropajīvyaṁ ca bhavad bhuvi sampūrṇaṁ pracarad-rūpaṁ syāt \| satyam uktam, yata eva ca sarva-pramāṇānāṁ cakravarti-bhūtam asmad-abhimataṁ śrīmad-bhāgavatam evodbhāvitaṁ bhavatā \|\|18\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Matsya Purāṇa establishes the classification of famous Purāṇas according to the topics of the respective works. There are means to distinguish between them to determine which Purāṇa is the best. There is a hierarchy according to the guṇas.**

***sattvāt sañjāyate jñānaṁ rajaso lobha eva ca***  
***pramāda-mohau tamaso bhavato ’jñānam eva ca \|\|***

**Knowledge arises from sattva. Greed arises from rajas. Inattention, confusion and ignorance arise from tamas. BG 14.17**

***pārthivād dāruṇo dhūmas tasmād agnis trayīmayaḥ \|***  
***tamasas tu rajas tasmāt sattvaṁ yad brahma-darśanam \|\|***

**Smoke is superior to dull wood, and fire, sacred to the Vedas, is superior to smoke. Similarly rajas is superior to tamas, and sattva is superior to rajas, since it is favorable for realizing the Lord. SB 1.2.24**

**If one classifies the Purāṇas according to guṇa, this means that sattvika Purāṇas are stronger for giving spiritual knowledge.**

**Even accepting the superiority of sattvika Purāṇas, how can one solve conflicting ideas concerning the ultimate spiritual goal made by logicians using various devices? It is said that, since the Lord in the form of Vyāsa wrote the Brahma-sūtras for explaining the meaning of all the Vedas and Purāṇas, by consulting it one can discern the meaning of all scriptures. But it is not understood by followers of various other sages who wrote other sūtras. Some will explain opposite meaning in the sūtras which are sparse in syllables and extremely deep in meaning. What is the solution?**

**The problem will be solved if there is one** ***apauruṣeya,*** **well-accepted, complete scripture manifest on earth, which explains Brahma-sūtras and which contains the essential meaning of all the Vedas, Itihāsas and Purāṇas.**

**This is a good point, because you have revealed by your questioning the Śrīmad-bhāgavatam, the king of all proofs, which is approved by me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

“But the Brahma-sūtras are also included in the Vedas. They can be used as proof.” But their meaning is very deep and expressed in few words.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The enumeration of the Purāṇas occurs in the chapter describing the results of gifting the Purāṇas. What will be the gradation of inferior and superior natures by which the superior one will be distinguished? He quotes verses to show that sāttvika Purāṇas are superior. He asks a question. Even though we accept sattvika Purāṇas as superior for discerning the highest goal, the highest goal is defined as having qualities, having no qualities, composed of knowledge only and completely unconscious by various types of crooked logic.

Though Brahmā-sūtras may be suggested as the explanation of the Vedas, various followers of sages like Gautama do not accept this. Why should we give regard to other sūtras when the Brahma-sūtras are accepted? Even then, some sages give other meanings to the sūtras which have deep meaning and few syllables.

The answer is now given. The ultimate scripture should firmly establish the meaning of Brahma-sūtras. The heart of the questioner then becomes clear: you have spoken correctly, because you have revealed (by your questioning) the Śrīmad-bhāgavatam, the king of all scriptures, which is approved by me.

### Text 19

*yat khalu purāṇa-jātam āvirbhāvya brahma-sūtraṁ ca praṇīyāpya aparituṣṭena tena bhagavatā nija-sūtrāṇām akṛtrima-bhāṣya-bhūtaṁ samādhi-labdham āvirbhāvitaṁ yasminn eva sarva-śāstra-samanvayo dṛśyate \| sarva-vedārtha-lakṣaṇāṁ gāyatrīm adhikṛtya pravartitatvāt \| tathā hi tat-svarūpaṁ mātsye –*  
*yatrādhikṛtya gāyatrīṁ varṇyate dharma-vistaraḥ \|*  
*vṛtrāsura-vadhopetaṁ tad-bhāgavatam iṣyate \|\|*  
*likhitvā tac ca yo dadyād dhema-siṁha-samanvitam \|*  
*prauṣṭha-padyāṁ paurṇamāsyāṁ sa yāti paramāṁ gatim \|*  
*aṣṭādaśa-sahasrāṇi purāṇaṁ tat prakīrtitam \|\|*  
*atra gāyatrī-śabdena tat-sūcaka-tad-avyabhicāri-dhīmahi-pada-saṁvalita-tad-arthaṁ eveṣyate \| sarveṣāṁ mantrāṇām ādirūpāyās tasyāḥ sākṣāt-kathanānarhatvāt \| tad-arthatā ca janmādy asya yataḥ, tena brahma hṛdā iti sarva-lokāśrayatva-buddhi-vṛtti-prerakatvādi-sāmyāt \| dharma-vistara ity atra dharma-śabdaḥ parama-dharma-paraḥ \| dharmaḥ projjhita-kaitavo’tra paramaḥ ity atraiva pratipāditatvāt \| sa ca bhagavad-dhyānādi-lakṣaṇa eveti purastād vyaktībhaviṣyati \|\|19\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Having manifested Purāṇas which were already there, having composed the Brahma-sūtras, Vyāsa was still unsatisfied. He then manifested in samādhi the natural commentary on the Brahma-sūtras in which there is a harmonization of all scriptures. Thus it begins with a reference to gāyatri, which represents the meaning of all the Vedas. The svarūpa of the Bhāgavatam is explained in Matsya Purāṇa:**

***yatrādhikṛtya gāyatrīṁ varṇyate dharma-vistaraḥ \|***  
***vṛtrāsura-vadhopetaṁ tad-bhāgavatam iṣyate \|\|***

**It is called the Bhāgavatam, which describes the killing of Vṛtrāsura, and in which dharma (bhakti) is described in detail after referring to the gāyatrī. Matsya Purāṇa 53.20**

***likhitvā tac ca yo dadyād dhema-siṁha-samanvitam \|***  
***prauṣṭha-padyāṁ paurṇamāsyāṁ sa yāti paramāṁ gatim \|***  
***aṣṭādaśa-sahasrāṇi purāṇaṁ tat prakīrtitam \|\|***

**The person who writes out the Bhāgavatam and the gifts it, placing it on a golden throne on the full moon of Bhadrā month, attains the highest goal. It is known as the Purāṇa with eighteen thousand verses. Matsya Purāṇa 53.22**

**The reference to gāyatrī is the word** ***dhīmahi*** **which invariably occurs in the gāyatrī mantra and is placed in the first verse of Bhāgavatam. The whole mantra is not given since it is not proper to directly show the original form of mantras. But other words in the first verse indicate the meaning of gāyatrī. The words** ***janmādy asya yatah*** **and** ***tene brahma hṛdā ya ādi-kavaye*** **indicates the Lord who is shelter of all the planets and who inspires the intelligence of Brahmā. (This is similar to the gāyatrī mantra which prays that our intelligence may be inspired by the Lord.)** ***Dharma-vistāraḥ*** **in the quote from Matsya Purāṇa means a detailed description of the highest dharma, bhakti. That is indicated in** ***dharmaḥ projjhita-kaitavo ‘tra paramaḥ,*** **the supreme dharma which completely rejects cheating, in the second verse. This means Bhāgavatam presents meditation on the Lord. This will be explained later.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

This section praises Bhāgavatam. Vyāsa was unsatisfied with the Purāṇas and Brahma-sūtras because the sweetness and highest powers of the Supreme Lord (Kṛṣṇa) were unclear and secret in the manifested Purāṇas and the Brahma-sūtras. He was satisfied with Bhāgavatam because in that work there are statements showing the Lord’s exceptional powers and sweetness.

The meaning of gāyatrī is expressed in *janmādy asya yataḥ* (the Lord from whom arises creation and destruction of the universe), also in the first verse of Bhāgavatam. This indicates meditation on Bhagavān. In other words, it indicates the path of pure bhakti.

### Text 20

*evaṁ skānde prabhāsa-khaṇḍe ca yatrādhikṛtya gāyatrīm ity ādi \|*  
*sārasvatasya kalpasya madhye ye syur narāmarāḥ \|*  
*sad-vṛttānodbhavaṁ loke tac ca bhāgavataṁ smṛtam \|\|*  
*likhitvā tac ca ity ādi \| aṣṭādaśa-sahasrāṇi purāṇaṁ tat prakīrtitam iti purāṇāntaraṁ ca –*  
*grantho’ṣṭādaśa-sāhasro dvādaśa-skandha-sammitaḥ \|*  
*hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā yatra vṛtra-vadhas tathā \|*  
*gāyatryā ca samārambhas tad vai bhāgavataṁ viduḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*atra hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā iti vṛtra-vadha-sāhacaryeṇa nārāyaṇa-varmaivocyate \| hayagrīva-śabdenātrāśvaśirā dadhīcir evocyate \| tenaiva ca pravartitā nārāyaṇa-varmākhyā brahma-vidyā \| tasyāśva-śirastvaṁ ca ṣaṣṭhe yad vā aśva-śiro nāma ity atra prasiddhaṁ nārāyaṇa-varmaṇo brahma-vidyātvaṁ ca –*  
*etac chrutvā tathovāca dadhyaṅ ātharvaṇas tayoḥ \|*  
*pravargyaṁ brahma-vidyāṁ ca sat-kṛto’satya-śaṅkitaḥ \|\| iti*  
*ṭīkotthāpita-vacanena ceti \| śrīmad-bhāgavatasya bhagavat-priyatvena bhāgavatābhīṣṭatvena ca parama-sāttvikatvam \| yathā pādme ambarīṣaṁ prati gautama-praśnaḥ –*  
*purāṇaṁ tvaṁ bhāgavataṁ paṭhase purato hareḥ \|*  
*caritaṁ daitya-rājasya prahlādasya ca bhūpate \|\|*  
*tatraiva vyañjulī-māhātmye tasya tasminn upadeśaḥ –*  
*rātrau tu jāgaraḥ kāryaḥ śrotavyā vaiṣṇavī kathā \|*  
*gītā nā a sahasraṁ ca purāṇaṁ śuka-bhāṣitam \|*  
*paṭhitavyaṁ prayatnena hareḥ santoṣa-kāraṇam \|\|*  
*tatraivānyatra –*  
*ambarīṣa śuka-proktaṁ nityaṁ bhāgavataṁ śṛṇu \|*  
*paṭhasva sva-mukhenaiva yadīcchasi bhava-kṣayam \|\|*  
*skānde prahlāda-saṁhitāyāṁ dvārakā-māhātmye –*  
*śrīmad-bhāgavataṁ bhaktyā paṭhate hari-sannidhau \|*  
*jāgare tat-padaṁ yāti kula-vṛnda-samanvitaḥ \|\|20\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In Skanda Purāṇa there are seen the same verses that are found in Matsya Purāṇa:**

***sārasvatasya kalpasya madhye ye syuḥ narāmarāḥ***  
***sad-vṛttāntodbhavaṁ loke tac ca bhāgavataṁ smṛtam***

**That which describes the activities of dharma of men and devatās during the Sārasvata-kalpa is called the Bhāgavatam.**

***likhitvā tac ca yo dadyād dhema-siṁha-samanvitam \|***  
***prauṣṭha-padyāṁ paurṇamāsyāṁ sa yāti paramāṁ gatim \|***  
***aṣṭādaśa-sahasrāṇi purāṇaṁ tat prakīrtitam \|\|***

**The person who writes out the Bhāgavatam and the gifts it, placing it on a golden throne on the full moon of Bhadrā month, attains the highest goal. It is known as the Purāṇa with eighteen thousand verses. Matsya Purāṇa 53.22**

**Elsewhere also eighteen thousand verses are mentioned.**

***grantho’ṣṭādaśa-sāhasro dvādaśa-skandha-sammitaḥ \|***  
***hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā yatra vṛtra-vadhas tathā \|***  
***gāyatryā ca samārambhas tad vai bhāgavataṁ viduḥ \|\|***

**The scripture has eighteen thousand verses and twelve volumes. In it, there is Hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā and the killing of Vṛtrāsura. It begins with the gāyatrī mantra. This is known as Bhāgavatam.**

**Hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā refers to the Nārāyaṇa-kavaca used in the killing of Vṛtrāsura. The word Hayagrīva (horse neck) refers to Dadhīci who had a horse’s head. He explained the Nārāyaṇa-kavaca which is known as brahma-vidyā. His horse head is described in the Sixth Canto:**

***sa vā adhigato dadhyaṅṅ aśvibhyāṁ brahma niṣkalam***  
***yad vā aśvaśiro nāma tayor amaratāṁ vyadhāt***

**Dadhīci personally assimilated the spiritual science called Aśvaśira (horse head) and then delivered it to the Aśvinī-kumāras. The Aśvinī-kumāras then became jīvan-muktas*****,*** **liberated even in this life. SB 6.9.52**

**The brahma-vidyā is described:**

***etac chrutvā tathovāca dadhyaṅ ātharvaṇas tayoḥ \|***  
***pravargyaṁ brahma-vidyāṁ ca sat-kṛto’satya-śaṅkitaḥ \|\|***

**Hearing this, Dadhyaṇ Ātharvaṇas, being worshipped by the Āśvini kumāras and fearing untruthfulness, spoke Brahma-vidyā and prāṇa-vidyā to them.**

**This is quoted in the commentary. Śrīmad Bhāgavatam is the highest sattvika scripture since it is dear to the Lord and treasured by the devotees.**

**Gautama says to Ambarīṣa:**

***purāṇaṁ tvaṁ bhāgavataṁ paṭhase purato hareḥ \|***  
***caritaṁ daitya-rājasya prahlādasya ca bhūpate \|\|***

**In front of the deity you should read Bhāgavatam, which contains the story of Prahlāda and the king of the demons. Padma Purāṇa**

**In glorifying Vyañjulī Mahā-dvādasī, this teaching is given:**

***rātrau tu jāgaraḥ kāryaḥ śrotavyā vaiṣṇavī kathā \|***  
***gītānām asahasraṁ ca purāṇaṁ śuka-bhāṣitam \|***  
***paṭhitavyaṁ prayatnena hareḥ santoṣa-kāraṇam \|\|***

**One should stay awake at night and hear about the Lord. One should attentively recite Bhagavad Gītā, the thousand names of Viṣṇu, and the Purāṇa spoken by Śukadeva, which pleases the Lord. Padma Purāṇa**

**Elsewhere it is said:**

***ambarīṣa śuka-proktaṁ nityaṁ bhāgavataṁ śṛṇu \|***  
***paṭhasva sva-mukhenaiva yadīcchasi bhava-kṣayam \|\|***

**O Ambarīṣa! Hear constantly the Bhāgavatam spoken by Śukadeva. Read it with your own mouth if you desire destruction of material life. Padma Purāṇa**

**In Skanda Purāṇa, Dvārakā-māhātmya, it is said:**

***śrīmad-bhāgavataṁ bhaktyā paṭhate hari-sannidhau \|***  
***jāgare tat-padaṁ yāti kula-vṛnda-samanvitaḥ \|***

**Reciting Bhāgavatam with devotion near the deity while staying awake at night, a person goes to Vaikuṇṭha with all his family. Skanda Purāṇa**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

This section gives other proofs for understanding the nature of Bhāgavatam. Those quotes from Skanda Purāṇa are the same as those in Matsya Purāṇa. The pastimes in other kalpas like Sārasvata-kalpa are generally the same. When Skanda Purāṇa says *padma-kalpam atho śṛnu*, the pastimes in this kalpa are also found in other kalpas.

Skanda Purāṇa says that Mahābhārata appeared after the eighteen Purāṇas. This is contrary to the statements in Bhāgavatam and Garuḍa Purāṇa. Thus it is explained that Mahābhārata was written previously and was then spoken to Janamejaya and others later. This is how the various *pramāṇas* are explained.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The author explains the words *hayagrīva-brahma-vidyā* in order to prevent mistaken meanings. Dadhyaṅ means Dadhīci. *Pravargyam* means *prāṇa-vidyā.*

The question may be asked “There are five other sattvika Purāṇas such as Padma Purāṇa. Why can they not be the authority?” Śrīmad Bhāgavatam is the highest sattvika scripture since it is dear to the Lord and treasured by the devotees. He quotes Padma Purāṇa which states the highest position of Bhāgavatam.

By the power of a single person hearing Bhāgavatam, his whole family (*kula-vṛnda-samanvitaḥ*) attains the abode of the Lord.

### Text 21

*gāruḍe ca –*  
*pūrṇaḥ so’yam atiśayaḥ \|*  
*artho’yaṁ brahma-sūtrāṇāṁ bhāratārtha-vinirṇayaḥ \|\|*  
*gāyatrī-bhāṣya-rūpo’sau vedārtha-paribṛṁhitaḥ \|*  
*purāṇānāṁ sāma-rūpaḥ sākṣād-bhagavatoditaḥ \|\|*  
*dvādaśa-skandha-yukto’yaṁ śatavic-cheda-saṁyutaḥ \|*  
*grantho’ṣṭādaśa-sāhasraḥ śrīmad-bhāgavatābhidhaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*brahma-sūtrāṇām arthas teṣām akṛtrima-bhāṣya-bhūta ity arthaḥ \| pūrvaṁ sūkṣmatvena manasy āvirbhūtaṁ tad eva saṅkṣipya sūtratvena punaḥ prakaṭitam paścād vistīrṇatvena sākṣāc-chrī-bhāgavatam iti \| tasmāt tad-bhāṣya-bhūte svataḥ-siddhe tasmin satyarvacīnam anyad anyeṣāṁ svasvakapola-kalpitaṁ tad-anugatam evādaraṇīyam iti gamyate \|*  
*bhāratārtha-vinirṇayaḥ –*  
*nirṇayaḥ sarva-śāstrāṇāṁ bhārataṁ parikīrtitam \|*  
*bhārataṁ sarva-vedāś ca tulām āropitāḥ purā \|*  
*devair brahmādibhiḥ sarvair ṛṣibhiś ca samanvitaiḥ \|\|*  
*vyāsasyaivājñayā tatra tvatyaricyata bhāratam \|*  
*mahattvād bhāravattvāc ca mahābhāratam ucyate \|\|*  
*ity ādy-ukta-lakṣaṇasya bhāratasyārtha-vinirṇayo yatra saḥ \| śrī-bhagavaty eva tātparyaṁ tasyāpi \| tad uktaṁ mokṣa-dharme nārāyaṇīye śrī-veda-vyāsaṁ prati janamejayena –*  
*idaṁ śata sahasrād dhi bhāratākhyāna-vistarāt \|*  
*āmathya matimanthena jñānodadhim anuttamam \|\|*  
*nava nītaṁ yathā dadhno malayāc candanaṁ yathā \|*  
*āraṇyakaṁ ca vedebhya oṣadhibhyo 'mṛtaṁ yathā \|\|*  
*samuddhṛtam idaṁ brahman kathāmṛtam anuttamam \|*  
*tapo nidhe tvayoktaṁ hi nārāyaṇa kathāśrayam \|\| iti \|21\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Garuḍa Purāṇa says:**

***pūrṇaḥ so’yam atiśayaḥ \|***  
***artho’yaṁ brahma-sūtrāṇāṁ bhāratārtha-vinirṇayaḥ \|\|***  
***gāyatrī-bhāṣya-rūpo’sau vedārtha-paribṛṁhitaḥ \|***  
***purāṇānāṁ sāma-rūpaḥ sākṣād-bhagavatoditaḥ \|\|***  
***dvādaśa-skandha-yukto’yaṁ śatavic-cheda-saṁyutaḥ \|***  
***grantho’ṣṭādaśa-sāhasraḥ śrīmad-bhāgavatābhidhaḥ \|\|***

**This work is most complete. It expresses the meaning of the Brahma-sūtras and Mahābhārata. It is the explanation of gāyatrī and reinforces the meaning of the Vedas. It is the Sāma Veda among the Purāṇas and has arisen directly from the Lord. It has twelve volumes and a hundred divisions. It has eighteen thousand verses. It is called Śrīmad-bhāgavatam.**

**It is the meaning of the Brahma-sūtras. This means it is the natural commentary on the sutras. Previously it appeared in his mind in a subtle form. Abbreviated, it appeared again as the sutras. Later it appeared as Bhāgavatam with great details. Though this is the self-evident commentary on the sūtras, other recent commentaries made by independent thinking are to be respected if they follow the Bhāgavatam.**

**Bhāgavatam gives the meaning of Mahābhārata (*****bhāratārtha-vinirṇayaḥ*****). (That meaning is described as follows.)**

***nirṇayaḥ sarva-śāstrāṇāṁ bhārataṁ parikīrtitam \|***  
***bhārataṁ sarva-vedāś ca tulām āropitāḥ purā \|***  
***devair brahmādibhiḥ sarvair ṛṣibhiś ca samanvitaiḥ \|\|***  
***vyāsasyaivājñayā tatra tv atiricyate bhāratam \|***  
***mahattvād bhāravattvāc ca mahābhāratam ucyate \|\| 1.1.209***

**Mahābhārata is famous as the conclusion of all scriptures. Previously Mahābhārata was weighed on scales against all the Vedas by all devatās such as Brahmā and all sages, on the order of Vyāsa. Mahābhārata surpassed the Vedas on the scale. It is called Mahābhārata because it is huge and weighty.**

**“Describing the meaning of Mahābhārata” means Mahābhārata is the sum of all the Vedas as mentioned above and Bhāgavatam has the same meaning as Mahābhārata (*****bhāratasyārtha-vinirṇayaḥ*****).**

**Janamejaya speaks to Veda-vyāsa concerning the work called Nārāyanīya in the Mokṣa-dharma section of Mahābhārata:**

***idaṁ śatasahasrād dhi bhāratākhyāna vistarāt \|***  
***āmathya matimanthena jñānodadhim anuttamam \|\|***  
***nava nītaṁ yathā dadhno malayāc candanaṁ yathā \|***  
***āraṇyakaṁ ca vedebhya oṣadhibhyo 'mṛtaṁ yathā \|\|***  
***samuddhṛtam idaṁ brahman kathāmṛtam anuttamam \|***  
***tapo nidhe tvayoktaṁ hi nārāyaṇa kathāśrayam \|\|***

**O ocean of austerity! Just as butter is churned from yogurt, sandalwood is extracted from Malaya, Upaniṣads are extracted from the Vedas and nectar is extracted from herbs, so from the abundant stories of the Mahābhārata with a hundred thousand verses, using the rod of intelligence, the incomparable sweet topics concerning Nārāyaṇa were extracted and spoken by you. Mahābhārata 12.331.2-4**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The Garuḍa Purāṇa states that Bhāgavatam is the supreme sattvika Purāṇa and that it elucidates the meaning of the Brahma-sūtras. The author then explains the meaning of the verses.

Though Bhāgavatam is the natural commentary, one can respect recent commentaries on Brahma-sūtras made by Vaiṣṇava ācāryas which are not contrary to the Bhāgavatam (*tad-anugatam*). This indicates that other commentaries which are contrary, such as those written by Śaṅkara, Bhaṭṭa and Bhāskara, are to be avoided.

Bhāgavatam explains the meaning of Mahābhārata. To show the meaning of Mahābhārata, the epic’s svarūpa is shown by quoting Mahābhārata. What is the meaning of Mahābhārata? Its meaning is in the Bhāgavatam. Statements in Nārāyanīya are quoted to show that Bhāgavatam gives the meaning of Mahābhārata.

### Text 22

*tathā ca tṛtīye –*  
*munir vivakṣur bhagavad-guṇānāṁ*  
*sakhāpi te bhāratam āha kṛṣṇaḥ \|*  
*yasmin nṛṇāṁ grāmya-sukhānuvādair*  
*matir gṛhītā nu hareḥ kathāyām \|\| iti*  
*tasmād gāyatrī-bhāṣya-rūpo’sau \| tathaiva hi viṣṇudharmottarādau tad-vyākhyāne bhagavān eva vistareṇa pratipāditaḥ \| atra janmādyasya ity asya vyākhyānaṁ ca tathā darśayiṣyate \|*  
*vedārtha-paribṛṁhitaḥ \| vedārthasya paribṛṁhaṇaṁ yasmāt \| tac coktam itihāsa-purāṇābhyām ity ādi \| purāṇānāṁ sāma-rūpaḥ \| vedeṣu sāmavat sa teṣu śreṣṭha ity arthaḥ \| ataeva skānde –*  
*śataśo’tha sahasraiś ca kim anyaiḥ śāstra-saṅgrahaiḥ \|*  
*na yasya tiṣṭhate gehe śāstraṁ bhāgavataṁ kalau \|\|*  
*kathaṁ sa vaiṣṇavo jñeyaḥ śāstraṁ bhāgavataṁ kalau \|*  
*gṛhe na tiṣṭhate yasya sa vipraḥ śvapacādhamaḥ \|\|*  
*yatra yatra bhaved vipra śāstraṁ bhāgavataṁ kalau \|*  
*tatra tatra harir yāti tridaśaiḥ saha nārada \|\|*  
*yaḥ paṭhet prayato nityaṁ ślokaṁ bhāgavataṁ mune \|*  
*aṣṭādaśa-purāṇānāṁ phalaṁ prāpnoti mānavaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*śata-viccheda-saṁyutaḥ \| pañcatriṁśad-adhika-śata-trayādhyāya-viśiṣṭa ity arthaḥ \| spaṣṭārtham anyat \| tad evaṁ paramārtha-vivitsubhiḥ śrī-bhāgavatam eva sāmprataṁ vicāraṇīyam iti sthitam \|\|22\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Again Mahābhārata is described (as delineating Bhagavān):**

***munir vivakṣur bhagavad-guṇānāṁ***  
***sakhāpi te bhāratam āha kṛṣṇaḥ***  
***yasmin nṛṇāṁ grāmya-sukhānuvādair***  
***matir gṛhītā nu hareḥ kathāyām***

**O sage! Your friend Vedavyāsa, desiring to describe the qualities of the Lord, wrote Mahābhārata. In that work he attracted men’s attention to topics of the Lord through topics about material happiness. SB 3.5.12**

**Therefore Bhāgavatam is the form of gāyatrī (*****gāyatrī-bhāṣya-rūpaḥ*****) in the quote from Garuḍa Purāṇa (since gāyatri is also a description of Bhagavān). In Viṣṇu-dharmottara, while explaining gāyatrī, Bhagavān is described in detail as its subject. The explanation of** ***janmādyasya yataḥ*** **will be given later to show this.**

**It makes the meaning of the Vedas clear (*****vedārtha-paribṛṁhitaḥ*** **from the GaruḍaPurāṇa quoted in the previous anuccheda). That was explained with the words** ***itihāsa-purāṇābhyāṁ vedaṁ samupabṛṁhayet*****: one should make the Vedas clear in meaning by the Itihāsas and Purāṇas. (Mahābhārata 1.1.267)**

**It is the Sāma Veda among the Purāṇas (*****sāma-rūpaḥ*** **in the Garuḍa Purāṇa quote). This means it is best among the Purāṇas just as the Sāma is best among the Vedas.**

***śataśo’tha sahasraiś ca kim anyaiḥ śāstra-saṅgrahaiḥ \|***  
***na yasya tiṣṭhate gehe śāstraṁ bhāgavataṁ kalau \|\|***  
***kathaṁ sa vaiṣṇavo jñeyaḥ śāstraṁ bhāgavataṁ kalau \|***  
***gṛhe na tiṣṭhate yasya sa vipraḥ śvapacādhamaḥ \|\|***  
***yatra yatra bhaved vipra śāstraṁ bhāgavataṁ kalau \|***  
***tatra tatra harir yāti tridaśaiḥ saha nārada \|\|***  
***yaḥ paṭhet prayato nityaṁ ślokaṁ bhāgavataṁ mune \|***  
***aṣṭādaśa-purāṇānāṁ phalaṁ prāpnoti mānavaḥ \|\|***

**For a person who does not have Bhāgavatam in his house in Kali-yuga, what is the use of hundreds and thousands of collections of other scriptures? How can he be considered a devotee? The brāhmaṇa who does not have Bhāgavatam in his house in Kali-yuga is lower than a dog eater. O brāhmaṇa! Wherever there is Bhāgavatam, there the Lord comes with the devatās! O Nārada! O sage! The person who recites daily the verses of Bhāgavatam with attention attains the result of the eighteen Purāṇas. Skanda Purāṇa**

***Śata-viccheda-saṁyutaḥ*** **means it has three hundred and thirty-five chapters. The rest of the Garuḍa Purāṇa quotation is clear in meaning. Since Bhāgavatam’s outstanding qualities have been proved, it is established that persons desiring the highest result should study Bhāgavatam.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

“How is Bhāgavatam to be accepted as defining the meaning of Mahābhārata?” A quote from the Third Canto is given. Vidura speaks to Maitreya. Vyāsa (*kṛṣṇaḥ*) was a friend (*sākhā*) of Maitreya since Vyāsa was the son of his guru (Parāśara). Mahābhārata has stories concerning the material world to show necessity of household dharma (*grāmya*) with examples like the mouse and cat, vulture and jackal. Probably this is the story of the mouse Palita and the cat caught in a hunter’s net. A brāhmaṇa’s son died. A vulture lectured the mourners on acceptance of inevitable death and a jackal lectured them on expression of love for offspring. 16 An assembly of men gather to hear Mahābhārata for stories interesting to them because they encourage material benefits, but by hearing Gītā they turn their minds to the Lord.

Bhāgavatam explains the subjects mentioned in Mahābhārata. Actually Mahābhārata depends on the Bhāgavatam. That is explained by the Bhāgavatam.

The verse from Skanda Purāṇa shows that Bhāgavatam is the best of the Purāṇas, like the Sāma among the Vedas. The meaning is clear. Thus (*tad evam*), since its outstanding qualities have been proved, Bhāgavatam should be studied.

### Text 23

*ataeva satsv api nānā-śāstreṣv etad evoktam – kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko’dhunoditaḥ iti \| arkatā-rūpakeṇa tad vinā nānyeṣāṁ samyag-vastu-prakāśakatvam iti pratipadyate \| yasyaiva śrīmad-bhāgavatasya bhāṣya-bhūtaṁ śrī-hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātre śāstra-prastāve gaṇitaṁ tantra-bhāgavatābhidhaṁ tantram \| yasya sākṣāc chrī-hanumadbhāṣya-vāsanābhāṣya-sambandhokti-vidvatkāmadhenu-tattvadīpikā-bhāvārthadīpikā-paramahaṁsapriyā-śukahṛdayādayo vyākhyā-granthāḥ \| tathā muktāphala-harilīlā-bhaktiratnāvaly-ādayo nibandhāś ca vividhā eva tat-tan-mata-prasiddha-mahānubhāva-kṛtā virājante \| yad eva ca hemādri-granthasya dāna-khaṇḍe purāṇa-dāna-prastāve matsya-purāṇīya-tal-lakṣaṇa-dhṛtyā praśastam \| hemādri-pariśeṣa-khaṇḍasya kāla-nirṇaye ca kali-yuga-dharma-nirṇaye kaliṁ sabhājayanty āryāḥ ity ādikaṁ yad-vākyatvenotthāpya yat pratipādita-dharma eva kalāv aṅgīkṛtaḥ \|*  
*atha yad eva kaivalyam apy atikramya bhakti-sukha-vyāhārādi-liṅgena nija-matasyāpy upari virājamānārthaṁ matvā yad apauruṣeyaṁ vedānta-vyākhyānaṁ bhayād acālayataiva śaṅkarāvatāratayā prasiddhena vakṣyamāṇa-svagopanādi-hetuka-bhagavad-ājñā-pravartitādvaya-vādenāpi tan-mātra-varṇita-viśva-rūpa-darśana-kṛta-vrajeśvarī-vismaya-śrī-vraja-kumārī-vasana-cauryādikaṁ govindāṣṭakādau varṇayatā taṭasthībhūya nija-vacaḥ-sāphalyāya spṛṣṭam iti \|\|23\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus, because of the excellent of qualities mentioned, though there are various scriptures, it is said:**

***kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha \|***  
***kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ’dhunoditaḥ \|\|***

**Though Kṛṣṇa left Dvārakā, arrived at Prabhāsa, and then disappeared along with his six great qualities, this Purāṇa, another form of the sun, has now risen in Kali-yuga for those who have lost their knowledge. SB 1.3.43**

**This means that other things cannot directly reveal an object without the sun. (Similarly without Bhāgavatam other scriptures cannot reveal the Lord directly.) The Tantra-bhāgavata, listed among scriptures in Hayaśīrṣa-pañcarātra, is a commentary on Bhāgavatam. There are other commentaries on Bhāgavatam called Hanumad-bhāṣya, Vāsanā-bhāṣya, Sambandhokti, Vidvat-kāma-dhenu, Tattva-dīpikā, Bhāvārtha-dīpikā (Śrīdhara Svāmī), Paramahaṁsa-priyā and Śuka-hṛdaya.**

**There are other recent works like Mukthā-phala, Hari-līlā, and Bhakti-ratnāvalī written by great souls famous for their own ideas (which comment on Bhāgavatam). Hemādri in Dāna-khaṇḍa speaks of donating Purāṇas and praises Bhāgavatam by quoting from Matsya Purāṇa. In the last section, Hemādri, discussing time, and in describing Kali-yuga, quoting the following from Bhāgavatam, accepts the dharma of Kali-yuga mentioned in Bhāgavatam:**

***kaliṁ sabhājayanty āryā guṇa jñāḥ sāra-bhāginaḥ***  
***yatra saṅkīrtanenaiva sarva-svārtho ’bhilabhyate***

**Those who are actually advanced in knowledge, who know quality, who have accepted the best, praise Kali-yuga because in this fallen age all perfection of life can easily be achieved by the performance of saṅkīrtana. SB 11.5.36**

**Though advocating Advaita philosophy by the order of the Lord in order to disguise himself, the avatāra of Śiva, Śaṅkara, thinking of surpassing liberation and showing Bhāgavatam’s superiority to his own ideas by illustrating the joy of bhakti, only indirectly touched Bhāgavatam in order make his words successful. Thus he described in Govindāṣṭaka Yaśodā’s astonishment on seeing the universal form and stealing the clothing of the young gopīs while avoiding Advaita commentary. He feared giving a false interpretation of the apauruṣeya Bhāgavatam which is the explanation of Brahma-sūtras.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Thus (*ataeva*)--because of the excellent of qualities mentioned above--though there are many Purāṇas, Bhāgavatam is described as the sun for Kali-yuga.

Bhāgavatam is accepted by ancient sages and modern scholars as well. That is illustrated by listing the commentaries. At the present moment works like Muktā-phala have appeared. Bhāgavatam is also accepted by authors of dharma-śāstra. Hemādri is quoted to show this. In his work, he accepts the dharma of saṅkīrtana which is recommended in Bhāgavatam in Kali-yuga.

“Why did Śaṅkarācārya not comment on Bhāgavatam?” That is explained next. The meaning is this. “In charge of destruction, I, a devotee of Viṣṇu, have followed his order in destroying the real conclusion by explaining the Upaniṣads in a certain way. If I misinterpret his very dear Bhāgavatam however, he will become angry with me. Thus I cannot misinterpret Bhāgavatam. But I will be deprived of the great happiness (prema), understood to be the highest goal. Therefore I will touch upon it a little.” He respected Bhāgavatam by describing in his poems Yaśodā seeing the universal form without commenting on it. Thus Bhāgavatam is respected by all.

### Text 24

*yad eva kila dṛṣṭvā śrī-madhvācārya-caraṇair vaiṣṇavāntarāṇāṁ tac-chiṣyāntara-puṇyāraṇyādi-rītika-vyākhyā-praveśa-śaṅkayā tatra tātparyāntaraṁ likhadbhir vartmopadeśaḥ kṛta iti ca sātvatā varṇayanti \| tasmād yuktam uktaṁ tatraiva prathama-skandhe –*  
*tad idaṁ grāhayāmāsa sutam ātmavatāṁ varam \|*  
*sarva-vedetihāsānāṁ sāraṁ sāraṁ sumuddhṛtam \|\|*  
*dvādaśe –*  
*sarva-vedānta-sāraṁ hi śrī-bhāgavatam iṣyate \|*  
*tad-rasāmṛta-tṛptasya nānyatra syād ratiḥ kvacit \|\|*  
*tathā prathame --*  
*nigama-kalpa-taror galitaṁ phalaṁ*  
*śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam \|*  
*pibata bhāgavataṁ rasam ālayaṁ*  
*muhur aho rasikā bhuvi bhāvukāḥ \|\|*  
*ataeva tatraiva –*  
*yaḥ svānubhāvam akhila-śruti-sāram ekam*  
*adhyātma-dīpam atititīrṣatāṁ tamo 'ndham \|*  
*saṁsāriṇāṁ karuṇayāha purāṇa-guhyaṁ*  
*taṁ vyāsa-sūnum upayāmi guruṁ munīnām \|\|*  
*śrī-bhāgavata-mataṁ tu sarva-matānām adhīśa-rūpam iti sūcakam \| sarva-munīnāṁ sabhā-madhyam adhyāsyopadeṣṭṛtvena teṣāṁ gurutvam api tasya tatra suvyaktam \|\|24\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Madhvācārya, who had accepted accept discipleship in that line, His guru was apparently a follower of Advaita philosophy.17 worried that his disciples would adopt non-devotional explanations of Bhāgavatam such as those of Puṇyāraṇya (disciple of Śaṅkarācārya), showed the path by writing another commentary on Bhāgavatam. So the devotees explain.**

**Therefore, because the Bhāgavatam has such great qualities, what has been said is correct. That is seen in the following:**

***tad idaṁ grāhayām āsa sutam ātmavatāṁ varam \|***  
***sarva-vedetihāsānāṁ sāraṁ sāraṁ samuddhṛtam \|\|***

**Vyāsa had his son Śukadeva, best of the self-controlled, study this scripture, which is the very essence of all the Vedas and histories. SB 1.3.41**

***sarva-vedānta-sāraṁ hi śrī-bhāgavatam iṣyate***  
***tad-rasāmṛta-tṛptasya nānyatra syād ratiḥ kvacit***

**Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam is declared to be the essence of all Vedānta philosophy. One who has felt satisfaction from its sweet** ***rasa*** **will never be attracted to any other literature. SB 12.13.15**

***nigama-kalpa-taror galitaṁ phalaṁ***  
***śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam***  
***pibata bhāgavataṁ rasam ālayaṁ***  
***muhur aho rasikā bhuvi bhāvukāḥ***

**O knowers of** ***rasa*****! O fortunate souls! Constantly drink from the mouth of Śukadeva the Bhāgavatam, the fruit of the tree of the Vedas, which has dropped from the tree to this earth, which is immortal, liquid, which is the essence of sweetness and which includes all types of liberation. SB 1.1.3**

***yaḥ svānubhāvam akhila-śruti-sāram ekam***  
***adhyātma-dīpam atititīrṣatāṁ tamo ’ndham \|***  
***saṁsāriṇāṁ karuṇayāha purāṇa-guhyaṁ***  
***taṁ vyāsa-sūnum upayāmi guruṁ munīnām \|\|***

**I surrender to the son of Vyāsa, the incomparable guru of all the sages, who mercifully spoke the Purāṇa full of hidden meanings, the essence of all the scriptures, the essence of hearing, for all the people of this world, even in the future; who spoke the Bhāgavatam, which is the highest authority, and which is the revealer of ātmā for those desiring to cross dense ignorance with ease. SB 1.2.3**

**The philosophy of Bhāgavatam is the king of all philosophies. Sitting in an assembly of all sages, Śukadeva is the guru, acting as their teacher.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Here it is explained that Madhvācārya also highly respected Bhāgavatam. Understanding that Śaṅkara did not misinterpret the Bhāgavatam but respected it, but worried that Vaiṣṇavas would be led astray by explanations of Śaṅkara’s followers like Puṇyāraṇya that claim that the highest entity in Bhāgavatam is pure consciousness with no qualities, he wrote a commentary favoring Bhagavān to refute those misconceptions and show the path for the Vaiṣṇavas. His name is mentioned in the plural to show respect. He was a previous ācārya. An incarnation of Vāyu, omniscient and powerful, he mastered the fourteen vidyās Four Vedas, 6 aṅgas (śīkṣā, vyākaraṇa, chandas, nirukta, jyotiṣa and kalpa), mīmāṁsā, Purāṇas, nyāya, dharma-śāstras18 in fourteen moments. Padmanābha was his famous disciple.

Because the Bhāgavatam has such great qualities it is correctly stated in the First Canto that it is the essence of all the Vedas and Itihāsas.

*Pibata bhāgavataṁ rasam ālayam* (SB 1.1.3) means “Drink the Bhāgavatam, the rasa which includes liberation.”

The last verse quoted means “I surrender to Śukadeva who spoke the Bhāgavatam (*purāṇa-guhyam)* by his mercy for the people in saṁsāra desiring to cross over deep ignorance.” *Svānubhāvam* means Bhāgavatam is an exceptional authority.

### Text 25

*yataḥ --*  
*tatropajagmur bhuvanaṁ punānā*  
*mahānubhāvā munayaḥ sa-śiṣyāḥ \|*  
*prāyeṇa tīrthābhigamāpadeśaiḥ*  
*svayaṁ hi tīrthāni punanti santaḥ \|\|*  
*atrir vasiṣṭhaś cyavanaḥ śaradvān*  
*ariṣṭanemir bhṛgur aṅgirāś ca \|*  
*parāśaro gādhi-suto 'tha rāma*  
*utathya indrapramadedhmavāhau \|\|*  
*medhātithir devala ārṣṭiṣeṇo*  
*bhāradvājo gautamaḥ pippalādaḥ \|*  
*maitreya aurvaḥ kavaṣaḥ kumbhayonir*  
*dvaipāyano bhagavān nāradaś ca \|\|*  
*anye ca devarṣi-brahmarṣi-varyā*  
*rājarṣi-varyā aruṇādayaś ca \|*  
*nānārṣeya-pravarān sametān*  
*abhyarcya rājā śirasā vavande \|\|*  
*sukhopaviṣṭeṣv atha teṣu bhūyaḥ*  
*kṛta-praṇāmaḥ sva-cikīrṣitaṁ yat \|*  
*vijñāpayām āsa vivikta-cetā*  
*upasthito 'gre 'bhigṛhīta-pāṇiḥ \|\| ity ādy-anantaram –*  
*tataś ca vaḥ pṛcchyam imaṁ vipṛcche*  
*viśrabhya viprā iti kṛtyatāyām \|*  
*sarvātmanā mriyamāṇaiś ca kṛtyaṁ*  
*śuddhaṁ ca tatrāmṛśatābhiyuktāḥ \|\|*  
*iti pṛcchati rājñi –*  
*tatrābhavad bhagavān vyāsa-putro*  
*yadṛcchayā gām aṭamāno 'napekṣaḥ \|*  
*alakṣya-liṅgo nija-lābha-tuṣṭo*  
*vṛtaś ca bālair avadhūta-veṣaḥ \|\|*  
*tataś ca -- pratyutthitās te munayaḥ svāsanebhyaḥ ity-ādy-ante*  
*sa saṁvṛtas tatra mahān mahīyasāṁ*  
*brahmarṣi-rājarṣi-devarṣi-saṅghaiḥ \|*  
*vyarocatālaṁ bhagavān yathendur*  
*graharkṣa-tārā-nikaraiḥ parītaḥ \|\| ity uktam \|\| 25\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is said that Śukadeva is clearly their guru because of the following description:**

***tatropajagmur bhuvanaṁ punānā***  
***mahānubhāvā munayaḥ sa-śiṣyāḥ \|***  
***prāyeṇa tīrthābhigamāpadeśaiḥ***  
***svayaṁ hi tīrthāni punanti santaḥ \|\|***

**Noble-minded sages who purify the world came along with their disciples to see Parīkṣit there on the pretext of visiting a holy place. The devotees, being holy places personified, actually purify the holy places.**

***atrir vasiṣṭhaś cyavanaḥ śaradvān***  
***ariṣṭanemir bhṛgur aṅgirāś ca \|***  
***parāśaro gādhi-suto ’tha rāma***  
***utathya indrapramadaḥ subāhuḥ \|\|***  
***medhātithir devala ārṣṭiṣeṇo***  
***bhāradvājo gautamaḥ pippalādaḥ \|***  
***maitreya aurvaḥ kavaṣaḥ kumbhayonir***  
***dvaipāyano bhagavān nāradaś ca \|\|***

**Atri, Vasiṣṭha, Cyavana, Śaradvān, Ariṣṭameni, Bhṛgu, Aṅgirās, Parāśara, Viśvamitra, Paraśurāma, Utathya, Indrapramada, Subāhu, Medhātithi, Devala, Ārṣṭiṣeṇa, Bhāradvāja, Gautama, Pippalāda, Maitreya, Aurava, Kavaṣa, Agastya, Vyāsa and Nārada came there.**

***anye ca devarṣi-brahmarṣi-varyā***  
***rājarṣi-varyā aruṇādayaś ca \|***  
***nānārṣeya-pravarān sametān***  
***abhyarcya rājā śirasā vavande \|\|***

**Other exalted sages among the devatās, sages of great penance, sages among kings and Aruṇas also came. Welcoming the best of sages, the king bowed his head.**

***sukhopaviṣṭeṣv atha teṣu bhūyaḥ***  
***kṛta-praṇāmaḥ sva-cikīrṣitaṁ yat \|***  
***vijñāpayām āsa vivikta-cetā***  
***upasthito ’gre ’bhigṛhīta-pāṇiḥ \|\|***

**When they were all comfortably seated, standing in front of them, with pure heart and folded hands, he again offered respects and inquired from them concerning what he wanted to do. SB 1.19.8-12**

**Later it is said:**

***tataś ca vaḥ pṛcchyam imaṁ vipṛcche***  
***viśrabhya viprā iti kṛtyatāyām\|***  
***sarvātmanā mriyamāṇaiś ca kṛtyaṁ***  
***śuddhaṁ ca tatrāmṛśatābhiyuktāḥ \|\|***

**O brāhmaṇas! I thus ask about your mercy. That should be determined. Since I have develop faith in your mercy, please consider and tell me in common agreement what is the pure activity, though there are many duties to be performed and many duties for those who are dying. SB 1.19.24**

***tatrābhavad bhagavān vyāsa-putro***  
***yadṛcchayā gām aṭamāno ’napekṣaḥ \|***  
***alakṣya-liṅgo nija-lābha-tuṣṭo***  
***vṛtaś ca bālair avadhūta-veṣaḥ \|\|***

**The great son of Vyāsa, Śukadeva, wandering the earth as he pleased, ignoring everyone, without indications of any āśrama, satisfied with his own attainment, wearing discarded cloth, and surrounded by children, arrived at that time. SB 1.19.25**

***sa saṁvṛtas tatra mahān mahīyasāṁ***  
***brahmarṣi-rājarṣi-devarṣi-saṅghaiḥ \|***  
***vyarocatālaṁ bhagavān yathendur***  
***graharkṣa-tārā-nikaraiḥ parītaḥ \|\|***

**The greatest among the great, Śukadeva, seated there, surrounded by the crowd of sages among brāhmaṇas, sages among kings and sages among devatās** **shone like the moon surrounded by groups of planets, constellations, and stars. SB 1.19.30**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

SB 1.2.3 quoted in the previous anuccheda mentioned that Śukadeva was the guru among sages (*guruṁ munīnām*). This section explains why this is so. He is the guru among sages because (*yataḥ*) it is stated (*ity uktam)* in the quoted verses. Aurva was taken from the womb because of fear of wicked kṣatriyas who destroyed brāhmaṇa families. He was placed in the thigh (*uru*). When born, he turned the kṣatriyas to ashes by his power. This is related in Mahābhārata. *Abhigṛhīta-pāniḥ* (SB 1.19.12) means they had folded hands.

What is to be done by a man in all circumstances as obligatory action (*kṛtyatāyām*) (SB 1.19.24)? This means “What is to be done by persons who are dying? What should be done which entails no violence (*śuddham*)? You should consider this.”

Śukadeva, without desire (*anapekṣaḥ*) wandered the earth (*gam*), satisfied with his attainment (*lābha-tuṣtaḥ*) of Kṛṣṇa, his own Lord (*nija)*, who had purified him. He was surrounded in the assembly *(tatra*) by great sages.

### Text 26

*atra yadyapi tatra śrī-vyāsa-nāradau tasyāpi guru-parama-gurū, tathāpi punas tan-mukha-niḥsṛtaṁ śrī-bhāgavataṁ tayor apy aśrutacaram iva jātam ity evaṁ śrī-śukas tāv apy upadideśa deśyam ity abhiprāyaḥ \| yad uktaṁ śuka-mukhād amṛta-dravya-saṁyutam iti \| tasmād evam api śrī-bhāgavatasyaiva sarvādhikyam \| mātsyādīnāṁ yat purāṇādhikyaṁ śrūyate tat tv āpekṣikam iti \| aho kiṁ bahunā, śrī-kṛṣṇa-pratinidhi-rūpam evedam \| yata uktaṁ prathama-skandhe--*  
*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha \|*  
*kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko 'dhunoditaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*ataeva sarva-guṇa-yuktatvam asyaiva dṛṣṭaṁ dharmaḥ projjhita-kaitavo’tra ity ādinā,*  
*vedāḥ purāṇaṁ kāvyaṁ ca prabhur mitraṁ priyeva ca \|*  
*bodhayantīti hi prāhus trivṛd bhāgavataṁ punaḥ \|\|*  
*iti muktāphale hemādri-kāra-vacanena ca \| tasmān manyantāṁ vā kecit purāṇāntareṣu veda-sāpekṣatvaṁ śrī-bhāgavate tu tathā sambhāvanā svayam eva nirastaity api svayam eva labdhaṁ bhavati \| ataeva parama-śruti-rūpatvaṁ tasya \| yathoktam --*  
*kathaṁ vā pāṇḍaveyasya rājarṣer muninā saha \|*  
*saṁvādaḥ samabhūt tāta yatraiṣā sātvatī śrutiḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*atha yat khalu sarvaṁ purāṇa-jātam āvirbhāvyety ādikaṁ pūrvam uktaṁ tat tu prathama-skandha-gata-śrī-vyāsa-nārada-saṁvādenaiva prameyam \|\|26\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Though Nārada was Śukadeva’s parama-guru and Vyāsa was his guru, the Bhāgavatam, coming from Śukadeva’s mouth, became more attractive even to them. For this reason Śukadeva taught Nārada and Vyāsa as well in that assembly. It was said** ***śuka-mukhād amṛta-dravya-saṁyutam:*** **Bhāgavatam from the mouth of Śukadeva become sweet and liquid. (SB 1.1.3)**

**Thus, since Śukadeva, the speaker, is the guru of all great sages, Bhāgavatam is the best of all. When it is said that Matsya or some other Purāṇa is the best, that is in comparison to other Purāṇas. What more can be said! Bhāgavatam is another form of Kṛṣṇa. It is said in the First Canto:**

***kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha \|***  
***kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ’dhunoditaḥ \|\|***

**Though Kṛṣṇa left Dvārakā, arrived at Prabhāsa, and then disappeared along with his six great qualities, this Purāṇa, another form of the sun, has now risen in Kali-yuga for those who have lost their knowledge. SB 1.3.43**

**It is endowed with all good qualities, being devoid of all cheating dharmas. (SB 1.1.2) Hemādri in Mukhā-phala says:**

***vedāḥ purāṇaṁ kāvyaṁ ca prabhur mitraṁ priyeva ca \|***  
***bodhayantīti hi prāhus trivṛd bhāgavataṁ punaḥ \|\|***

**They say that the Vedas, Purāṇas and poetic works give understanding as the master, friend and lover respectively. However Bhāgavatam gives understanding as all three.**

**Some may think that the Purāṇas’ authority depends on the Vedas. That possibility is rejected for Bhāgavatam, and is stated in Bhāgavatam itself: it is the highest śruti.**

***kathaṁ vā pāṇḍaveyasya rājarṣer muninā saha \|***  
***saṁvādaḥ samabhūt tāta yatraiṣā sātvatī śrutiḥ \|\|***

**How did the conversation of Parīkṣit with Śukadeva arise, through which this Vaiṣṇava śruti appeared? SB 1.4.7**

**It was already explained anuccheda 19 that the Bhāgavatam which was manifested by Vyāsa had previously appeared. This is understood from the conversation between Vyāsa and Nārada in the First Canto.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Bhāgavatam is the greatest of all, since Śukadeva, the speaker, is the guru of all great sages. When it is said other Purāṇas like Matsya are the greatest it means that they are great compared to other Purāṇas. Bhāgavatam is the supreme scripture! What more can be said, since it is another form of Kṛṣṇa, endowed with all qualities like him!

Hemādri says that poems (*kāvyam*) are like lovers (*priyāḥ*). Bhāgavatam is endowed with all three qualities (*trivṛt*--master, friend and lover).

Some may think that the Purāṇas authority depends on statements in the Vedas. Because it reveals the highest goal, Bhāgavatam, the end of the Vedas, is the highest śruti.

In SB 1.4.7, *yatra* means “in which conversation.”  *Sātvatī* means with Vaiṣṇava qualities.

The Bhāgavatam had already previously appeared in a conversation between Brahmā and Nārāyaṇa:

*idaṁ bhagavatā pūrvaṁ brahmaṇe nābhi-paṅkaje*  
*sthitāya bhava-bhītāya kāruṇyāt samprakāśitam*

**The Supreme Lord first revealed the Śrīmad Bhāgavatam to Brahmā. At the time, Brahmā, frightened by material existence, was sitting on the lotus flower that had grown from the Lord's navel. SB 12.13.10**

This version was included in Bhāgavatam, which is the conversation between Vyāsa and Nārada, counted as one of eighteen Purāṇas. The two are counted as one in the enumeration of Purāṇas in Matsya Purāṇa. Such expansion of scripture is also seen concerning the making of Mahābhārata. In the beginning without the stories, Mahābhārata was twenty-four thousand verses. With the stories it was fifty thousand verses.

### Text 27

*tad evaṁ parama-niḥśreyasa-niścayāya śrī-bhāgavatam eva paurvāparyāvirodhena vicāryate \| tatrāsmin sandarbha-ṣaṭkātmake granthe sūtra-sthānīyam avatārikā-vākyaṁ viṣaya-vākyaṁ śrī-bhāgavata-vākyam \| bhāṣya-rūpā tad-vyākhyā tu samprati madhya-deśādau vyāptān advaita-vādino nūnaṁ bhagavan-mahimānam avagāhayituṁ tad-vādena karburita-lipīnāṁ parama-vaiṣṇavānāṁ śrīdhara-svāmi-caraṇānāṁ śuddha-vaiṣṇava-siddhāntānugatā cet tarhi yathāvad eva vilikhyate \| kvacit teṣām evānyatra-dṛṣṭa-vyākhyānusāreṇa draviḍādi-deśa-vikhyāta-parama-bhāgavatānāṁ teṣām eva bāhulyena tatra vaiṣṇavatvena prasiddhatvāt \| śrī-bhāgavata eva, kvacit kvacin mahārāja draviḍeṣu ca bhūriśaḥ (BhP 11.5.39) ity anena prathita-mahimnāṁ sākṣāc chrī-prabhṛtitaḥ pravṛtta-sampradāyānāṁ śrī-vaiṣṇavābhidhānāṁ śrī-rāmānuja-bhagavat-pāda-viracita-śrī-bhāṣyādi-dṛṣṭa-mata-prāmāṇyena mūla-grantha-svārasyena cānyathā ca \| advaita-vyākhyānaṁ tu prasiddhatvān nātivitāyate \|\|27\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**To remove all doubts, in order to determine the highest good, Bhāgavatam will be examined with no contradiction between previous and later statements. In the six Sandarbhas, statements from the Bhāgavatam, which are the subject of discussion, acting as sūtras, will be quoted. The explanations will be like a commentary on the sūtras. In order to immerse the proponents of Advaita who are prominent in the middle states into the great qualities of Bhagavān, the golden manuscripts of Śrīdhara Svāmī, the greatest Vaiṣṇava, are quoted verbatim if they follow the pure Vaiṣṇava conclusions. Sometimes the explanations will follow commentaries seen elsewhere, since the famous devotees of the Southern states are well known for being stalwart Vaiṣṇavas.**

***kṛtādiṣu prajā rājan kalāv icchanti sambhavam***  
***kalau khalu bhaviṣyanti nārāyaṇa-parāyaṇāḥ***  
***kvacit kvacin mahā-rāja draviḍeṣu ca bhūriśaḥ***

**O King! The inhabitants of Satya-yuga and other ages eagerly desire to take birth in this age of Kali, since in this age there will be many devotees of the Supreme Lord, Nārāyaṇa. These devotees will appear in various places but will be especially numerous in South India and places like Bengal. SB 11.5.38**

**This indicates the glorious Śrī-vaiṣṇava sampradāya started by Lakṣmī herself. The ideas seen in Śrī-bhāṣya written by Rāmānuja are in agreement with the Bhāgavatam text. In other cases, I have written the commentary according to other views. The explanations of Advaita, being well known, are not discussed in detail.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

“The Vedas are our proof. How can you accept Purāṇas as the proof?” One should not be misled in this way. Because of this (tad evam), Bhāgavatam will be discussed systematically in order to determine the highest goal. Or “Since the Purāṇas are accepted as the Vedas by śruti itself with *are ‘sya mahato bhūtasya* (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.4.1), Bhāgavatam will be examined to determine the highest good.” Since Bhāgavatam was determined to be the best among the Purāṇas, just as Vedānta is the best of the Vedas, Bhāgavatam is the proof. Nothing illogical has been stated.

The method of the Sandarbhas is therefore similar to a commentary on Brahma-sūtras. There is a statement of the subject (*viṣaya*), that which should be discussed, which is like the sūtra. The explanation of the Bhāgavatam verse is like a commentary on a sūtra.

Śrīdhara Svāmī is considered a Vaiṣṇava because he states in his commentaries that the Lord’s vibhūtis, abodes, qualities and forms, as well as the bodies of his associates are eternal and that bhakti to Bhagavān is the most attractive type of liberation. Sometimes he writes māyāvāda ideas, but it should be understood that its purpose is to lure the māyāvādīs to bhakti, by offering bait on a hook. Similarly pure Vaiṣṇava philosophy accepts the non-contrary parts of Sāṅkhya. In that way, the parts of Śrīdhara Svāmī which follow pure Vaiṣṇava ideas are quoted.

Sometimes I have written the explanation or commentary according to Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary, and sometimes I have explained the verses on the authority of opinions expressed in Śrī-bhāṣya of Rāmānujācārya, which agree with the Bhāgavata conclusions. The explanations are not something imaginary, that I have produced on my own. The commentary is based upon other’s explanations.

“One should explain Advaita philosophy as the pūrva-pakṣa (opponent’s position).” Since their position is well known it is not quoted.

### Text 28

*atra ca sva-darśitārtha-viśeṣa-prāmāṇyāyaiva, na tu śrīmad-bhāgavata-vākya-prāmāṇyāya, pramāṇāni śruti-purāṇādi-vacanāni yathā-dṛṣṭam evodāharaṇīyāni \| kvacit svayam adṛṣṭākarāṇi ca tattva-vāda-gurūṇām anādhunikānāṁ pracura-pracārita-vaiṣṇava-mata-viśeṣāṇāṁ dakṣiṇādi-deśa-vikhyāta-śiṣyopaśiṣyībhūta-vijayadhvaja-vyāsatīrthādi-veda-vedārtha-vid-varāṇāṁ śrī-madhvācārya-caraṇānāṁ bhāgavata-tātparya-bhārata-tātparya-brahma-sūtra-bhāṣyādibhyaḥ saṅgṛhītāni \| taiś caivam uktaṁ bhārata-tātparye –*  
*śāstrāntarāṇi saṁjānan vedāntasya prasādataḥ \|*  
*deśe deśe tathā granthān dṛṣṭvā caiva pṛthag-vidhān \|\|*  
*yathā sa bhagavān vyāsaḥ sākṣān nārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ \|*  
*jagāda bhāratādyeṣu tathā vakṣye tad-īkṣayā \|\| iti \|*  
*tatra tad-uddhṛtā śrutiś caturveda-śikhādyā, purāṇaṁ ca gāruḍādīnāṁ samprati sarvatrāpracarad-rūpam aṁśādikam \| saṁhitā ca mahā-saṁhitādikā tantraṁ ca tantra-bhāgavatādikaṁ brahma-tarkādikam iti jñeyam \|\|28\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In order to prove the particular meaning shown by me, not in order to prove the statement of Bhāgavatam, statements from śruti and Purāṇas are quoted in the Sandarbhas. Sometimes statements from scriptures I have not personally seen are presented from Madhvācārya’s commentaries on Brahma-sūtra, Bhāgavatam and Mahābhārata. Along with others in his disciplic line like Vyāsatirtha and Vijayadhvaja, he was famous in the South as guru of tattva-vāda, holding excellent Vaiṣṇava views well known since ancient times, and was most learned in the Vedas and their meaning. In his commentary on Mahābhārata he says:**

***śāstrāntarāṇi saṁjānan vedāntasya prasādataḥ \|***  
***deśe deśe tathā granthān dṛṣṭvā caiva pṛthag-vidhān \|\|***  
***yathā sa bhagavān vyāsaḥ sākṣān nārāyaṇaḥ prabhuḥ \|***  
***jagāda bhāratādyeṣu tathā vakṣye tad-īkṣayā \|\|***

**Understanding other scriptures by the mercy of the Lord, who is the goal of the Vedas, seeing scriptures of various types in various provinces, I will speak according to the Lord’s view, just as Vyāsa, who is directly Lord Nārāyaṇa, spoke in the Mahābhārata.**
