**The śrutis that he quotes, such as Caturveda-śikhā and some Purāṇas like Garuḍa, are now available in partial form or not available at all. Other scriptures like Mahā-saṁhitā, Tantra-bhāgavata and Brahma-tarka are also in this category.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In the Sandarbhas, the statements I have taken from śruti and Purāṇas are to prove the particular interpretation that I have shown in a Bhāgavatam statement, not to prove that the Bhāgavatam statements are true, since Bhāgavatam is its own proof (being equivalent of śruti). Examining those scriptures, the verses are taken intact and quoted. Some statements however I have not seen directly. These statements have been seen by Madhvācārya in certain scriptures and are quoted. Thus, sometimes, to prove a particular meaning in my explanation, statements from śruti and Purāṇa have been gathered from Madhvācārya’s Bhāgavat-tātparya and other works. It is implied that the author of these works (Madhvācārya) is truthful.

*kaumāra-brahmacaryavān naiṣṭhiko yaḥ satya-taponidhiḥ svapne’py anṛtaṁ noce ca*

**The person who is a lifelong celibate, an ocean of sense control and truth, does not speak falsely even in dreams.**

The works under discussion are described. They belong to the tattva-vāda school. Tattva-vāda means “a philosophy which advocates that all things of substance (the Lord, jīva and matter) are real.” In arguments with Śaṅkara, Vyāsa approved of Madhva’s ideas, well known since ancient times, and rejected those of Śaṅkara. This is a traditional story. It is said that Śaṅkara argued with Vyāsa concerning the meaning of Brahma-sūtras but Madhva became Vyāsa’s disciple and learned from him.19

The particular teachings he preached are that devotee brāhmaṇas are qualified for liberation, devatās are the chief devotees, Brahmā attains sayujya-mukti, and Lakṣmī is a jīva. His followers were famous in the southern districts and other places. This means that Mādhavendra Puri and several others became his followers.

In his introduction to his book, Madhva indicates that he did not see all the scriptures in their entirety but understood them by the mercy of the Lord. He gathered scriptures when he travelled about defeating others.

## Prameya prakaraṇa

### Text 29

*atha namaskurvann eva tathābhūtasya śrīmad-bhāgavatasya tātparya-tad-vaktur hṛdaya-niṣṭhā-paryālocanayā saṅkṣepatas tāvan nirdhārayati –*  
*sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastāny abhāvo’*  
*py ajita-rucir alīlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam \|*  
*vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ*  
*tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato’smi \|\|*  
*ṭīkā ca śrīdhara-svāmi-viracitā – śrī-guruṁ namaskaroti \| sva-sukhenaiva nibhṛtaṁ pūrṇaṁ ceto yasya saḥ \| tenaiva vyudasto’nyasmin bhāvo bhāvanā yasya tathābhūto’py ajitasya rucirābhir līlābhir ākṛṣṭaḥ sāraḥ sva-sukha-gataṁ dhairyaṁ yasya saḥ \| tattva-dīpaṁ paramārtha-prakāśakaṁ śrī-bhāgavataṁ yo vyatanuta taṁ nato’smi ity eṣā \| evam eva dvitīye tad-vākyam eva prāyeṇa munayo rājan ity ādi-padya-trayam anusandheyam \| atrākhila-vṛjinaṁ tādṛśa-bhāvasya pratkūlam udāsīnaṁ ca jñeyam \| tad evam iha sambandhi-tattvaṁ brahmānandād api prakṛṣṭo rucira-līlāvaśiṣṭaḥ śrīmān ajita eva \| sa ca pūrṇatvena mukhyatayā śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṁjña eveti śrī-bādarāyaṇa-samādhau vyaktībhaviṣyati \| tathā prayojanākhyaḥ puruṣārthaś ca tādṛśa-tad-āsakti-janakaṁ tal-līlāśravaṇādi-lakṣaṇaṁ tad-bhajanam evety āyātam \| atra vyāsa-sūnum iti brahma-vaivartānusāreṇa śrī-kṛṣṇa-varāj janmata eva māyayā tasyāspṛṣṭatvaṁ sūcitam \|\| 12.12 \|\| śrī-sūtaḥ śrī-śaunakam \|\|29\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The meaning of Bhāgavatam as described above is presented in brief through seeing the devotion in the heart of Śukadeva, the speaker of Bhāgavatam, as Sūta offers respects.**

***sva-sukha-nibhṛta-cetās tad-vyudastānya-bhāvo***  
***'py ajita-rucira-līlākṛṣṭa-sāras tadīyam***  
***vyatanuta kṛpayā yas tattva-dīpaṁ purāṇaṁ***  
***tam akhila-vṛjina-ghnaṁ vyāsa-sūnuṁ nato 'smi***

**Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto the son of Vyāsadeva, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, who, though fixed in his own bliss with no distractions, became attracted (abhidheya) to the pleasing pastimes of Kṛṣṇa (sambandha) and mercifully spread the Bhāgavatam which describes the goal (prayojana) and destroys obstacles to attraction. SB 12.12.69**

**Śrīdhara Svāmī comments, “Sūta offers respects to his guru whose heart was full** ***(nibhṛtam*****) of its own bliss (*****sva-sukham),*** **by which all other thoughts were destroyed. But in spite of his firmness in that happiness** ***(sāraḥ*****) he became attracted to the pastimes of the Lord. I offer respects to he who spread the Bhāgavatam which reveals the puruṣārtha (*****tattva-dīpam*****).”**

**One should also see these three verses from the Second Canto:**

***prāyeṇa munayo rājan nivṛttā vidhi-ṣedhataḥ \|***  
***nairguṇya-sthā ramante sma guṇānukathane hareḥ \|\|***

**O King! Generally the sages who are beyond rules and prohibitions, who are situated beyond the guṇas, take pleasure in glorifying the qualities of the Lord.**

***idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam \|***  
***adhītavān dvāparādau pitur dvaipāyanād aham \|\|***

**This scripture concerning the Supreme Lord is a Purāṇa equal to the Lord himself. I learned this scripture from my father Vyāsa at the beginning of the last part of Dvāpara-yuga.**

***pariniṣṭhito ’pi nairguṇya uttama-śloka-līlayā \|***  
***gṛhīta-cetā rājarṣe ākhyānaṁ yad adhītavān \|\|***

**Though I was fixed in Brahman and beyond the guṇas, my mind became attracted to the pastimes of the Lord. I thus learned about his pastimes. SB 2.1.7-9**

***Akhila-vṛjina-ghnam*** **means Bhāgavatam destroys all things indifferent to or opposed to attraction to Kṛṣṇa. Sambandhi-tattvam (the principal entity in to be described in the work) is indicated:** ***ajita*** **(unsurpassed in SB 12.12.69) refers to the person who is endowed with sweet pastimes more attractive than the bliss of Brahman. Matter and Brahman are subservient to Kṛṣṇa.20 It means Kṛṣṇa primarily, since he is full of all qualities. This will be revealed in Vyāsa’s samādhi.**

**The puruṣārtha called prayojana (*****tattva-dīpam*****) is thus the happiness of prema which produces attraction for Kṛṣṇa. The abhidheya is worship of Kṛṣṇa, in the form of hearing his pastimes, which generates prema. It is said here that Śukadeva was the son of Vyāsa. This suggests that according to Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa, by the blessing of Kṛṣṇa, he would not be touched by māyā from his very birth (and thus he came out of the womb). Sūta speaks to Śaunaka.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

At the beginning of the prameya-prakaraṇa (what is proved by the *pramāṇa*) the author begins with this verse offering respects to Śukadeva. Numbers are placed at the end of the Bhāgavatam verses, the subject matter, which act as sūtras, to show the order of the statements. This will conform to Krama Sandarbha.

Sūta speaks the verse in the twelfth chapter of the Twelfth Canto.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The author began the work (anuccheda 8) with a verse which indicated Kṛṣṇa as the *sambandhi* (the subject of Bhāgavatam), bhakti as the abhidheya and prema as the prayojana.

*yasya brahmeti saṁjñāṁ kvacid api nigame yāti cin-mātra-sattāpy*  
*aṁśo yasyāṁśakaiḥ svair vibhavati vaśayann eva māyāṁ pumāṁś ca \|*  
*ekaṁ yasyaiva rūpaṁ vilasati parama-vyomni nārāyaṇākhyaṁ*  
*sa śrī-kṛṣṇo vidhattāṁ svayam iha bhagavān prema tat-pāda-bhājām \|\|*

**May Śrī-kṛṣṇa, Bhagavān Svayam, whose existence as pure consciousness is defined in some scriptures as Brahman, whose aṁśa, the puruṣāvatāra, controlling māyā, shines with his expansions, and one of whose forms called Nārāyaṇa plays in Vaikuṇṭha, give prema to those who worship his feet! 21**

To begin the work the author now quotes a verse from Bhāgavatam describing Śukadeva, with indications of the same elements. *Atha* indicates auspiciousness. The verse indicates that the heart of the speaker of the scripture was fixed only in the entity propounded by the scripture and in nothing else.

*Purāṇa* refers to the Purāṇa which describes the Lord (*ajita*). The commentary of Śrīdhara Svāmī explains that *sva-sukham* means that Śukadeva was full of his own bliss. This bliss was greater than the bliss of the jīva, just as honey is sweeter than unrefined sugar. He had experienced the great bliss of self-manifesting Brahman, a form of bliss-- but without manifestation of the Lord’s abode, qualities, powers and pastimes. He became attracted to the Lord’s pastimes with his associates, with blissful forms, attractive (*rucira*) because of combinations of sweetness mixed with the highest powers, like some sweet potion. Bhāgavatam destroys all obstacles – hostility to or rejection of the subject. Sūta speaks to Śaunaka. This last statement describes the context of the verse quoted. The speaker will be stated after each Bhāgavatam verse quoted in the Sandarbha in order to understand the context.

### Text 30

*tādṛśam eva tātparyaṁ karisyamāṇa-tad-grantha-pratipādya-tattva-nirṇaya-kṛte tat-pravaktṛ-śrī-bādarāyaṇa-kṛte samādhāv api saṅkṣepata eva nirdhārayati –*  
*bhakti-yogena manasi samyak praṇihite 'male \|*  
*apaśyat puruṣaṁ pūrṇaṁ māyāṁ ca tad-apāśrayam \|\|*  
*yayā sammohito jīva ātmānaṁ tri-guṇātmakam \|*  
*paro 'pi manute 'narthaṁ tat-kṛtaṁ cābhipadyate \|\|*  
*anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje \|*  
*lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām \|\|*  
*yasyāṁ vai śrūyamāṇāyāṁ kṛṣṇe parama-pūruṣe \|*  
*bhaktir utpadyate puṁsaḥ śoka-moha-bhayāpahā \|\|*  
*sa saṁhitāṁ bhāgavatīṁ kṛtvānukramya cātma-jam \|*  
*śukam adhyāpayām āsa nivṛtti-nirataṁ muniḥ \|\|*  
*tatra --*  
*sa vai nivṛtti-nirataḥ sarvatropekṣako muniḥ \|*  
*kasya vā bṛhatīm etām ātmārāmaḥ samabhyasat \|\|*  
*iti śaunaka-praśnānantaraṁ ca –*  
*ātmārāmāś ca munayo nirgranthā apy urukrame \|*  
*kurvanty ahaitukīṁ bhaktim ittham-bhūta-guṇo hariḥ \|\|*  
*harer guṇākṣipta-matir bhagavān bādarāyaṇiḥ \|*  
*adhyagān mahad ākhyānaṁ nityaṁ viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ \|\|*  
*bhakti-yogena premṇā \|*  
*astv evam aṅga bhagavān bhajatāṁ mukundo*  
*muktiṁ dadāti karhicit sma na bhakti-yogam \|\|*  
*ity atra prasiddheḥ \| praṇihite samāhite samādhinānusmara tad-viceṣṭitam iti taṁ prati śrī-nāradopadeśāt \| pūrṇadasya mukta-pragrahayā vṛttyā –*  
*bhagavān iti śabdo’yaṁ tathā puruṣa ity api \|*  
*vartate nirupādhiś ca vāsudeve’khilātmani \|\|*  
*iti pādmottara-khaṇḍa-vacanāvaṣṭambhena, tathā –*  
*kāma-kāmo yajet somam akāmaḥ puruṣaṁ param \|\|*  
*akāmaḥ sarva-kāmo vā mokṣa-kāma udāra-dhīḥ \|*  
*tīvreṇa bhakti-yogena yajeta puruṣaṁ param \|\|*  
*ity asya vākya-dvayasya pūrva-vākye puruṣaṁ paramātmānaṁ prakṛty-ekopādhim uttara-vākye puruṣaṁ pūrṇaṁ nirupādhim iti ṭīkānusāreṇa ca pūrṇaḥ puruṣo’tra svayaṁ bhagavān ucyate \|\|30\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The same meaning of Bhāgavatam is indicated in brief in the samādhi of the speaker Vyāsa, which defined the tattva to be explained in the future scripture.**

***bhakti-yogena manasi samyak praṇihite ’male \|***  
***apaśyat puruṣaṁ pūrṇaṁ māyāṁ ca tad-apāśrayām \|\|***

**In that mind which was fixed because it was pure from the performance of bhakti (prema), he saw Lord Kṛṣṇa, complete with all his energies and spiritual expansions, and also saw māyā who took shelter behind the Lord.**

**Bhakti means prema as in the following:**

***astv evam aṅga bhagavān bhajatāṁ mukundo***  
***muktiṁ dadāti karhicit sma na bhakti-yogam***

**The Lord gives liberation but not prema (bhakti-yogam) to those who worship him. SB 5.6.18**

***Praṇihite*** **means “fixed.” Nārada instructed** ***samādhinānusmara tad-viceṣṭitam:*** **remember with attention the pastimes of the Lord. (SB 1.5.13) The words** ***pūṛṇam puruṣam*** **(complete person), according to** ***mukta-pragraha-vṛtti*** **(tending to the highest meaning), means Kṛṣṇa, for it is said:**

***bhagavān iti śabdo’yaṁ tathā puruṣa ity api \|***  
***vartate nirupādhiś ca vāsudeve’khilātmani \|\|***

**The words Bhagavān and puruṣa, when indicating someone devoid of all upādhis, mean Vāsudeva, the soul of all beings. Padma Purāṇa**

***kāma-kāmo yajet somam akāmaḥ puruṣaṁ param \|\|***

**The person desiring sense gratification should worship Soma. The person desiring to destroy all material desires should worship the Supreme Lord.**

***akāmaḥ sarva-kāmo vā mokṣa-kāma udāra-dhīḥ \|***  
***tīvreṇa bhakti-yogena yajeta puruṣaṁ param \|\|***

**The person desiring destruction of all desires, the person with all desires, and even the person with an intense desire for liberation, if he has good intelligence, will worship the Supreme Lord with pure bhakti. SB 2.3.9-10**

**In verse 9** ***puruṣam*** **refers to Paramātmā, who has the one upādhi of prakṛti. In verse 10** ***puruṣam*** **means Svayam Bhagavān, who is free of upādhis, being complete. This is according to Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary. Thus the meaning is that Vyāsa saw Svayam Bhagavān Kṛṣṇa (*****puruṣam pūrṇam*****).**

***yayā sammohito jīva ātmānaṁ tri-guṇātmakam \|***  
***paro ’pi manute ’narthaṁ tat-kṛtaṁ cābhipadyate \|\|***

**Bewildered by that māyā, the jīva, though separate from the three guṇas, considers himself made of the three guṇas and takes on material existence created by the guṇas*****.***

***anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje \|***  
***lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām \|\|***

**And Vyāsa saw bhakti-yoga to the Lord which effectively destroys jīva’s saṁsāra. Learned Vyāsa then wrote the Bhāgavatam for ignorant people.**

***yasyāṁ vai śrūyamāṇāyāṁ kṛṣṇe parama-pūruṣe \|***  
***bhaktir utpadyate puṁsaḥ śoka-moha-bhayāpahā \|\|***

**By hearing Bhāgavatam*****,*** **bhakti for the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa appears. Hearing it also destroys lamentation, illusion and fear in the human being.**

***sa saṁhitāṁ bhāgavatīṁ kṛtvānukramya cātma-jam \|***  
***śukam adhyāpayām āsa nivṛtti-nirataṁ muniḥ \|\|***

**Having already made the Bhāgavatam, the sage Vyāsa revised it at this time and taught it to his son Śukadeva who had realized Brahman. SB 1.7.4-8**

**Śaunaka asks:**

***sa vai nivṛtti-nirataḥ sarvatropekṣako muniḥ \|***  
***kasya vā bṛhatīm etām ātmārāmaḥ samabhyasat \|\|***

**Śukadeva was fixed in the Brahman, indifferent to everything, and enjoying in the self. Why did he study this elaborate work? SB 1.7.9**

**Sūta replies:**

***ātmārāmāś ca munayo nirgranthā apy urukrame \|***  
***kurvanty ahaitukīṁ bhaktim ittham-bhūta-guṇo hariḥ \|\|***

**Some sages who are ātmārāmas, beyond the scriptures, false ego and rules, also practice unmotivated, pure bhakti to the master of pure bhakti, Kṛṣṇa, since he possesses qualities attractive to even them.**

***harer guṇākṣipta-matir bhagavān bādarāyaṇiḥ \|***  
***adhyagān mahad ākhyānaṁ nityaṁ viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ \|\|***

**Śukadeva, the son of Vyāsa, whose mind became distracted from trance by the qualities of Kṛṣṇa, and who became attracted to the Vaiṣṇavas, studied this great Bhāgavatam. SB 1.7.10-11**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Sūta explains to Śaunaka. The verses are followed by short explanations. This procedure is followed throughout the work.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Having quoted a verse to show the devotion of Śukadeva, now verses are quoted to show the devotion of Vyāsa. *Nivṛtti-niratam* means Śukadeva was devoid of desire for material objects since he was absorbed in the bliss of Brahman. What is the result of studying this work *(kasya*)? *Adhyagāt* means “he studied.” *Mukta-pragrahaya* indicates that, just as a horse freed from his reins runs to the limit of his strength, the word *pūrṇa* takes on its highest limit of “fullness” as Svayam Bhagavān.

### Text 31

*pūrvam iti pāṭhe pūrvam evāham ihāsam, iti tat-puruṣasya puruṣatvam iti śrauta-nirvacana-viśeṣa-puraskāreṇa ca sa evocyate \| tam apaśyat śrī-veda-vyāsa iti svarūpa-śaktim antam evety etat svayam eva labdham pūrṇaṁ candram apaśyad ity ukte kāntim antam apaśyad iti labhyate \| ataeva –*  
*tvam ādyaḥ puruṣaḥ sākṣād*  
*īśvaraḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ*  
*māyāṁ vyudasya cic-chaktyā*  
*kaivalye sthita ātmani \|\| ity uktam \|*  
*ataeva māyāṁ ca tad-apāśrayam ity anena tasmin apa apakṛṣṭa āśrayo yasyāḥ \| nilīya sthitatvād iti māyāyā na tat-svarūpa-bhūtatvam ity api labhyate \| vakṣyate ca – māyā paraity abhimukhe ca vilajjamānā iti \| svarūpa-śaktir iyam atraiva vyaktībhaviṣyati anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje ity anena ātmārāmāś ca ity anena ca \| pūrvatra hi bhakti-yoga-prabhāvaḥ khalv asau māyābhibhāvakatayā svarūpa-śakti-vṛttitvenaiva gamyate \| paratra ca te guṇā brahmānandasyāpy uparicaratayā svarūpa-śakteḥ parama-vṛttitām evārhantīti \| māyādhiṣṭhātṛ-puruṣas tu tad-aṁśatvena, brahma ca tadīya-nirviśeṣāvirbhāvatvena, tad-antarbhāva-vivakṣayā pṛthaṅ nokte iti jñeyam \| ato’tra pūrvavad eva sambandhi-tattvaṁ nirdhāritam \|\|31\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Instead of** ***puruṣam pūrṇam*** **sometimes** ***puruṣaṁ pūrvam*** **is seen. This refers to śruti which says** ***pūrvam evāham ihāsam iti tat-puruṣasya puruṣatvam*****: it is said “In the beginning I alone existed in this world.” Because the Lord was the only person (*****puruṣasya*****) existing previously (*****pūrvam*****), he is called the** ***puruṣa.*** **(*****Taittirīya Āraṇyaka*** **1.23.1) This indicates Bhagavān.**

**Seeing the** ***puruṣam pūrṇam*** **means that Vyāsa saw the Lord endowed with his svarūpa-śakti, just as “He saw the full moon” means “He saw the moon with its bright light.” Thus it said:**

***tvam ādyaḥ puruṣaḥ sākṣād īśvaraḥ prakṛteḥ paraḥ \|***  
***māyāṁ vyudasya cic-chaktyā kaivalye sthita ātmani \|\|***

**You are the original Lord, the controller, beyond material energy. You are situated in your spiritual form, being one with your spiritual energy (*****cic-chaktyā*****), and separate from the material energy. SB 1.7.23**

**Therefore, since māyā is inferior to the svarūpa-śakti, it is said that māyā took shelter of the Lord as an inferior (*****māyām ca tad-apāśrayām*****). She was standing concealed. This indicates that māyā does not arise from his svarūpa.** ***Māyā paraity abhimukhe ca vilajjamānā: māyā,*** **being ashamed*****,*** **cannot stand before the Lord. (SB 2.7.47)**

**The svarūpa-śakti (as bhakti) will be explained with** ***anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje:*** **Vyāsa saw bhakti-yoga to the Lord which effectively destroys jīva’s saṁsāra. (SB 1.7.6) Also see** ***ātmārāmāś ca munayo nirgranthā apy urukrame kurvanty ahaitukīṁ bhaktim:*** **persons free of material energy perform unmotivated bhakti. (SB 1.7.10) The power of bhakti-yoga mentioned in the first text is understood to be the function of the svarūpa-śakti since it is the defeater of māyā. In the second verse (SB 1.7.10), bhakti’s qualities act as the highest function of the svarūpa-śakti (prema) to minimize the bliss of Brahman.**

**The puruṣa who oversees māyā is an aṁśa of Bhagavān. Brahman is not mentioned separately since, as the Lord’s manifestation without qualities, it is included within Bhagavān. As previously (anuccheda 29) sambandhi-tattva is indicated by this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The alternate version with *pūrvam* instead of *pūrṇam* is explained. Since the Lord alone as a person *(puruṣasya)* existed previously he is called *puruṣa (puruṣasya puruṣattvam*). Thus Bhagavān is indicated by *pūrvam puruṣam*. Proof is given that the Lord is endowed with his svarūpa-śakti. There are also śrutis that state this. *Parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca*: the Lord has various śaktis inherent in him such as jñāna-śakti, bala-śakti and kriyā-śakti. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8) There is also the following from smṛti:

*hlādinī sandhinī saṁvit tvayy ekā sarva-saṁsthitau \|*  
*hlāda-tāpa-karī miśra tvayi no guṇa-varjite \|\|*

**In you who are the shelter of all spiritual qualities exist the energies of hlādinī, sandhinī and saṁvit. In you who are devoid of material qualities, the mixture of happiness and distress found in the material world does not exist. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.12.68**

*Ity uktam* after SB 1.7.23 means the verse was spoken by Arjuna. Therefore, since māyā is inferior to the svarūpa-śakti, it is said that māyā took shelter of the Lord as an inferior (*māyām ca tad-apāśrayām*). It is understood from the text that the cit-śakti arises from the Lord’s svarūpa. The svarūpa-śakti is like a queen and māyā-śakti is like a servant at the outer door. There is a great difference between them.

The first verse describes bhakti as the essence of the svarūpa-śakti among all the Lord’s qualities. The bliss of Brahman is devoid of manifestation of abode and qualities of the Lord. “Why did Vyāsa not see the forms of Paramātmā and Brahman?” Paramātmā is Bhagavān’s aṁśa. Brahman is included in him. Thus they are not mentioned separately.

### Text 32

*atha prāk-pratipāditasyaivābhedhyeasya prayojanasya ca sthāpakaṁ jīvasya svarūpata eva parameśvarād vailakṣaṇyam apaśyad ity āha— yayā māyayā sammohito jīvaḥ svayaṁ cid-rūpatvena triguṇātmakāj jaḍāt paro’py ātmānaṁ triguṇātmakaṁ jaḍaṁ dehādi-saṅghātaṁ manute \| tan-manana-kṛtam anarthaṁ saṁsāra-vyasanaṁ cābhipadyate \| tad evaṁ jīvasya cid-rūpatve’pi yayā sammohita iti manute iti ca svarūpa-bhūta-jñāna-śālitvaṁ vyanakti prakāśaika-rūpasya tejasaḥ svapara-prakāśana-śaktivat \| ajñānenāvṛtaṁ jñānaṁ tena muhyanti jantavaḥ iti śrī-gītābhyaḥ \| tad evaṁ upādher eva jīvatvaṁ tan-nāśasyaiva mokṣatvam iti matāntaraṁ parihṛtavān \| atra yayā sammohitaḥ ity anena tasyā eva tatra kartṛtvaṁ bhagavatas tatrodāsīnatvaṁ matam \| vakṣyate ca --*  
*vilajjamānayā yasya sthātum īkṣā-pathe’muyā*  
*vimohitā vikatthante mamāham iti durdhiyaḥ \|\| iti \|*  
*atra vilajjamānayā ity anenedam āyāti tasyā jīva-sammhohanaṁ karma śrī-bhagavate na rocata iti yadyapi sā svayaṁ jānāti, tathāpi bhayaṁ dvitīyābhiniveśataḥ syād īśād apetasya iti diśā jīvānām anādi-bhagavad-ajñāna-maya-vaimukhyam asahamānā svarūpāvaraṇam asvarūpāveśaṁ ca karoti \|\|32\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa saw the essential difference between the Lord and the jīva. This difference establishes the abhidheya and prayojana previously mentioned.** ***Yayā māyayā sammohito jīvaḥ:*** **jīva was bewildered by that māyā. Though the jīva is superior to matter made of the three guṇas, since he is conscious in nature, he thinks himself to be body made of the three guṇas. The obstacle created by that conception leads to the suffering of saṁsāra. Though actually a conscious entity, he identifies with the body because of māyā’s influence. This shows that the jīva has svarūpa-bhūta-jñāna, knowledge as his svarūpa (knowledge is inherent in his nature as jīva), just as fire by its nature of being a flame (svarūpa) reveals itself and other things.**

**Gītā says:**

***nādatte kasyacit pāpaṁ na caiva sukṛtaṁ vibhuḥ \|***  
***ajñānenāvṛtaṁ jñānaṁ tena muhyanti jantavaḥ \|\|***

**The perfect Lord is not responsible for the sinful or pious acts of the jīvas. But since their knowledge is covered by their hostility to the Lord the living entities are bewildered by that ignorance and blame the Lord. BG 5.15**

**Therefore the idea that the jīva is Brahman in a state of upādhis and that by destroying the upādhis there is liberation in Brahman is refuted. Māyā is the agent of bewildering the jīva. The Lord is neutral in this matter. It is said:**

***vilajjamānayā yasya sthātum īkṣā-pathe ’muyā \|***  
***vimohitā vikatthante mamāham iti durdhiyaḥ \|\|***

**The ignorant jīvas, bewildered by māyā who is ashamed to stand in sight of the Lord, boast about “I” and “mine.” SB 2.5.13**

**Since it is said that māyā is ashamed to stand before the Lord, her action of bewildering the jīva is not pleasing to the Lord. Though she knows this, not tolerating the jīvas’ beginningless aversion to the Lord in the form of ignorance of the Lord, she covers jīva’s svarūpa and makes him think he is something else.**

***bhayaṁ dvitīyābhiniveśataḥ syād***  
***īśād apetasya viparyayo 'smṛtiḥ***  
***tan-māyayāto budha ābhajet taṁ***  
***bhaktyaikayeśaṁ guru-devate ātmā***

**For the jīva averse to the Lord, there will be saṁsāra consisting of identity with body and lack of identity with the soul, because of his absorption in the material coverings on the soul, arising from the Lord’s māyā. Therefore, the intelligent person, taking guru as his Lord and very self, should fully worship the Lord with pure bhakti. SB 11.2.37**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Vyāsa saw the factual difference between the jīva and the Lord: because of that difference the jīva worships the Lord, attains prema for him and becomes free of māyā. That is explained in this section. He saw the innate difference: eternal qualities of each, such as the servant and the served, the small particle and the complete whole (*vibhu*).

“The śrutis say the jīva is pure consciousness. *Yo vijñāne tiṣṭhan*: the jīva is situated in knowledge. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.7.22) *Vijñānaṁ yajñaṁ tanute*: the jīva, knowledge, performs sacrifice. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.5.1)) Because the jīva is knowledge itself or pure consciousness, the jīva does not have knowledge as a quality (*dharma-bhūta-jñāna*) eternally, by which the jīva can be bewildered and develop material identity. Jīva is not a knower of objects but is merely knowledge itself.22 Because sattva gives rise to a type of knowledge (BG 14.17), that shadow of consciousness which is present in sattva is the knowledge by which the bewilderment and material identity seen by Vyāsa develops.” The jīva in pure state is Brahman, and when covered with material sattva becomes the individual jīva. 23

The answer is given with “Though actually a conscious entity, he identifies with the body because of māyā’s influence.” Because Brahman cannot become a shadow of consciousness, it is not proper to imagine such a scenario.

“What is this svarūpa-bhūta-jñāna?” Fire by its nature as a flame reveals itself and other things. This is explained in the following:

*ubhaya-vyapadeśāt tv ahi-kuṇḍalavat*

**The Lord is both knowledge itself and qualified with knowledge (knowing things), like a snake and its coils because scriptures say the Lord is both. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.28**

I will discuss this in detail in Paramātmā Sandarbha. Śaṅkara has the idea that liberation for the jīva is destruction of the antaḥkaraṇa. This is refuted since it is said in the text that the jīva is separate from the guṇas.

*yayā sammohito jīva ātmānaṁ tri-guṇātmakam \|*  
*paro ’pi manute ’narthaṁ tat-kṛtaṁ cābhipadyate \|\|*

**Bewildered by that māyā, the jīva, though separate from the three guṇas, considers himself made of the three guṇas and takes on material existence created by the guṇas. SB 1.7.5**

Referring to SB 2.5.13, it is said that māyā is ashamed because she bewilders the jīvas. Māyā is the agent. Brahmā speaks that verse. *Amuyā* in the same verse means “by māyā.” Māyā cannot tolerate the jīva’s aversion to the Lord. She does her job as a servant in making the jīva suffer for being averse to him. Māyā covers the jīva who is overcome by his aversion to the Lord. It is like a lamp covered by a pot being covered by darkness.

### Text 33

*śrī-bhagavāṁś cānādita eva bhaktāyāṁ prapañcādhikāriṇyāṁ tasyāṁ dākṣiṇyaṁ laṅghituṁ na śaknoti \| tathā tad-bhayenāpi jīvānāṁ sva-sāmmukhyaṁ vāñchann upadiśati --*  
*daivī hy eṣā guṇa-mayī mama māyā duratyayā \|*  
*mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te \|\| iti*  
*satāṁ prasaṅgān mama vīrya-saṁvido*  
*bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ \|*  
*taj-joṣaṇād āśv apavarga-vartmani*  
*śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati \|\|*  
*līlayā śrīmad-vyāsa-rūpeṇa tu viśiṣṭayā tad-upadiṣṭavān ity anantaram evāyāsyati \| anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād iti \| tasmād dvayor api tat tat samañjasaṁ jñeyam \|*  
*nanu māyā khalu śaktiḥ śaktiś ca kārya-kṣamatvaṁ \| tac ca dharma-viśeṣaḥ \| tasyāḥ kathaṁ lajjādikam \| ucyate evaṁ saty api bhagavati tāsāṁ śaktīnām adhiṣṭhātṛ-devyaḥ śrūyante, yathā kenopaniṣadi mahendra-māyayoḥ saṁvādaḥ \| tad āstāṁ, prastutaṁ prastūyate \|\|33\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The Lord does not interfere with the skilful actions of māyā, the controller of the material world, who has been his devotee without beginning. Desiring that the jīvas become favorable to him out of fear of māyā, the Lord teaches:**

***daivī hy eṣā guṇa-mayī mama māyā duratyayā \|***  
***mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te \|\|14\|\|***

**My māyā, made of the guṇas, fit for the jīva’s pleasure, is hard to surpass, but those who surrender to me alone can cross over this māyā. BG 7.14**

***satāṁ prasaṅgān mama vīrya-saṁvido***  
***bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ***  
***taj-joṣaṇād āśv apavarga-vartmani***  
***śraddhā ratir bhaktir anukramiṣyati***

**From association with the best devotees, topics of my glorious pastimes become directly realized, bringing the devotee to** ***niṣṭhā*****. Then the topics become an elixir for the heart and ears at the stage of** ***ruci*****. By taste for these topics,** ***āsakti, bhāva*** **and then** ***prema*** **for the Lord who is the destroyer of material life, quickly develop in sequence. SB 3.25.25**

**The Lord, as ācārya, later teaches this particularly through his form of Vyāsa, a līlāvatāra. Vyāsa saw bhakti which destroys māyā (*****anarthas anarthopaśamaṁ*****). Thus there are coordinated functions of the māyā and the Lord concerning bewilderment of the jīva and desiring to make the jīva favorable.**

**“Māyā is a śakti and a śakti has ability to act. Śakti is merely a quality. Why then does māyā have the particular quality of being ashamed (if she is just an insentient śakti)?” Scriptures describe that there are controlling deities of these śaktis. One should see the discussion between Indra and Māyā (who is personified as a person) in Kena Upaniṣad. Now let us return to the topic.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

“Why does the Lord tolerate this bewilderment of the jīva?” This is answered in the first sentence. Thus there is no defect in his quality of mercy. He desires that the jīva becomes favorable to him. Out of their fear of māyā he desires that the jīvas turn towards him. In this way there is no injury to his mercy (by having māyā give them suffering). The verse from the Gītā indicates surrender to the Lord. He also teaches that the cause of becoming favorable is association with devotees.

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā \|*  
*upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ \|\|34\|\|*

**Know the Lord by surrender, questioning and service. The seers of truth, the jñānīs, will teach you this knowledge. BG 4.34**

Vyāsa teaches this as a līlāvatāra, in the form of an ācārya. There is thus a consistency in māyā causing bewilderment and the Lord desiring that the jīva be favorable.

“It was said that māyā is ashamed because she bewilders the jīva. How is that possible, since māyā is insentient and is just a quality like enthusiasm?” It is concluded in scripture that māyā has a controlling deity. This is similar to the deities which control mountains like the Vindhyas.

There is story about māyā in Kena Upaniṣad starting with *brahma ha devebhyo vijigye*: Brahman desired victory for the devatās. (Kena Upaniṣad 3.1) Paramātmā, seeing the pride of Agni, Vāyu and Indra, appeared in order to curb their pride. Not recognizing him, they began to question among themselves. The Lord tested their power by holding a blade of grass. Agni, who said he could burn anything, could not burn it and Vāyu, who said he could take up anything, could not take up the straw. To understand the powers of Indra, the Lord disappeared. Indra approached Umā (māyā) in the sky and asked “Who is that?” She said, “That is Brahman.”

### Text 34

*tatra jīvasya tādṛśa-cid-rūpatve’pi parameśvarato vailakṣaṇyaṁ tad-apāśrayam iti, yathā sammohita iti ca darśayati \|\|34\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**That the jīva, though a conscious entity, is different from the Lord is shown in SB 1.7.4-5: māyā takes shelter behind the Lord and she bewilders the jīva.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

*Māyāṁ ca tad-apāśrayām* shows that māyā is controlled by the Lord. *Yayā sammohito jīva* shows that the jīva is controlled by māyā. This shows that the jīva is different from the Lord. It is said in the verse that Vyāsa saw (*apaśyat*) māyā. This implies time. Thus Vyāsa in samādhi saw four tattvas: the Lord, the jīva, māyā and time. These four are eternal.

*atha ha vāva nityāni puruṣaḥ prakṛtir ātmā kālaḥ*

**The Lord, prakṛti, jīva and time are eternal. Bhallaveya-śruti**

*nityo nityānāṁ cetanaś cetanānām eko bahūnāṁ yo vidadhāti kāmān*

**The Lord is the chief eternal and the chief consciousness. He is the one Lord who fulfills the desires of many. Kaṭha Upaniṣad 2.2.13**

*ajām ekāṁ lohita-śukla-kṛṣṇāṁ*  
*bahvīḥ prajāḥ sṛjamānāṁ sarūpāḥ \|*  
*ajo hy eko juṣamāṇo’nuśete*  
*jahāty enāṁ bhukta-bhogām ajo’nyaḥ \|\|*

**One unborn entity, satisfied, follows another unborn entity--a female entity who is red, white and black and who produces many offspring of similar nature. Another male gives up this female who has been enjoyed. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 4.5**

*avikārāya śuddhāya nityāya parmātmane \|*  
*sadaika-rūpa-rūpāya viṣṇave sarva-jiṣṇave \|\|*

**I offer respects to the Lord Viṣnu who is without change, pure, eternal, who is always in one form and who conquers all beings. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.2.1**

*pradhānaṁ puruṣaṁ cāpi praviśyātmecchayā hariḥ \|*  
*kṣobhayāmāsa samprāpte sarga-kāle vyayāvyayau \|\|*

**By his will the Lord enters pradhāna which is subject to change and also enters the jīva not subject to change, and agitates them at the time of creation. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.2.29**

*avyaktaṁ kāraṇaṁ yat tat pradhāna ṛṣi-sattamaiḥ \|*  
*procyate prakṛtiḥ sūkṣmā nityaṁ sad-asad-ātmakam \|\|*

**The best of sages say that prakṛti, subtle and eternal, composed of cause and effect, is called avyakta, kārana and pradhāna. Visṇu Purāṇa 1.2.19**

*anādir bhagavān kālo nānto’sya dvija vidyate \|*  
*avyucchinnās tatas tv ete sarga-sthity-anta-saṁyamau \|\|*

**There is no end for the Lord and time, which also have no beginning. The two are continuous, and control creation, maintenance and destruction. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 1.2.26**

Among them, the Lord, possessor of śaktis, is independent. The jīva, prakṛti and time, which are his śaktis, are dependent.

*viṣṇu-śaktiḥ parā proktā kṣetrajñākhyā tathāparā \|*  
*avidyā-karma-saṁjñānyā tṛtīyā śaktir iṣyate \|\|*

**The Lord has a superior energy, another energy called the jīva and a third energy, the material energy, called avidyā-karma. Viṣnu Purāna 6.7.61**

*puraiva puṁsāvadhṛto dharā-jvaro*  
*bhavadbhir aṁśair yaduṣūpajanyatām*  
*sa yāvad urvyā bharam īśvareśvaraḥ*  
*sva-kāla-śaktyā kṣapayaṁś cared bhuvi*

**Kṛṣṇa is already aware of the distress on earth. For as long as the source of all forms of the Lord remains visible on earth to diminish its burden, all of you devatās should appear as sons and grandsons along with his eternal associates in the family of the Yadus by his energy of time. SB 10.1.22**

The Lord is the all powerful conscious entity and the jīva is a minute conscious entity. Both have the quality of eternal knowledge. Māyā is insentient matter endowed with three guṇas. Time is insentient substance devoid of three guṇas, the cause of using expressions concerning past, present and future. Karma has no beginning but is liable to destruction.

*na karmāvibhāgād iti cen nānāditvāt*

**If you argue that in the beginning there was no division of karma, the answer is no, since karmas are without beginning. Brahma-sūtra 2.1.35**

The nature of existing substances or vastu should be known from śruti and smṛti.

### Text 35

*yarhy eva yad ekaṁ cid-rūpaṁ brahma māyāśrayatā-valitaṁ vidyāmayaṁ tarhy eva tan-māyā-viṣayatāpannam avidyā-paribhūtaṁ cety ayuktam iti jīveśvara-vibhāgo ‘vagataḥ \| tataś ca svarūpa-sāmarthya-vailakṣaṇyena tad dvitayaṁ mitho vilakṣaṇa-svarūpam evety āgatam \|\|35\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**It is illogical to say that the same Brahman or consciousness which becomes endowed with vidyā (knowledge) when giving shelter to māyā (becoming īśvara) then becomes overpowered by avidyā (ignorance) when subjected to māyā (as jīva). It is understood that there is difference between īśvara and jīva. From the verses quoted from Bhāgavatam, it is understood that the svarūpas of īsvara and jīva are different, being different in the abilities of their svarūpas.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

This will be explained in Paramātmā Sandarbha.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The one Brahman which is mere consciousness without qualities is described as the ultimate reality in śruti statements like *yat tu ekam evādvitīyam:* Brahman is one without a second, (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.2.1), *vijñānam ānandaṁ brahma:* Brahman is knowledge and bliss (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.28), and *neha nānāsti kiñcana:* there is no variety at all. (Bṛha-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.4.19). Endowed with vidyā, Brahman becomes īśvara-caitanya and endowed with avidyā, Brahman becomes jīva-caitanya through diffferentiation of cause and effect, a relationship caused by indescribable ignorance (*ajñāna*) which functions as vidyā and avidya. When ignorance is destroyed by knowledge of one’s svarūpa, states of īśvara and jīva cease to exist. Only the one state of mere consciousness without qualities exists. This is cunning Śaṅkara’s view. That is presented in this anuccheda. The meaning is clear.

Simultaneously, suddenly, by combination with ignorance, one part of Brahman gives shelter to vidyā and another part is defeated by avidyā. By what fault has Brahman become subject to various disturances and experiences of suffering? It is impossible to explain how Brahman develops a relationship with ignorance. Thus the categories described by Śaṅkara are not accepted. We accept what was seen by Vyāsa.

### Text 36

*na copādhitāratamya maya-pariccheda-pratibimbatvādi-vyavasthayā tayor vibhāgaḥ syāt \|\|36\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Jīva and Brahman cannot become separate by an arrangement of reflection or bifurcation through different upādhis.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Brahman divides into īśvara and jīva by division of the one Brahman through māyā according to śruti. *yat tu indro māyābhiḥ puru-rūpa īyate:* Brahman appears as many by his māyā*.* (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 2.5.19) Divided by vidyā, īśvara, a huge portion appears. Divided by avidyā, a small portion, the jīva, appears. This is similar to the sky being covered by a pot and a small cup.

*yathā hy ayaṁ jyotir ātmā vivasvān*  
*apo bhittvā bahudhaiko’nugacchan \|*  
*upādhinā kriyate bheda-rūpo*  
*devaḥ kṣetreṣv evam ajo’yam ātmā \|\|*

**Just as the one light of the sun, divided by water becomes many, the unborn ātmā is made into many forms by upādhis in various bodies.**

The division of īśvra and jīva is said to take place by a reflection of Brahman as described above. The reflection in vidyā is īśvara. The reflection in avidyā is the jīva. This is similar to a reflection of the sun in a lake and in a pot. This is Śaṅkara’s explanation. To refute this view the author speaks this anuccheda. The distinction of īśvara and jīva does not take place by this means.

### Text 37

*tatra yady upādher anāvidyakatvena vāstavatvaṁ tarhy aviṣayasya tasya pariccheda-viṣayatvāsambhavaḥ \| nirdharmakasya vyāpakasya niravayavasya ca pratibimbatvāyogo’pi \| upādhi-sambandhābhāvāt bimba-pratibimba-bhedābhāvāt, dṛśyatvābhāvāc ca \| upādhi-paricchinnākāśastha-jyotir-aṁśasyeva pratibimbo dṛśyate, na tv ākāśasya dṛśyatvābhāvād eva \|\|37\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If Brahman has as its real nature the quality of not being subjected to anything else, it is impossible for Brahman, not subject to anything, to be subjected to bifurcation by upādhis.**

**That which is without qualities cannot be subject to reflection, since something without qualities can have no relationship to upādhis. That which is all pervading cannot be subject to reflection, since it cannot be differentiated into object and reflection (since it extends everywhere as one entity). That which is without limbs (having no form) cannot be subject to reflection because it is invisible. A portion of light within the sky interrupted by an upādhis becomes a reflection. The sky itself cannot be seen as a reflection.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

This anuccheda explains why the theory is illogical. The proposal concerning dividing Brahman is first refuted (in the first sentence). If Brahman exists as a real object without an unreal upādhi of ignorance such as mistaking a rope for a snake, it is impossible for Brahman which is not the object of any action to be the object of division. *Agṛhyo na hi gṛhyate:* Brahman which is imperceptible cannot be perceived. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.26) It should also be understood that īśvara and jīva cannot be parts of severed Brahman, separated by real upādhis, like a chisel which cuts stone, since Brahman cannot be cut and is understood to be one continuous entity. This would mean that īśvara and jīva have a beginning since bifrucation is a process of cutting one entity into two or three. If one argues that Brahman is undivided but has separate places which are endowed with upādhis, this proposition is untenable.

Since the upādhis (jīva and īśvara) move about, it is not possible for the part of Brahman endowed with upādhis to move about also (Brahman is supposed to be immovable) and at every moment there would be difference in the location of Brahman endowed with upādhis. And at every moment different parts of Brahman would be endowed and unendowed with upādhis. ( If one argues that the whole of Brahman is covered by upādhis), Brahman cannot be completely covered because then it would not be called by the name “uncovered Brahman.” If one proposes that the upādhis do not have their support in Brahman at all, then there would be no liberation for jīva and īśvara (since they would have no relationship with Brahman at any stage). Thus the doctine of bifurcation Brahman has been defeated.

The theory of reflection of Brahman is then refuted. There can be no reflected image of Brahman as īśvara and jīva because an upādhi cannot have a relationship with something without qualities, because something which is all-pervading cannot have difference of object and its reflection (since it encompassese everything) and something without limbs is invisible. One can see a reflection of the sun which has qualities like form, which is limited and which has limbs on an upādhi like water, with distance between them. This cannot be said of Brahman (which has no qualities, is unlimited and has no limbs).

“Just as we can see a reflection of the sky, which has similar qualities of being unlimited and without form and qualities, so we can see a reflection of Brahman.” No, we see the reflections of portions of light-- planets and constellations, limited by upādhis-- not the sky. Otherwise one could also claim to see air, time and space. “The reflection of Brahman is like a sound and its echo.” That also is not appropriate since it becomes a different proposal. Thus the theory of reflection is also insignificant.

### Text 38

*tathā vāstava-paricchedādau sati sāmānādhikaraṇya-jñāna-mātreṇa na tat-tyāgaś ca bhavet \| tat-padārtha-prabhāvas tatra kāraṇam iti ced asmākam eva mata-sammatam \|\|38\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If there is actual bifurcation of Brahman, that cannot be given up merely by knowledge of jīva being none other than Brahman. If you say that the cause of jīva being separate from Brahman is the power of Brahman itself, that is our view.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

If the upādhi is real, then it is impossible to believe that just by the knowledge of “I am Brahman” one attains that identity. That is explained. This applies to Brahman being bifurcated by upādhis or being reflected. Just by one day saying “I am a king” it is never seen that one becomes a king.

“Let the liberation of īśvara and jīva occur because of the power of some arrangement of Brahman.” That is harmful to your position (since it admits Brahman has awareness beyond itself and the ability to act).

### Text 39

*upādher āvidyakatve tu tatra tat-paricchinnatvāder apy aghaṭamānatvād āvidyakatvam eveti ghaṭākāśādiṣu vāstavopādhimaya-tad-darśanayā na teṣām avāstava-svapna-dṛṣṭāntopajīvināṁ siddhāntaḥ sidhyati, ghaṭamānāghaṭamānayoḥ saṅgateḥ kartum aśakyatvāt \| tataś ca teṣāṁ tat tat sarvam avidyāvilasitam eveti svarūpam aprāptena tena tena tat-tad-vyavasthāpayitum aśakyam \|\|39\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**But if the upādhi is unreal, then bifurcation of Brahman or reflection of Brahman must be unreal since it cannot actually occur. Jīva’s existence being insubstantial like a dream cannot be proved by showing upādhis on real object like pots restricting part of the sky or reflecting part of the sky, since it is impossible to make a connection (by analogy) between something which occurs with something which does not occur. Thus these explanations are all a manifestation of ignorance.**

**It is impossible to establish these concepts of bifurcation and reflection, which cannot logically exist.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Bifurcation and reflection of Brahman by unreal upādhis is now refuted.  *Āvidyaka* means false, like thinking a rope is a snake. Bifurcation and reflection caused by false upādhis are also false since nothing actually occurs. By showing examples of real upādhis where a pot divides up the sky and the water in a pot produces a reflection of the sky, one cannot prove a claim depending on falsity of īśvara and jīva like a dream, since they are suppposed to be one consciousness alone.

If the upādhis are false, then bifurcation and reflection of Brahman is also false. It is contradictory to use real pots and real water in pots as an example for unreal upādhis. Showing the example of pot and water in a pot is a real occurance whereas what is to be proved, vidya and avidyā covering Brahman, is not a real occurance. It is imposible to combine the two on the basis of similarity, since there is no similarity at all. Thus the theory of bifurcation and reflection of Brahman is a manifestation of ignorance only. According to what was said, it is impossible to establish (*tat-tad-vyavasthāpayitum*) creation of īśvara and jīva by bifurcation (*tena*) or reflection (*tena*) which do not even exist (*aprāptena*).

Thus the categories shown by Vyāsa are true, since the counter proposals have been defeated.

### Text 40

*iti brahmāvidyayoḥ paryavasāne sati yad eva brahma cinmātratven āvidyāyogasyāty antābhāvāspadatvāc chuddhaṁ tad eva tad-yogād aśuddhyā jīvaḥ \| punas tad eva jīvāvidyā-kalpita-māyāśrayatvād īśvaras tad eva ca tan-māyā-viṣayatvāj jīva iti virodhas tad-avastha eva syāt \| tatra ca śuddhāyāṁ city avidyā \| tad-avidyā-kalpitopādhau tasyām īśvarākhyāyāṁ vidyeti, tathā vidyāvattve’pi māyikatvam ity asamañjasā ca kalpanā syād ity ādy anusandheyam \|\|40\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**This philosophy ends in two elements: Brahman and ignorance. Brahman is pure since it can never be subjected to ignorance, being consciousness alone at all times. Because of a relationship with ignorance, which is impurity, jīva arises. Then īśvara arises from Brahman giving shelter to māyā produced by jīva’s ignorance. Other jīvas arise by Brahman being the subject of that same māyā. Thus Brahman is filled with contradictions. Ignorance exists in the pure consciousness and vidyā exists in an entity called īśvara, an upādhi created by that ignorance. Though appearing to be a thoughtful theory, it is actually cheating. The contradictions should be considered.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

“Our conclusion is not bifurcation or reflection, since these are explanations made for the understanding of ignorant people. The conclusion is one jīva only.” That is described in the following:

*sa eva māyā parimohitātmā*  
*śarīram āsthāya karoti sarvam \|*  
*striyanna-pānādi-vicitra-bhogaiḥ*  
*sa eva jāgrat parituṣṭim eti \|\|*

**This ātmā, bewildered by māyā, situated in the body, does everything. In waking state it becomes satisfied by various enjoyments like woman, food and drink. Kaivalya Upaniṣad 12**

Śruti also says *ekam evādvitīyam*: there is one entity without a second. Thus there is one ātmā, mere consciousness, and nothing else. It produces māyā made of the guṇas, as an effect of disturbance by avidyā and then produces one entity identified as “I” (*aham artha)* and many entities identified as you (*yuṣmad artha--*perceived objects). The entity with identity of “I” is the puruṣa with his own form. The entities identified as perceived by *aham-artha* are mahat-tattva and other elments down to earth, which are all material and unconscious, other puruṣas of the same sort, and one special puruṣa called the supreme Lord. Thus there are three varieties of objects perceived.

*jīveśāv ābhāsena karoti māyā cāvidyā ca svayam eva bhavati*

**Māyā and avidyā appear by themselves and create jīva and īśvara as shadows. Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad**

By association with the guṇas the capacity as agent and enjoyer is placed in ātmā. In a dream someone imagines a palace, a king and citizens and thinks himself the ruler. When knowledge appears, when one wakes up, nothing else exists. Only the one ātmā which is consciousness remains.

This anuccheda refutes this idea. According to what was previously said, with the refutation of bifurcation and reflection of Brahman, only Brahman and ignorance are left to be discussed. Brahman is completely free of igorance since it is not subject to anything. *Agṛhyo na hi gṛyate*: that which is imperceptible cannot be perceived. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 3.9.26) Because the two (Brahman and ignorance) are contradictory it is impossible to establish their interaction.

When there is suddenly a relation between avidyā and pure Brahman (*tatra śuddhāyām*), jīva arises from the relationship. Becoming the shelter of māyā produced by that jīva, Brahman becomes īśvara. Brahman overcome by the māyā of īśvara becomes the individual jīvas.

This is the gossip of the ignorant, not the learned. Though appearing to be a learned conclusion it is cheating. The quotation from Kaivalya Upaniṣad means that jīva is none other than Brahman because Brahman functions as jīva’s support and because Brahman is all-pervading. This is the explanation of the wise. Saying the jīva and īśvara are created by māyā in Nṛsiṁha-tāpanī Upaniṣad is merely expressing the ideas created by logicians who are bewildered by māyā.

### Text 41

*kiṁ ca, yady atrābheda eva tātparyam abhaviṣyat tarhy ekam eva brahmājñānena bhinnaṁ, jñānena tu tasya bhedamayaṁ duḥkhaṁ vilīyata ity apaśyad ity evāvakṣyat \| tathā śrī-bhagaval-līlādīnāṁ vāstavatvābhāve sati śrī-śuka-hṛdaya-virodhaś ca jāyate \|\|41\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If the conclusion were ultimately non-difference of the Lord and the jīva in Bhāgavatam, it would be stated that Vyāsa had seen that, by ignorance of Brahman, the one Brahman would become different (producing jīva) and that by knowledge, suffering caused by difference disappeared. Since the Lord’s pastimes would become completely unreal, this conception is contrary to the heart of Śukadeva.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

This is contrary to the heart of Śukadeva. If the powers of the Lord were just creations of avidyā, Śukadeva would not have become attracted to Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes as stated in the text. Later in Bhagavat Sandarbha this will be discussed in detail.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Another failure in the proposition is discussed. If non-difference is the conclusion in Bhāgavatam, Sūta would have said that Vyāsa saw Brahman divided by ignorance and suffering destroyed by knowledge. But that is not stated. Vyāsa saw the complete puruṣa and the jīva bewildred by māyā which took shelter of the Lord and he saw bhakti of the Lord which destroyed the anarthas.

### Text 42

*tasmāt pariccheda-pratibimbatvādi-pratipādaka-śāstrāṇy api kathañcit tat-sādṛśyena gauṇyaiva vṛttyā pravarteran \| ambuvad agrahaṇāt tu na tathātvam , vṛddhi-hrāsa-bhāktvam antar- bhāvād ubhaya-sāmañjasyodevam iti pūrvottara-pakṣamaya-nyāyābhyām \|\|42\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Scriptural statements concerning jīva as a limitation or reflection of Brahman are employed with** ***gauṇya-vṛrti*** **(secondary meaning) to show similarity. The argument and conclusion are stated in the following:**

***ambuvad agrahaṇāt tu na tathātvam***

**There is no similarity because one does not see distance between the Lord and the jīva as one sees distance between the sun and water to cause the reflection. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.19**

***vṛddhi-hrāsa-bhāktvam antarbhāvād ubhaya-sāmañjasyodevam***

**The comparison with the sun is accepted in the sense that it illustrates the nature of greatness of the Lord and dependence of the jīva, since this is included in the scriptural conclusion and harmonizes the elements of the comparison. Brahma-sūtra 3.2.20**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The scriptures describing reflection and bifurcation are used to show similarity to reflection and bifurcation using gauṇa-vṛṭti. Devadatta is a lion. By gauṇa-vṛtti it means that Devadatta is similar to a lion not that he is a lion. “How can this be determined as the meaning?” Vyāsa has shown this in two sūtras.

The first sūtra refutes both propositions (bifurcation and reflection) because of their impossibility. A piece of land is cut by water and Brahman is not cut by upādhis. Brahman cannot accept upādhis as land is cut by water. Śruti says *agṛhyo na hi gṛhyate*: Brahman is not subject to any upādhi. (Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upanisād 3.9.26) Thus it is not true that Brahmaṇ is cut by upādhis.

The reflection of the sun is seen in water as a fragment, but the reflection of Brahman is not seen since Brahman is all pervading. Brahman does not have a reflection.

What do the scriptural statements mean? The second sūtra answers. The two statements do not employ mukhya-vṛtti (literal meaning). The examples are used to illustrate only qualities of jīva and Brahman (*vṛddhi-hrāsa-bhāktvam*). Thus the Lord and jīva are a big piece of land and a small piece of land or the sun represents the Lord and the reflection indicates the dependent jīva. Why? Because taking the partial analogy fulfills the meaning of scripture (*antarbhāvāt*). Only by taking this meaning is there a correct correlation of the object and the compared object.

In the first sūtra both proposals are refuted. In the second sūtra, the truth is established by gauṇa-vṛtti. The opinion of the author of the sūtras is that the jīva is like an aṁśa or reflection of Brahman and the opinion of cheaters who are opposed to the Lord is that the Lord is also an aṁśa or reflection of Brahman.

### Text 43

*tata evābheda-śāstrāṇy ubhayoś cid-rūpatve jīva-samūhasya durghaṭa-ghaṭanā-paṭīyasyā svābhāvika-tad-acintya-śaktyā svabhāvata eva tad-raśmi-paramāṇu-guṇa-sthānīyatvāt tad-vyatirekeṇāvyatirekeṇa ca virodhaṁ parihṛtyāgre muhur api tad etad-vyāsa-samādhi-labdha-siddhānta-yojanāya yojanīyāni \|\|43\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Thus scriptures speaking of non-difference of Brahman and jīva should be coordinated with the conclusion that Vyāsa experienced in samādhi. Both the jīva and the Lord are conscious entities. By the acintya-śakti of the Lord, natural to him, which makes the impossible possible, there can be both difference and non-difference of jīva and Brahman. Thus contradiction is avoided. This is understood by comparing the jīva to a particle in the ray of the sun which represents the Lord.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Thus (*tataḥ*), because it was concluded that the the meaning of bifurcation and reflection of Brahman mentioned in the scriptures is similarity only, scriptures speaking of non-difference of Brahman and jīva should be coordinated with the conclusion that Vyāsa experienced in samādhi.

Some statements of non- difference are as follows. *tvaṁ vā aham asmi bhagavo deva aham vai tvam asi*: O Lord, I am you and you are me.(*Varāha Upaniṣad* 33) *tattvam asi:* you are that. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad)

These statements of non-difference should always be construed to agree with Vyāsa’s conclusion in samādhi.

The Lord and the jīva are conscous entities, just as two brāhmaṇas, pale and dark, young and very young are one in the sense that both are brāhmaṇas. They are non-different in class (being conscious entities), but not in specific appearance. By the acintya-śakti of the Lord, natural to him, which makes the impossible possible, there can be both difference and non-difference of jīva and Brahman, because of qualities like being particle of light and the sun. In this manner contradiction is avoided.

The superior śakti of the Lord, related to his svarūpa, is like the warmth of the sun. Śruti says:

*parāsya śaktir vividhaiva śrūyate svābhāvikī jñāna-bala-kriyā ca*

**The Lord has many intrinsic energies called knowledge, strength and action. Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.8**

Smṛti says:

*viṣṇu-śaktiḥ parā proktā*

**The śakti of Viṣnū is called parā. Viṣṇu Purāṇa 6.7.61**

This superior śakti controls everything else. Because they exist without giving up their natures, prakṛti, time and karma cannot touch the Lord who is situated within them. Rather, out of fear of him they maintain their particular natures. Though the jīvas have a nature similar to the Lord, they cannot achieve the same status as the Lord. Taking shelter of him, the jīvas attain their true function. The Lord in relation to the jīva is like the chief prāṇa in relation to the senses like the ear. Statements of non-difference saying that the jīvas is a form of the Lord, though he is dependent on the Lord and functions only in relation to the Lord, are not contradictory to statements of difference, which is what Vyāsa saw in samādhi.

Thus here it is proved that there can be no absolute identity between the svarūpas of the Lord and the jīva.

### Text 44

*tad evaṁ māyāśrayatva-māyā-mohitatvābhyāṁ sthite dvayor bhede tad-bhajanasyaivābhidheyatvam āyātam \|\|44\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since jīva is bewildered by māyā and Brahman is the shelter of māyā, they are established as different. That ultimately means that Brahman (the Lord) should be worshipped. Thus the abhidheya is established.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The meaning is clear. The Lord should be worshipped because he destroys māyā.

### Text 45

*ataḥ śrī-bhagavata eva sarva-hitopadeṣṭṛtvāt, sarva-dhuḥkha-haratvāt, raśmīnāṁ sūryavat sarveṣāṁ parama-svarūpatvāt sarvādhika-guṇa-śālitvāt, parama-premayogatvam iti prayojanaṁ ca sthāpitam \|\|45\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since the Lord gives instructions for everyone’s benefit, since he destroys all suffering, since he is the highest svarūpa among all beings, like the sun in relation to its rays, and since he is endowed with more qualities than anyone else, the Lord is the suitable object of the highest prema. Thus the prayojana is established.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Worship of Bhagavān is the abhidheya since he destroys the illusions of māyā. Bhagavān is suitable for the prema of the worshippers. Because of this (*ataḥ*), it is established that he is the prayojana. Because by worshipping him māyā is destroyed, Bhāgavan is suitable object of the highest prema. The jīvātmā is qualified for prema and the superme ātmā, Bhagavān, is suitable for the highest prema. Why? Four reasons are given.

The sun is not the svarūpa of the rays but the supreme svarūpa. Similarly Bhagavān is the supreme svarūpa for the jīvas. Oneness is rejected. It is said in Antaryāmī-brāhmaṇa and Saubāla-brāhmaṇa that the jīvas are the body of the Lord. The Lord is the śarīrī. The difference is clear. Thus it is said here that he has greater qualities than the jīva.

### Text 46

*tatrābhidheyaṁ ca tādṛśatvena dṛṣṭavān api, yatas tat-pravṛtty-arthaṁ śrī-bhāgavatākhyām imāṁ sātvata-saṁhitāṁ pravartitavān ity āha anartheti \| bhaktiyogaḥ śravaṇa-kīrtanādi-lakṣaṇaḥ sādhana-bhaktiḥ, na tu prema-lakṣaṇaḥ \| anuṣṭhānaṁ hy upadeśāpekṣaṁ prema tu tat-prasādāpekṣam iti tathāpi tasya tat-prasāda-hetos tat-prema-phala-garbhatvāt sākṣād evānarthopaśamanatvaṁ, na tv anyasāpekṣatvena \| yat karmabhir yat tapasā jñāna-vairāgyataś ca yat ity ādau sarvaṁ mad-bhakti-yogena mad-bhakto labhate’ñjasā \| svargāpavargaṁ ity ādeḥ \| jñānādes tu bhakti-sāpekṣatvam eva śreyaḥ-sṛtiṁ bhaktim ity ādeḥ \|*  
*athavā anarthasya saṁsāra-vyasanasya tāvat sākṣād-avyavadhānenopaśamanaṁ sammohādi-dvayasya tu premākhya-svīya-phala-dvārety arthaḥ \| ataḥ pūrvavad evātrābhidheyaṁ darśitam \|\|46\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Since Vyāsa saw the abhidheya as bhakti which destroys māyā, he advocated the Sātvata-saṁhitā called Bhāgavatam in order to promote bhakti as the abhidheya.**

***anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje \|***  
***lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām \|\|***

**And Vyāsa saw bhakti-yoga to the Lord which effectively destroys jīva’s saṁsāra. Learned Vyāsa then wrote the Bhāgavatam for ignorant people. SB 1.7.6**

**Bhakti-yoga here means sādhana-bhakti consisting of hearing, chanting and other actions rather than prema. Performance of sādhana-bhakti depends on instruction whereas prema depends on the Lord’s mercy. Since the performance of sādhana is a cause of the Lord’s mercy, and includes in it the result called prema, sādhana directly destroys jīva’s saṁsāra** ***(anarthopaśamam*****). The destruction of saṁsāra does not depend on anything else.**

***yat karmabhir yat tapasā jñāna-vairāgyataś ca yat***  
***yogena dāna-dharmeṇa śreyobhir itarair api***  
***sarvaṁ mad-bhakti-yogena mad-bhakto labhate ’ñjasā***  
***svargāpavargaṁ mad-dhāma kathañcid yadi vāñchati***

**Everything that can be achieved by karma, penance, jñāna, vairāgya, mystic yoga, charity, dharma and all other auspicious means of perfecting life is easily achieved by my devotee through bhakti. If somehow or other my devotee desires Svarga, liberation, or residence in my abode, he easily achieves such benedictions. SB 11.20.32-33**

**Jñāna and other processes depend on bhakti:**

***śreyaḥ-sṛtiṁ bhaktim udasya te vibho***  
***kliśyanti ye kevala-bodha-labdhaye***  
***teṣām asau kleśala eva śiṣyate***  
***nānyad yathā sthūla-tuṣāvaghātinām***

**O Lord! If fools give up bhakti, the all-inclusive path, and suffer to attain realization of ātmā without bhakti, they simply attain suffering and nothing else. They are like fools who beat empty husks. SB 10.14.4**

**Or another meaning is that sādhana-bhakti destroys suffering of saṁsāra directly, without interruption. The destruction of bewilderment and false identity however is accomplished by bhakti’s result, prema. As previously, the abhidheya is bhakti.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Vyāsa saw the abhidheya as that which destroys māyā . The performance of bhakti, using the senses, depends on instructions. Prema depends on the Lord’s mercy. The cause of mercy is performance of hearing and chanting. Destruction of anarthas does not need assistance of karma or jñāna. Jñāna refers to meditation on Brahman.  *Sammohanādi* means bewilderment and thinking oneself to be the material body. Since sādhana bhakti destroys anarthas, the abhidheya is still bhakti.

### Text 47

*atha pūrvadeva prayojanaṁ ca spaṣṭayituṁ pūrvoktasya pūrṇa-puruṣasya ca śrī-kṛṣṇa-svarūpatvaṁ vyañjayituṁ, grantha-phala-nirdeśa-dvārā tatra tad-anubhavāntaraṁ pratipādayann āha yasyām iti \| bhaktiḥ premā śravaṇa-rūpayā sādhana-bhaktyā sādhyatvāt \| utpadyate āvirbhavati \| tasyānuṣaṅgikaṁ guṇam āha śoketi \| atraiṣāṁ saṁskāro’pi naśyatīti bhāvaḥ \| prītir na yāvan mayi vāsudeva na mucyate deha-yogena tāvat iti śrī-ṛṣabhadeva-vākyāt \| parama-puruṣe pūrvokta-pūrṇa-puruṣe \| kim ākāra ity apekṣāyām āha kṛṣṇe \| kṛṣṇas tu bhagavān svayam ity ādi śāstra-sahasra-bhāvitāntaḥ-karaṇānāṁ paramparayā tat-prasiddi-madhya-pātināṁ cāsāṅkhya-lokānāṁ tan-nāma-śravaṇa-mātreṇa yaḥ prathama-pratīti-viṣayaḥ syāt, tathā tan-nāmnaḥ prathamākṣara-mātraṁ mantrāya kalpamānaṁ yasyābhimukhyāya syāt tad-ākāra ity arthaḥ \| āhuś ca nāma-kaumudī-kārāḥ \| kṛṣṇa-śabdasya tamāla-śyāmala-tviṣi yaśodāyāḥ stanandhaye para-brahmaṇi rūḍhiḥ iti \|\|47\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In order to make the prayojana clear and in order to indicate that the pūrṇa-puruṣa is the svarūpa of Kṛṣṇa, Vyāsa explains other realizations in samādhi, through indicating the result of studying the book.**

***yasyāṁ vai śrūyamāṇāyāṁ kṛṣṇe parama-pūruṣe \|***  
***bhaktir utpadyate puṁsaḥ śoka-moha-bhayāpahā \|\|***

**By hearing Bhāgavatam, prema for the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa appears. Hearing it also destroys lamentation, illusion and fear in the human being. SB 1.7.7**

**By hearing, prema** ***(bhaktiḥ*****) appears** ***(utpadyate*****) since prema is the result of sādhana of hearing. Secondary results of prema are mentioned: destruction of lamentation, fear and illusion. With the appearance of prema, the impressions** ***(saṁskāra)*** **of these are destroyed. Ṛṣabha says:**

***evaṁ manaḥ karma-vaśaṁ prayuṅkte***  
***avidyayātmany upadhīyamāne***  
***prītir na yāvan mayi vāsudeve***  
***na mucyate deha-yogena tāvat***

**Thus, karmas make the mind fixed upon further action. Since the jīva is engaged in action by ignorance, having accepted a subtle body, as long as he does not have devotion for me, Vāsudeva, he will not be liberated from the body. SB 5.5.6**

**This verse uses the words** ***parama-pūruṣe*** **to explain** ***pūṛnam puruṣam*** **mentioned previously*****.***  **What form is that? It is Kṛṣṇa** ***(kṛṣṇe*****). This means Kṛṣṇa is Svayam Bhagavān. This form becomes the first object of realization, just by hearing his name, for countless people--for those who have realized thousands of scriptures and those in the line of succession who were famous in the Middle Ages. Kṛṣṇa’s form is such that just by hearing the first syllable of his name, it becomes a mantra which turns a person towards him. That is the purpose in mentioned Kṛṣṇa’s name. The writer of Nāma-kaumudī says:**

***kṛṣṇa-śabdasya tamāla-śyāmala-tviṣi yaśodāyāḥ stanandhaye para-brahmaṇi rūḍhiḥ***

**The word Kṛṣṇa has the conventional meaning--the supreme Brahman in the form of Yaśodā’s son, having the complexion of a** ***tamāla*** **tree.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Vyāsa explains his other realizations in samādhi in order to make the prayojana or bhagavat-prema clear. Prema appears (rather than being created). It is impossible that it is created at a certain time, since it is essence of the cit-śakti (which is eternal). Along with prema, accompanying qualities are mentioned. With the appearance of prema , the impressions *(saṁskāra)* are destroyed.

The form of Kṛṣṇa is the prime object of realization for countless people such as Sūta and Jayadeva. *Tan-nāma* means Kṛṣṇa’s name. *Rūḍhi* is the meaning without taking the meaning of the roots and affixes, just as *mandapa* means a type of house or tent. Kṛṣṇa is famous as the son of Yaśodā.

### Text 48

*atha tasyaiva prayojanasya brahmānandānubhavād api paramatvam anubhūtavān \| yatas tādṛśaṁ śukam api tad-ānanda-vaiśiṣṭya-lambhanāya tām adhyāpayāmāsety āha sa saṁhitām iti \| kṛtvānukramya ceti prathamataḥ svayaṁ saṅkṣepeṇa kṛtvā paścāt tu śrī-nāradopadeśād anukrameṇa vivṛtyety arthaḥ \| ataeva śrīmad-bhāgavataṁ bhāratānantaraṁ yad atra śrūyate, yac cānyatrāṣṭādaśa-purāṇānantaraṁ bhāratam iti tad-dvayam api samāhitaṁ syāt \| brahmānandānubhava-nimagnatvāt nivṛtti-nirataṁ sarvato nivṛttau nirataṁ, tatrāvyabhicāriṇam apīty arthaḥ \|\|48\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Vyāsa realized this prayojana (prema) to be much greater that realization of the bliss of Brahman since he taught Śukadeva this scripture so that he could attain this bliss of prema. Previously Śukadeva was absorbed in the bliss of Brahman.**

***sa saṁhitāṁ bhāgavatīṁ kṛtvānukramya cātma-jam \|***  
***śukam adhyāpayām āsa nivṛtti-nirataṁ muniḥ \|\|***

**Having already made the Bhāgavatam, the sage Vyāsa revised it and taught it to his son Śukadeva who had realized Brahman. SB 1.7.8**

***Kṛtvānukramya*** **means that first Vyāsa composed Bhāgavatam in a condensed version and later, on instructions of Nārada, expanded it methodically. It is said that Bhāgavatam was written after Mahābhārata in Bhāgavatam and it is said elsewhere that Mahābhārata was written after the eighteen Purāṇas. The above information solves the contradiction. Śukadeva was completely** ***(niratam)*** **absorbed in bliss of Brahman (*****nivṛtti*****). Though fixed in Brahman, Vyāsa taught him this work.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Vyāsa realized prema was much more attractive than the happiness from Brahman. He taught this scripture to Śukadeva who had realized the bliss of Brahman in order that Śukadeva could attain the bliss of prema for Kṛṣṇa. It is said in Bhāgavatam (*yat atra*) that it was written after Mahābhārata. And in Matysa and other Purāṇa it is said that Mahābhārata was written after the eighteen Purāṇas by Vyāsa.

*aṣṭādaśa purāṇāni kṛtvā satyavatī-sutaḥ \|*  
*cakre bhāratam ākhyānaṁ vedārthair upabṛṁhitam \|\|*

**Having written the eighteen Purāṇas, Vyāsa wrote the Mahābhārata which explains the meaning of the Vedas. Skanda Purāna**

Śukadeva was fixed in the bliss of Brahman (*tatra avyabhicāriṇam*).

### Text 49

*tam etaṁ śrī-veda-vyāsasya samādhi-jātānubhavaṁ śrī-śaunaka-praśnottaratvena viśadayan sarvātmārāmānubhavena sahetukaṁ saṁvādayati ātmārāmāś ceti \| nirgranthā vidhi-niṣedhātītā nirgatāhaṅkāra-granthayo vā \| ahaitukīṁ phalānusandhi-rahitām \| atra sarvākṣepaparihārārtham āha itthambhūta ātmārāmāṇām apy ākarṣaṇa-svabhāvo guṇo yasya sa iti \| tam evārthaṁ śrī-śukasyāpy anubhavena saṁvādayati harer guṇeti \| śrī-vyāsadevād yat-kiñcic-chrutena guṇena pūrvam ākṣiptā matir yasya saḥ \| paścād adhyagāt mahad-vistīrṇam api \| tataś ca tat-saṅkathā-sauhārdena nityaṁ viṣṇu-janāḥ priyā yasya tathābhūto vā, teṣāṁ priyo vā svayam abhavad ity arthaḥ \|*

*ayaṁ bhāvaḥ brahma-vaivartānusāreṇa pūrvaṁ tāvad ayaṁ garbham ārabhya śrī-kṛṣṇasya svaritayā māyā-nivārakatvaṁ jñātavān \| tatra śrī-vyeda-vyāsas tu taṁ vaśīkartuṁ tad-ananya-sādhanaṁ śrī-bhāgavatam eva jñātvā, tad-guṇātiśaya-prakāśamayāṁs tadīya-padya-viśeṣān kathañcc chrāvayitvā tena tam ākṣipta-matiṁ kṛtvā, tad eva pūrṇaṁ tam adhyāpayāmāseti śrī-bhāgavata-mahimātiśayaḥ proktaḥ \| tad evaṁ darśitaṁ vaktuḥ śrī-śukasya vedavyāsasya ca samāna-hṛdayam \| tasmād vaktur hṛdayānurūpam eva sarvatra tātparyaṁ paryālocanīyaṁ nānyathā \| yad yat tad anyathā paryālocanaṁ, tatra tatra kupatha-gāmitaiveti niṣṭaṅkitam \|\| 1.7 \|\| śrī-sūtaḥ \|\| 49 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Clarifying the realization of Vyāsa which he had attained during samādhi by the question of Śaunaka (how could Śukadeva become attracted to anything other than Brahman), Sūta explains the reason, citing the experience of all ātmārāmas.**

***ātmārāmāś ca munayo nirgranthā apy urukrame \|***  
***kurvanty ahaitukīṁ bhaktim ittham-bhūta-guṇo hariḥ \|\|***

**Sages who are ātmārāmas, beyond the scriptures, false ego and rules, also practice unmotivated, pure bhakti to the master of pure bhakti*****,*** **Kṛṣṇa, since he possesses qualities attractive to even them. SB 1.7.10**

***Nirgrantha*** **means “beyond rules and prohibitions” or “free of the knot of ahaṅkāra.”** ***Ahaitukīm*** **means “without searching for results.” Ātmārāmas beyond rules perform unmotivated bhakti. The Lord has the quality of attracting ātmārāmas (*****itthambhūta-guṇaḥ*****). This is stated to prevent any criticism of the ātmārāma (who should be attracted to anything material). In order to show this, Sūta then speaks a verse using Śukadeva’s experience.**

***harer guṇākṣipta-matir bhagavān bādarāyaṇiḥ \|***  
***adhyagān mahad ākhyānaṁ nityaṁ viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ \|\|***

**Śukadeva, the son of Vyāsa, whose mind became distracted from trance by the qualities of Kṛṣṇa, and who became attracted to the Vaiṣṇavas, studied this great Bhāgavatam*****. SB 1.7.11***

**Śukadeva’s mind was agitated previously by qualities of Kṛṣṇa which he heard to a small degree from Vyāsadeva. He later studied the Bhāgavatam, though it was very large. He became affectionate to the devotees or they became affectionate to him (*****viṣṇu-jana-priyaḥ*****), with friendly conversations.**

**The meaning is this. According to Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa, previously, starting from his stay in the womb of his mother, Śukadeva was aware that Kṛṣṇa personally could ward off māyā. Vyāsadeva brought Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa showed himself to Śukadeva in the womb. Since māyā was thus removed, thinking himself successful, Śukadeva came out of the womb and left the house. In order to control him, Vyāsa taught him Bhāgavatam, which produces nothing except attraction to Kṛṣṇa. Making him hear particular verses which revealed Kṛṣṇa’s great qualities, Vyāsa agitated his mind. Then he made him study the full Bhāgavatam. This shows the greatness of Bhāgavatam. It has been shown that the speaker Śukadeva and Vedavyāsa had the same heart. Thus according to the heart of the speaker one should study the meaning of Bhāgavatam at all times. There is no other way. If one studies in another way, one will be led astray. That is indicated.**

**Sūta speaks the verse.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

There is acceptance of what Vyāsa saw in samādhi by all knowers of tattva. *Nirgranthā* means “persons free of ahaṅkāra which is generated from mahat-tattva.” The identity of the self within the ātmā is not extinguished at this time. This will be explained in Bhagavat Sandarbha. Vyāsa had Śukadeva hear certain verses, such as Pūtanā attaining the position of a nurse and Kṛṣṇa acting as Arjuna’s charioteer and doorkeeper. In Brahma-vaivarata Purāṇa it is explained that Śukadeva was born from a womb, but in Mahābhārata it is said that he was not born from a womb. Mahābhārata says Śukadeva appeared from the fire of a stick of wood.24 It is also described that he accepted a wife and had a daughter. This is adjusted by explaining that the differing events happened in a different day of Brahmā.

### Text 50

*atha krameṇa vistaratas tathaiva tātparyaṁ nirṇetuṁ sambandhābhidheya-prayojaneṣu ṣaḍbhiḥ sandarbhair nirṇeṣyamāṇeṣu prathamaṁ yasya vācya-vācakatā-sambandhīdaṁ śāstraṁ tad eva dharmaḥ projjhita-kaitavaḥ ity ādi-padye sāmānyākāratas tāvad āha vedyaṁ vāstavam atra vastu iti \|\| ṭīkā ca atra śrīmati sundare bhāgavate vāstavaṁ paramārtha-bhūtaṁ vastu vedyaṁ, na tu vaiśeṣikādivad dravya-guṇādi-rūpam ity eṣā \|\|1.1\|\| veda-vyāsaḥ \|\|50\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In order to discern the conclusion methodically and in detail, according to Śukadeva’s position, sambandha, abhidheya and prayojana will be discussed in six Sandarbhas.**

**First, the scripture Bhāgavatam has been described generally in the second verse as having the relationship (*****sambandha*****) of the object** ***(vācya)*** **and means of indicating it (*****vācakatā*****).**

***dharmaḥ projjhita-kaitavo ’tra paramo nirmatsarāṇāṁ satāṁ***  
***vedyaṁ vāstavam atra vastu śivadaṁ tāpa-trayonmūlanam***  
***śrīmad-bhāgavate mahā-muni-kṛte kiṁ vā parair īśvaraḥ***  
***sadyo hṛdy avarudhyate ’tra kṛtibhiḥ śuśrūṣubhis tat-kṣaṇāt \|\|1.1.2\|\|***

**The Supreme Lord becomes immediately captured in the heart of the accomplished devotees by hearing Bhāgavatam and even by those who have suddenly developed a desire to hear it. This does not happened with other works. In the Bhāgavatam alone, created by the Lord himself, is presented the real, permanent object which can be understood by those without selfish intentions, and which bestows auspiciousness and release from the material world of miseries. In the** ***Bhāgavatam*** **alone is presented the process for attaining that highest object, devoid of all material goals and liberation. SB 1.1.2**

***Vedyaṁ vāstavam atra vastu*** **indicates the object (*****vācya*****). The commentary says, “*****Vastu vedyam*** **means the highest spiritual object** ***(vastu*****) which is real (*****vāstavam*****), to be known, in the beautiful Bhāgavatam (*****atra*****). It is not a form made of material matter or material qualities as presented in Vaiśeṣika philosophy.” Veda-vyāsa speaks.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Now the author begins to show in detail the sambandha, abhidheya and prayojana which were mentioned in brief. This is done according to the views of Śukadeva and others (*tathaiva*). The second verse indicates the sambandha generally, without describing the Lord’s svarūpa, qualities or vibhūtis. The supreme goal to be known is not the matter and guṇas as described in the scriptures of Kaṇada and Gautama (which are also called *vastu*).

### Text 51

*atha kiṁ rūpaṁ tad-vastu-tattvam ity atrāha vadanti tat tattva-vidas tattvaṁ yaj jñānam advayam iti \| jñānaṁ cid-eka-rūpam \| advayatvaṁ cāsya svayaṁ-siddhatādṛśātādṛśa-tattvāntarābhāvaāt svaśaktyeka-sahāyatvāt \| paramāśrayaṁ taṁ vinā tāsām asiddhatvāc ca \| tattvam iti parama-puruṣārthatā-dyotanayā parama-sukha-rūpatvaṁ tasya bodhyate \| ataeva tasya nityatvaṁ ca darśitam \|\|1.2\|\| śrī-sutaḥ \|\|51\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**What is the form of that entity (*****vastu*****)?**

***vadanti tat tattva-vidas tattvaṁ yaj jñānam advayam \|***  
***brahmeti paramātmeti bhagavān iti śabdyate \|\|***

**The knowers of truth call this truth advayam-jñānam, the supreme conscious being, called Brahman by the jñānīs, Paramātmā by the yogīs and Bhagavān by the devotees. SB 1.2.11**

**This entity is a single form of consciousness (*****jñānam*****). It is without a second (*****advaitam*****) because it is assisted by its own śakti; because by its own eternal nature (*****svayam siddha*****) there is no other entity similar to or different from it; and because, without him as the supreme shelter, the śaktis cannot exist or function.** ***Tattvam*** **means the form of the highest happiness, since** ***tattva*** **denotes the highest puruṣārtha. Thus this highest entity must be eternal as well. Sūta speaks.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In order to describe the *vastu* or *tattva* with indications of its svarūpa, the author quotes another verse. *Svayam-siddha* means that it is accomplished by itself. For instance one may say that the servants are automatically austere. This means that the austere situation of a servant accomplishes his austerity by its nature. There is no similar entity—another supreme entity or jīva, and there is no dissimlar entity—material substance like prakṛti and kāla. The non-existence of anything else is self-accomplished or *svayam-siddha*. That is because everything else cannot exist without the Lord as the supreme shelter. It is *advayam* because it is assisted only by its own śakti, just as one may say “The bow is a second Pāṇḍu.”

“In the Upaniṣads Brahman is described as knowledge and bliss. That is its svarūpa. Why do you say that the supreme entity is only knowledge (*jñānam*).” The word *tattva* is used. This word indicates the highest entity. This also means happiness since happiness is the goal of all methods. Thus *tattva* means knowledge and happiness. “But knowledge and happiness are temporary.” The highest entity must also be eternal. It is eternal because it was explained that it was *svayam-siddhi-* self accomplished, with no cause. *Sad-akāraṇaṁ yat tan nityam*: that which has no cause is eternal. (Tīrthakāra)
