Thus this scripture deals with the sambandhi (topic of discussion) known as Brahman with the nature just described.

### Text 52

*nanu nīla-pītādy-ākāraṁ kṣaṇikam eva jñānaṁ dṛṣṭam, tat punar advayaṁ nityaṁ jñānaṁ kathaṁ lakṣyate yan-niṣṭham idaṁ śāstram \| ity atrāha sarva-vedānta-sāraṁ yad brahmātvekatva-lakṣaṇam \| vastv advitīyaṁ tan-niṣṭham iti satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma iti yasya svarūpam uktam, yenāśrutaṁ śrutaṁ bhavati iti yad-vijñānena sarva-vijñānaṁ pratijñātam \| sad eva saumyedam agra āsīt ity ādinā nikhila-jagad-eka-kāraṇatā \| tad aikṣata bahu syām ity anena satya-saṅkalpatā ca yasya pratipāditā, tena brahmaṇā svarūpa-śaktibhyāṁ sarva-bṛhattamena sārdham \| anena jīvenātmanā iti tadīyoktāvidantānirdeśena tato bhinnatve’py ātmatānirdeśena tad-ātmāṁśa-viśeṣatvena labdhasya bādarāyaṇa-samādhi-dṛṣṭa-yukter atyabhinnatā-rahitasya jīvātmano yad ekatvaṁ tattvam asi ity ādau jātyā tad-aṁśa-bhūta-cid-rūpatvena samānākāratā \| tad eva lakṣaṇaṁ prathamato jñāne sādhakatamaṁ yasya tathābhūtaṁ yat sarva-vedānta-sāram advitīyaṁ vastu tan-niṣṭhaṁ tad-eka-viṣayam idaṁ śrī-bhāgavatam iti prāktana-padyasthenānuṣaṅgaḥ \| yathā janma-prabhṛti kaścid gṛha-guhāvaruddhaḥ sūryaṁ vividiṣuḥ kathaṅcid gavākṣa-patitaṁ sūryāṁśu-kaṇaṁ darśayitvā kenacid upadiśyate eṣa sa iti \| etat tad-aṁśa-jyotiḥ-samānākāratayā tan-mahā-jyotir-maṇḍalam anusandhīyatām ity arthas tadvat \| jīvasya tathā tad-aṁśatvaṁ ca tac-chakti-viśeṣa-siddhatvenaiva paramātma-sandarbhe sthāpayiṣyāmaḥ \| tad etaj-jīvādi-lakṣaṇāṁśa-viśiṣṭatayaivopaniṣadas tasya sāṁśatvam api kvacid upadiśanti \| niraṁśatvopadeśikā śrutis tu kevala-tan-niṣṭhā \| atra kaivalyaika-prayojanam iti caturtha-pādaś ca kaivalya-padasya śuddhatva-mātra-vacanatvena śuddhatvasya ca śuddha-bhaktitvena paryavasānena prīti-sandarbhe vyākhyāsyate \|\|12\|13\|\| śrī-sūtaḥ \|\|52\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**“This jñāna is seen to become blue and then yellow (through awareness of objects). It is temporary. How can Bhāgavatam state that this jñāna is eternal and without a second?”**

***sarva-vedānta-sāraṁ yad brahmātmaikatva-lakṣaṇam***  
***vastv advitīyaṁ tan-niṣṭhaṁ kaivalyaika-prayojanam***

**This Bhāgavatam is the essence of all Vedānta philosophy because its subject matter is one Brahman, a substance with no duality. The main goal of the work is pure bhakti. SB 12.13.12**

**Brahman’s svarūpa is described in the following** ***satyaṁ jñānam anantaṁ brahma*****: Brahman is existence, knowledge and bliss. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.1.1)**

**He is the cause of the universe. That is stated in the following.** ***Yenāśrutaṁ śrutaṁ bhavati:*** **by this entity what was not heard becomes heard. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.1.3)** ***Yad-vijñānena sarva-vijñānaṁ pratijñātam:*** **by knowing him, everything becomes known. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad) This is the promise.** ***Sad eva saumyedam agra āsīt:*** **O gentle one, the Lord exists before everything else. (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.2.1) The Lord is satya-saṅkalpa (all his desires are fulfilled).** ***Tad aikṣata bahu syām*****: he glanced, thinking “Let me be many.” (Chāndogya Upaniṣad)**

**In the following, though the jīva is actually separate from the Lord, by calling him** ***idam (anena*****), it is also indicated that the jīva is a portion of the Paramātmā by calling him** ***ātmā.*** **He accompanies the Lord who is the greatest, who is endowed with his svarūpa and his śakti.**

***anena jīvenātmanānupraviśya nāma-rūpe vyākaravāṇi***

**Let me enter with the jīva, ātmā, and divide name and form. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.3.2**

**In this statement spoken by the Lord, the jīva is considered to be one with the Lord since he is devoid of extreme difference, according to what Vyāsa saw in samādhi. Thus, in statements like** ***tattvam asi*****: you are that (Chāndogya Upaniṣad 6.8.7) there is identity of the Lord and jīva since the jīva is a conscious aṁśa of the Lord. Speaking of identity is a most effective means of presenting jñāna in the beginning.**

**The essence of all Vedānta (*****sarva-vedānta-sāram*****), having** ***advitīya- vastu***  **as the sole object (*****niṣṭham*****), is Bhāgavatam. This is understood from the previous verse:**

***ādi-madhyāvasāneṣu vairāgyākhyāna-saṁyutam***  
***hari-līlā-kathā-vrātā- mṛtānandita-sat-suram***

**From beginning to end, the Śrīmad Bhāgavatam is full of the Lord’s pastimes which give bliss to devotees endowed with a sense of renunciation. SB 12.13.11**

**Some person, desiring to see the sun, but confined to closed room from birth, can be taught the nature of the sun by showing him the rays of the sun falling through the window. By equating the portion of light with the sun, one can then seek out its great orb of light. Thus in Paramātmā Sandarbha I will establish that jīva is an aṁśa of the Lord, existing as the Lord’s special śakti.**

**Sometimes the Upaniṣads teach that the jīva is an aṁśa, showing the Lord is endowed with aṁśas such as jīvas. But there are also Upaniṣads which teach Brahman without aṁśas, dedicated to describing the object itself, Brahman (apart from its descriptive elements).**

**The word** ***kaivalya*** **in the phrase** ***kaivalyaika-prayojanam*** **means simply “pure.” This ultimately means pure bhakti. This will be explained in Prīti Sandarbha. Sūta speaks.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Having confirmed the eternal nature of the highest entity, the author describes the relationship of the scriptures with Brahman possessing qualities. In the Lord’s statement in Chāndogya Upaniṣad, the word *anena* signifies that the jīva *(ātmā*) is different from the Lord. Here the word *ātmā* refers to the jīva who is an aṁśa of the supreme ātmā, the Lord’s separated aṁśa (*vibhinnāṁśa*), not a form like Matsya which is a svāṁśa.

Statements of extreme non-difference of the jīva from the Lord in statements of apposition (Eg. “”You are that.”) indicate similarity of type only, since the jīva is a form of consciousness like the Lord. In that sense, there is oneness of jīva with Brahman. The distinct differences are obvious. This is shown through examples.

Some statements in the Upaniṣads speak of the Lord with aṁśas. *So’kāmayata bahu syām*: the Lord desired, “May I be many.” There are also statements in the Upaniṣads showing the Lord without aṁśas. *Satyaṁ jñānam anantam:* Brahman is eternal existence and knowledge. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.1) *Niṣkalaṁ niṣkriyaṁ śāntaṁ niravadyaṁ nirañjanam:* the Lord is without parts, without action, peaceful, without fault, without contamination. (Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad 6.19) Śrutis which describe the Lord without aṁśas aim at defining the object apart from its descriptive elements. They speak of Brahman without revelation of its form and qualities.

### Text 53

*tatra yadi tvam-padārthaysa jīvātmano jñānatvaṁ nityatvaṁ ca prathamato vicāra-gocaraḥ syāt tadaiva tat-padārthasya tādṛśatvaṁ subodhaṁ syād iti \| tad bodhayitum anyārthaś ca parāmarśaḥ iti nyāyena jīvātmanas tad-rūpatvam āha \|*  
*nātmā jajāna na mariṣyati naidhate’sau*  
*na kṣīyate savanavid-vyabhicāriṇāṁ hi \|*  
*sarvatra śaśvad anapāyy upalabdhi-mātraṁ*  
*prāṇo yathendriya-balena vikalpitaṁ sat \|\|*  
*ātmā śuddho jīvaḥ \| na jajāna na jātaḥ \| janmābhāvād eva tad-anantarāstitā-lakṣaṇo vikāro’pi nāsti \| naidhate na vardhate \| vṛddhy-abhāvād eva vipariṇāmo’pi nirastaḥ \| hi yasmāt \| vyabhicāriṇām āgamāpāyināṁ bāla-yuvādi-dehānāṁ deva-manuṣyādy-ākāra-dehānāṁ vā \| savanavit tat-tat-kāla-draṣṭā \| nahy avasthāvatāṁ draṣṭā tad-avastho bhavatīty arthaḥ \| niravasthaḥ ko’sāv ātmā \| ata āha upalabdhi-mātraṁ jñānaika-rūpam \| kathambhūtam \| sarvatra dehe, śaśvat sarvadā anuvartamānam iti \|*  
*nanu nīla-jñānaṁ naṣṭaṁ pīta-jñānaṁ jātam iti pratīter na jñānasyānapāyitvam \| tatrāha indriya-baleneti \| sad eva jñānam ekam indriya-balena vividhaṁ kalpitam \| nīlādy-ākārā vṛttaya eva jāyante naśyanti ca na jñānam iti bhāvaḥ \| ayam āgamāpāyitadavadhi-bhedena prathamas tarkaḥ \| draṣṭṛ-dṛśya-bhedena dvitīyo’pi tarko jñeyaḥ \| vyabhicāriṣv avasthā-vyabhicāre dṛṣṭāntaḥ prāṇo yatheti \|\|53\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**If it is first concluded that** ***tvam*** **or the jīva is an eternal, conscious entity, one can then easily conclude that** ***tat,*** **the Lord is a similar eternal, conscious entity. For that reason, the jīva is identified with the Lord.** ***Anyārthaś ca parāmarśaḥ:*** **the jīva is mentioned for another purpose (not to equate it with the Lord). Brahma-sūtra 1.3.20**

**Its nature is expressed in the following.**

***nātmā jajāna na mariṣyati naidhate 'sau***  
***na kṣīyate savana-vid vyabhicāriṇāṁ hi***  
***sarvatra śaśvad anapāyy upalabdhi-mātraṁ***  
***prāṇo yathendriya-balena vikalpitaṁ sat***

**The jīva does not undergo birth, death, growth, or deterioration since he is the seer of all conditions of changing bodies. That jīva is knowledge alone, existing constantly in the body, but endowed with activity by the power of the senses, just as prāṇa is one but moves from body to body. SB 11.3.38**

**The pure jīva (*****ātmā*****) is not born (*****na jajāna*****). Since he is not born, he has no changes that take place after birth. He does not increase (*****naidhate*****). Since he has no increase, there is no transformation since (*****hi*****) he is the seer (*****savana-vit)*** **at all times of various bodies with changes of infancy, youth etc. (*****vyabhicārinām*****). This means that the seer of these conditions does not become the condition. What is this ātmā without these conditions? He is a form of knowledge alone (*****upalabdhi-mātram*****) existing in a body (*****sarvatra*****) at all times** ***(śaśvat*****).**

**“Knowledge of blue disappears and knowledge of yellow appears. With such perception, knowledge cannot be constant (*****anapāyi*****).” By the strength of the senses, one’s knowledge takes on variety. In this way the forms of blue and yellow, arising from functions of the senses, arise and disappear. That is not knowledge. The difference because of change (of body) and no change (jīva) is the first inference of the jīva and the Lord. The second inference is the difference between the seer and the seen. An example is give to show the unchanging state of the seer in changing situations (the seen). It is like prāṇa, which is one but moves to various bodies.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

It is said that if jīva is understood as ātmā, Paramātmā can be easily understood. For that purpose the author gives quotations while describing the jīva. He quotes from Brahma-sūtra. The dahara-vidyā in Chāndogya Upaniṣad says:

*yad idam asmin brahma-pure daharaṁ puṇḍarīkaṁ veśma daharo’sminn antar-ākāśas tasmin yad-antas tad anveṣṭavyam*

**In the city of Brahman there is a small lotus dwelling. That dwelling is small. In that place, there is an inner space. Within that space is that which should be sought. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.1.1**

The body of the worshipper is the city of Brahman. In that body one should meditate on Paramātmā in the space situated in the lotus of the heart. It is taught that one should seek out that form endowed with the eight qualities starting with freedom from sin. The eight qualities are freedom from sin, freedom from old age, freedom from death, freedom from sorrow, freedom from hunger, freedom from thirst, satya-kāma and satya-saṅkalpa. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.7.3

25 This is the conclusion. In that section there is also the following:

*evam evaiṣa samprasādo 'smāt śarīrāt samutthāya paraṁ jyotir upasampadya svena rūpeṇābhiniṣpadyate sa uttamaḥ puruṣaḥ.*

**Receiving realization, rising from his body, he attains the supreme light and manifests with his own form. The Lord is the supreme person. Chāndogya Upaniṣad 8.12.3**

Receiving realization (*samprasādaḥ*), the jīva attains the supreme light, which is the supreme person *(uttamaḥ puruṣaḥ*). Why is the jīva discussed in dahara-vidyā? Brahma- sūtra 1.3.20 says, “Jīva is discussed for another purpose.” The jīva, attaining Paramātmā, attains his svarūpa for the purpose of knowing Paramātmā (*sa uttamaḥ puruṣaḥ).*

There are six transformations: birth, existence, growth, transformation, decline and death.These do not exist in the jīva.

“The jīva is a form of knowledge. But knowledge becomes blue or yellow. It is thus not eternal.” The jīva is also a knower. This is like the sun which is self-revealing object which reveals other things. Knowledge itself is eternal since it is related to the svarūpa of the jīva. Perception of blue takes place through the senses and prāṇas. The functions, senses contacting objects, taking the form of blue etc. appear and then disappear.

### Text 54

*dṛṣṭāntaṁ vivṛṇvann indriyādilayena nirvikārātmopalabdhiṁ darśayati --*  
*aṇḍeṣu peśiṣu taruṣv aviniściteṣu*  
*prāṇo hi jīvam upadhāvati tatra tatra \|*  
*sanne yad indriya-gaṇe’hami ca prasupte*  
*kūṭastha āśayam ṛte tad anusmṛtir naḥ \|\|*  
*aṇḍeṣu aṇḍajeṣu \| peśiṣu jarāyujeṣu \| taruṣu udbhijjeṣu \| aviniściteṣu svedajeṣu \| upadhāvati anuvartate \| evaṁ dṛṣṭānte nirvikāratvaṁ pradarśya dārṣṭāntike’pi darśayati \| katham \| tadaivātmā savikāra iva pratīyate yadā jāgare indriya-gaṇaḥ \| yadā ca svapne tat-saṁskāravān ahaṅkāraḥ \| yadā tu prasuptaṁ tadā tasmin prasupta indriya-gaṇe sanne līne \| ahami anaṅkāre ca sanne līne \| kūṭastho nirvikāra evātmā \| kutaḥ \| āśayam ṛte liṅga-śarīram upādhiṁ vinā \| vikāra-hetor upādher abhāvāt ity arthaḥ \|*  
*nanv ahaṅkāra-paryantasya sarvasya laye śūnyam evāvaśiṣyate \| kva tadā kūṭastha ātmā \| ata āha tad-anusmṛtir naḥ \| tasyākhaṇḍātmanaḥ suṣpti-sākṣiṇaḥ smṛtiḥ naḥ asmākaṁ jāgrad-draṣṭṝṇāṁ jāyate etāvantaṁ kālaṁ sukham aham asvāpsaṁ na kiñcid avediṣam iti \| ato’nanubhūtasya tasyāsmaraṇād asty eva suṣptau tādrg-ātmānubhavaḥ \| viṣaya-sambandhābhāvāc ca na spaṣṭa iti bhāvaḥ \| ataḥ sva-prakāśa-mātra-vastunaḥ sūryādeḥ prakāśavad upalabhdi-mātrasyāpy ātmana upalabhdiḥ svāśraye’sty evety āyātam \| tathā ca śrutiḥ -- yad vai tan na paśyati paśyan vai draṣṭavyān na paśyati, na hi draṣṭur dṛṣṭorviparilopo vidyate iti \|*  
*ayaṁ sākṣi-sākṣya-vibhāgena tṛtīyas tarkaḥ \| duḥkhi-premāspadatva-vibhāgena caturtho’pi tarko’vagantavyaḥ \|\|54\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Giving an example, the realization of the unchanging ātmā with destruction of the sense functions is shown.**

***aṇḍeṣu peśiṣu taruṣv aviniściteṣu***  
***prāṇo hi jīvam upadhāvati tatra tatra***  
***sanne yad indriya-gaṇe 'hami ca prasupte***  
***kūṭa-stha āśayam ṛte tad-anusmṛtir naḥ***

**Prāṇa follows the jīva in bodies born from eggs, wombs, seeds and perspiration. When the senses and ahaṅkāra cease to function in deep sleep, the fixed ātmā alone remains, without the covering of the subtle body, but with memory of the experience of deep sleep. SB 11.3.39**

**Prāṇa follows the jīva in bodies born from eggs (*****aṇḍeṣu),*** **wombs** ***(peśiṣu),*** **seeds** ***(taruṣu*****) and perspiration (*****aviniściteṣu*****). In this example the unchanging nature of the ātmā is shown in various bodies. Then the unchanging nature in one body is shown. The ātmā seems to change when (*****yat*****) the senses are active in the waking state. When in a dreaming condition the ahaṅkāra remains active, holding impressions of the senses. When in deep sleep, with the senses and ahaṅkāra** ***(ahami*****) merged (*****sanne*****) in the ātmā, the ātmā remains unchanged (*****kūṭasthaḥ*****). Why? It is devoid of upādhis of the subtle body (*****āśayam ṛte):*** **the cause of change, the upādhis, is absent, having been dissolved.**

**“When ahaṅkāra and senses stop functioning, what remains is a state of void. How is the unchanging ātmā present at that time?” The memory or witness** ***(smṛtiḥ*****) of deep sleep of the indivisible ātmā (*****tat*****) arises for us (*****naḥ),*** **the seers, in the waking state. We think, “For that time I slept and was not aware of anything.” There is this awareness of ātmā in deep sleep because of not remembering any particular experience. It is not clear awareness since there is lack of relationship with objects. Like the manifestation of self-revealing objects like the sun, this awareness of the ātmā, being simply awareness of the self, takes shelter of itself.**

***yad vai tan na paśyati paśyan vai draṣṭavyān na paśyati, na hi draṣṭur dṛṣṭor viparilopo vidyate***

**He certainly does not see. Although seeing, he does not see what is to be seen. The seeing capacity of the seer is not lost. Bṛhad-āraṇyaka Upaniṣad 4.3.23**

**The third inference for ātmā is the difference between the witness and what is witnessed (seen in the above example). The fourth inference for ātmā can be understood to be the difference between the sufferer (because of attachment to the body) and the shelter of prema (experienced apart from the body). Prema is represented by “I slept happily.” 26**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In the example, the prāṇa does not change since it remains in one form in various bodies. When in deep sleep, with the senses and ahaṅkāra *(ahami*) merged (*sanne*) in the ātmā, the ātmā remains unchanged (*kūṭasthaḥ*). Absence of upādhis means that they have been dissolved. However liberation does not take place in deep sleep because of the existence of very subtle impressions. When the material ahaṅkāra dissolves, the sense of “I “related to the svarūpa of the jīva remains. Because of this, the jīva thinks, “I slept happily.” That is explained by raising the doubt “If ahaṅkāra is dissolved only void remains.” This means “There can be no perception of ātmā because the sense of “I” is absent (ahaṅkāra being destroyed.)” The ātmā is described as indivisible (*akhaṇḍa*) because, being very small, it cannot be divided.

“Because of the combination with ahaṅkāra which appears when rising from sleep, one thinks ‘I slept happily’ in the waking state. The ātmā is merely consciousness during deep sleep.” The person does not remember because he does not have experience of objects. Memory and experience are equated. Therefore ātmā experiences this. “Ātmā is awareness only. How can you say that ātmā is aware of something?” The awareness takes shelter of the ātmā. The Upaniṣad says, “The ātmā does not see.” In deep sleep it does not see, because of absence of objects to be seen, and not because it is not a seer. The rest is clear.

### Text 55

*tad uktaṁ –*  
*anvaya-vyatirekākhyas tarkaḥ syāc caturātmakaḥ \|*  
*āgamāpāyitadavadhi-bhedena prathamo mataḥ \|\|*  
*draṣṭṛ-dṛśya-vibhāgena dvitīyo’pi matas tathā \|*  
*sākṣi-sākṣya-vibhāgena tṛtīyaḥ sammataḥ satām \|\|*  
*duḥkhi-premāspadatvena caturthaḥ sukha-bodhakaḥ \|\|*  
*\|\|11.3\|\| iti śrī-pippalāyano nimim \|\|55\|\|*

**Перевод:**

***anvaya-vyatirekākhyas tarkaḥ syāc caturātmakaḥ \|***  
***āgamāpāyi-tad avadhi-bhedena prathamo mataḥ \|\|***  
***draṣṭṛ-dṛśya-vibhāgena dvitīyo’pi matas tathā \|***  
***sākṣi-sākṣya-vibhāgena tṛtīyaḥ sammataḥ satām \|\|***  
***duḥkhi-premāspadatvena caturthaḥ sukha-bodhakaḥ \|\|***

**There are four inferences in terms of positive and negative conclusions (ātmā is puruṣārtha, body is not puruṣārtha). The first inference for ātmā is by seeing the difference between the temporary and permanent. The second inference is through the difference between the seer and the seen. The third inference is through the difference between the witness and the witnessed. The fourth inference which gives awareness of happiness is through the difference between the suffering jīva and the jīva as the shelter of prema.**

**Pippalāyana speaks to Nimi.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In expḷaining the two Bhāgavatam verses, four inferences are made. These are summarized in six lines. *Tarka* refers to anumāna which is an aṅga of *tarka*. Because the body comes and go, because the body is seen, because the body is witnessed, because the body is the seat of suffering, it can be concluded that the ātmā is separate from the body. Because the ātmā remains, because it sees, because it is the witness, and because it is the abode of prema, step by step, it may be inferred that the ātmā exists. This can be expressed negatively (as *vyatireka*) as well. Because the body changes and the ātmā does not change, the body cannot be the puruṣārtha. Another vyatireka is “If ātmā did not exist, there would be no witness or seer of the body.”27

### Text 56

*evambhūtānāṁ jīvānāṁ cin-mātraṁ yat svarūpaṁ tayaivākṛtyā tad-aṁśitvena ca tad-abhinnaṁ yat tattvaṁ tad atra vācyam iti vyaṣṭi-nirdeśa-dvārā proktam \| tad eva hy āśraya-saṁjñakaṁ mahāpurāṇa-lakṣaṇa-rūpaiḥ sargādibhir arthaiḥ samaṣṭi-nirdeśa-dvārāpi lakṣyata ity atrāha dvābhyām --*  
*atra sargo visargaś ca sthānaṁ poṣaṇam ūtayaḥ \|*  
*manvantareśānukathā nirodho muktir āśrayaḥ \|\|*  
*daśamasya viśuddhy-arthaṁ navānām iha lakṣaṇam \|*  
*varṇayanti mahātmānaḥ śrutenārthena cāñjasā \|\|*  
*manvantarāṇi ceśānukathāś ca manvantareśānukathāḥ \| atra sargādayo daśārthā lakṣyanta ity arthaḥ \| tatra ca daśamasya viśuddhy-arthaṁ tattva-jñānārthaṁ navānāṁ lakṣaṇaṁ svarūpaṁ varṇayanti \| nanv atra naivaṁ pratīyate \| ata āha śrutena śrutyā kaṇṭhoktyaiva stutayādi-sthāneṣu, añjasā sākṣād varṇayanti \| arthena tātparya-vṛttyā ca tat-tad-ākhyāneṣu \|\|56\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The nature of this jīva is pure consciousness** ***(cinmātram*****). That tattva which is non-different from the Lord because of being a similar conscious knower and because of being the Lord’s aṁśa is described here. That is described in terms of the individual jīva’s condition (*****vyasti*****).**

**What is defined as the shelter or** ***āśraya*** **is described through the ten characteristics of a Mahā-purāṇa, indicating the complete conscious being (*****samasti*****) in two verses:**

***atra sargo visargaś ca sthānaṁ poṣaṇam ūtayaḥ \|***  
***manvantareśānukathā nirodho muktir āśrayaḥ \|\|***

**In this Purāṇa there are ten topics: creation, secondary creation, protection, mercy of the Lord, material activities, the conduct of the Manus*****,*** **stories of the Lord, destruction of the universe, liberation and the ultimate shelter.**

***daśamasya viśuddhy-arthaṁ navānām iha lakṣaṇam \|***  
***varṇayanti mahātmānaḥ śrutenārthena cāñjasā \|\|***

**The great devotees such as Vidura and Maitreya describe properly the nine topics in order to impart the highest knowledge of the tenth topic, through the words of the scripture and stories to illustrate their meaning. SB 2.10.1-2**

***Manvantara*** **and** ***īśānukathā*** **are two separate items. Ten elements starting with** ***sarga*** **(primary creation) are described. In that list, in order to attain knowledge of tattva (*****daśamasya viśuddhy-artham*****) the great devotees describe the svarūpas (*****lakṣaṇam*****) of the other nine (*****navānām).*** **“But that tenth topic is not perceived in this list.” The devotees describe the nine to indicate the supreme tattva directly** ***(añjasā*****) by various verses of praise** ***(śrutena*****) and by purports of various stories** ***(arthena*****).**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In order to explain knowledge of the Lord, knowledge of the svarūpa of jīva has been explained. Similarly, one can use similar inferences to determine the svarūpa of the Lord. It was previously concluded that the svarūpa of the jīva is consiousness and having awareness of other objects. The jīva is considered non-different by being of the same type, a conscious knower (*tayā ākṛtyā)* and by being an aṁśa, jīvāṁśa rather than svāṁśa*. Ākṛtis tu striyāṁ rūpe sāmānya-vapuṣor api: ākṛti* means women, form, same type, and body. (Medinī)

In that sense, jīva is non-different from Brahman tattva. The aṁśa is not separate from the aṁśī. That is the meaning of *vyaṣṭi*. Brahman or the Lord who possesses jīva as his śakti is the *samaṣṭi. Vyaṣṭi* is the jīva. By defining Brahman as the *samaṣṭi* possessing śakti, Brahman’s real nature is truthfully indicated.

The tenth topic is the Lord. The rest of the verse is clear.

### Text 57

*tam eva daśamaṁ vispaṣṭayituṁ teṣāṁ daśānāṁ vyutpādikāṁ sapta-ślokīm āha –-*  
*bhūta-mātrendriya-dhiyāṁ janma sarga udāhṛtaḥ*  
*brahmaṇo guṇa-vaiṣamyād visargaḥ pauruṣaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|*  
*bhūtāni khādīni \| mātrāṇi ca śabdādīni indriyāṇi ca \| dhī-śabdena mahad-ahaṅkārau \| guṇānāṁ vaiṣamyāt pariṇāmāt \| brahmaṇaḥ parameśvarāt kartṛ-bhūtādīnāṁ janma sargaḥ \| puruṣo vairājo brahmā, tat-kṛtaḥ pauruṣaś carācara-sargo visarga ity arthaḥ \|*  
*sthitir vaikuṇṭha-vijayaḥ poṣaṇaṁ tad-anugrahaḥ*  
*manvantarāṇi sad-dharma ūtayaḥ karma-vāsanāḥ*  
*avatārānucaritaṁ hareś cāsyānuvartinām*  
*puṁsām īśa-kathāḥ proktā nānākhyānopabṛṁhitāḥ \|\|*  
*vaikuṇṭhasya bhagavato vijayaḥ sṛṣṭānāṁ tat-tan-maryādā-pālanenotkarṣaḥ \| sthitiḥ sthānam \| tataḥ sthiteṣu svabhakteṣu tasyānugrahaḥ poṣaṇam \| manvantarāṇi tat-tan-manvantara-sthitānāṁ manvādīnāṁ tad-anugṛhītānāṁ satāṁ caritāni, tāny eva dharmas tad-upāsanākhyaḥ sad-dharmaḥ \| tatraiva sthitau nānā-karma-vāsanā ūtayaḥ \| sthitāv eva harer avatārānucaritam asyānuvartināṁ ca kathā īśānukathāḥ proktā ity arthaḥ \|*  
*nirodho 'syānuśayanam ātmanaḥ saha śaktibhiḥ \|*  
*muktir hitvānyathā rūpaṁ sva-rūpeṇa vyavasthitiḥ \|\|*  
*sthity-anantaraṁ cātmano jīvasya śaktibhiḥ svopādhibhiḥ sahāsya harer anuśayanaṁ, hari-śayanānugatatvena śayanaṁ nirodha ity arthaḥ \| tatra hareḥ śayanaṁ prapañcaṁ prati dṛṣṭi-nimīlanam \| jīvānāṁ śayanaṁ tatra laya iti jñeyam \| tatraiva nirodhe’nyathārūpam avidyādhyastam ajñatvādikaṁ hitvā svarūpeṇa vyavasthitir muktiḥ \|\|57\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**In order to make the tenth topic clear, seven verses are spoken to give the meaning of the ten topics.**

***bhūta-mātrendriya-dhiyāṁ janma sarga udāhṛtaḥ \|***  
***brahmaṇo guṇa-vaiṣamyād visargaḥ pauruṣaḥ smṛtaḥ \|\|***

***Sarga*** **refers to the manifestation of the gross material elements, the tan-mātrās, the senses, the false ego and mahat-tattva, caused when the Lord transforms the guṇas. Visarga is the creation of bodies for the jīvas carried out by Brahmā. SB 2.10.3**

**Sarga is the creation of gross elements like ether** ***(bhūta*****), senses objects** ***(mātra*****), senses** ***(indriya),*** **ahaṅkāra and mahat-tattva (*****dhiyām*****) caused by the Lord (*****brahmaṇaḥ*****) through transforming the guṇas (*****guṇa-vaiṣamyāt).*** **Visarga, the creation of moving and non-moving beings, is done by Brahmā (*****pauruṣaḥ*****).**

***sthitir vaikuṇṭha-vijayaḥ poṣaṇaṁ tad-anugrahaḥ \|***  
***manvantarāṇi sad-dharma ūtayaḥ karma-vāsanāḥ \|\|***

***Sthānam*** **refers to the Lord’ excellence in situating the living beings within the universe.** ***Poṣaṇam*** **means the Lord’s mercy.** ***Manvantara*** **refers to the conduct of the kings ruling the** ***manvantara*** **periods.** ***Ūti*** **refers to the impressions created by the jīva’s actions which lead them to future births. SB 2.10.4**

***Sthiti*** **or place for the living beings is the expertise (*****vijayaḥ*****) of the Lord (*****vaikuṇṭhasya*****) in maintaining the boundaries of what was created.** ***Poṣaṇam*** **is mercy to devotees in those places. The** ***manvantara*** **means the activities involving worship (*****sad-dharmaḥ*****) performed by Manus and the devotees who received their mercy while living during the Manvantara periods.** ***Utayaḥ*** **refers to the various impressions of karma during this time.**

***avatārānucaritaṁ hareś cāsyānuvartinām \|***  
***puṁsām īśa-kathāḥ proktā nānākhyānopabṛṁhitāḥ \|\|***

***Īśa-kathā*** **refers to descriptions of the Lord’s** ***avatāras*** **and their devotees, filled with various stories. SB 2.10.5**

***Īśānukathaḥ*** **means stories about the avatāras of the Lord and stories about the followers of the Lord.**

***nirodho ’syānuśayanam ātmanaḥ saha śaktibhiḥ \|***  
***muktir hitvānyathā rūpaṁ sva-rūpeṇa vyavasthitiḥ \|\|***

***Nirodha*** **refers to the merging of the jīva along with his material identity into the Lord at the time of final devastation of the universe.** ***Mukti*** **means the jīva’s attainment of his form as pure ātmā after giving up gross and subtle material bodies. SB 2.10.6**

***Nirodha*** **means sleeping of the jīva (*****ātmanaḥ*****) with his upādhis** ***(śaktibhiḥ*****) along with the Lord’s (*****asya*****) sleep (*****anuśayanam)*** **at the end of the maintainance period. The sleep of the Lord means he closes his eyes to the material world. The sleeping of the jīva means merging into the Lord. Liberation, which is in the category of** ***nirodha, Absolute destruction of upādhis of the jīva (liberation) is the fourth type of nirodha.28*** **is being situated with one’s svarūpa after giving up the covering of ignorance (*****anyathā-rūpam)*****.**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The ten topics starting with sarga are defined. *Brahmaṇo guṇa-vaiṣamyāt* means “from the disturbance of the guṇas caused by the Lord.” This means that the Lord is the original creator of the universe from its most sublte cause (prakṛti). Brahmā is the creator using the effects (material elements).

Giving up (*hitvā)* the false form (*anyathā-rūpam*)-- that of a devatā or human produced by avidyā, corresponding to aversion to the Lord--destroying it by bhakti, which follows from favorability to the Lord, a person becomes situated in a special way (*vyavasthitiḥ*)--near the Lord, with no return to birth and death. He becomes situated in the svarūpa of the jīva*(svarūpeṇa*), endowed with the eight qualities starting with absence of sin.

### Text 58

*ābhāsaś ca nirodhaś ca yato 'sty adhyavasīyate*  
*sa āśrayaḥ paraṁ brahma paramātmeti śabdyate \|*  
*ābhāsaḥ sṛṣṭiḥ \| nirodho layaś ca yato bhavati \| adhyavasīyata upalabhyate jīvānāṁ jñānendriyeṣu prakāśate ca \| sa brahmeti paramātmeti prasiddha āśrayaḥ kathyate \| iti śabdaḥ prakārārthaḥ \| tena bhagavān iti ca \| asya vivṛtir agre vidheyā \|\|58\|\|*

**Перевод:**

***ābhāsaś ca nirodhaś ca yato ’sty adhyavasīyate \|***  
***sa āśrayaḥ paraṁ brahma paramātmeti śabdyate \|\|***

**The** ***āśray*****a, Bhagavān, from whom arise creation, sub-creation, protection, mercy, karma, dharma of the kings, the appearance of avatāras, destruction and liberation is also called Paramātmā and Brahman.** **SB 2.10.7**

**The famous** ***āśraya*** **is called Brahman and Paramātmā from whom creation (*****ābhāsaḥ)*** **and destruction (*****nirodhaḥ*****) appear (*****adhyavasīyate*****) in the knowledge senses of the jīvas. The word** ***iti*** **indicates “these types” and thus indicates Bhagavān as well. The āśraya will be be more explicity explained in the next verses.**

**Комментарий. Автор :**

The *āśraya-tattva,* characterized by the nine beginning with sarga, is described. *Yataḥ* means cause, “from which.”

### Text 59

*sthitau ca tatrāśraya-svarūpam aparokṣānubhavena vyaṣṭi-dvārāpi spaṣṭaṁ darśayitum adhyātmādi-vibhāgam āha –*  
*yo 'dhyātmiko 'yaṁ puruṣaḥ so 'sāv evādhidaivikaḥ \|*  
*yas tatrobhaya-vicchedaḥ puruṣo hy ādhibhautikaḥ \|\|*  
*ekam ekatarābhāve yadā nopalabhāmahe \|*  
*tritayaṁ tatra yo veda sa ātmā svāśrayāśrayaḥ \|\|*  
*yo’yam ādhyātmikaḥ puruṣaś cakṣur-ādi-karaṇābhimānī draṣṭā jīvaḥ \| sa evādhidaivikaś cakṣur ādy-adhiṣṭhātā sūryādiḥ \| deha-sṛṣṭeḥ pūrvaṁ karaṇānāām adhiṣṭhānābhāvenākṣamatayā karaṇa-prakāśa-kartṛtvābhimāni-tat-sahāyayor ubhayor api tayor vṛtti-bhedānudayena jīvatva-mātrāviśeṣāt \| tataś cobhayaḥ karaṇābhimāni-tad-adhiṣṭhātṛ-devatā-rūpo dvirūpo vicchedo yasmāt \| sa ādhibhautikaś cakṣur golakādy-upalakṣito dṛśyo dehaḥ puruṣa iti puruṣasya jīvasyopādhiḥ \| sa vā eṣa puruṣo’nna-rasa-mayaḥ ity ādi śruteḥ \|\|59\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Divisions of sense perception are mentioned in order to show clearly the svarūpa of the āśraya through direct realization by means of the individual (*****vyaṣṭi*****): These two verses follow the description of the ten topics in the Second Canto.29**

***yo ’dhyātmiko ’yaṁ puruṣaḥ so ’sāv evādhidaivikaḥ \|***  
***yas tatrobhaya-vicchedaḥ puruṣo hy ādhibhautikaḥ \|\|***

**The covering on the jīva known as the subtle sense organ is not distinguished from the presiding deities of the senses. The gross organ of the material body then arises, because of which the distinction between sense organ and sense deity arises.**

**The seer, the jīva, who identifies with the senses such as the eye (*****adhyātmikaḥ puruṣaḥ*****) is also the presiding deity, such as the sun for the eye. There is no distinction between the sense and the deity before the creation of the material body. At that time the senses cannot act because of lack of a support. The functions of contacting objects caused by the sense and making the sense active, caused by the devatā, are absent. The jīva exists without differentiation between the senses and their deities.**

**The body (*****puruṣaḥ*** **) which is seen, the covering on the jīva, which is represented by the gross eye** ***(ādhibhautikaḥ*****) appears, because of which (*****yat*****) the senses and the controlling deities become distinct.** ***Sa vā eṣa puruṣo’nna-rasa-mayaḥ*****: that body is made of food and rasa. (Taittirīya Upaniṣad 2.1)**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

“How does the sun deity instill action in the senses of the jīva who identifies with the senses?” Before the creation of the body, the senses have no ability to act because they have no support. For instance the eye (which is subtle) does not have the eye ball. The jīva exists without differentiation because the functions of senses contacing objects, belonging to the sense, and instigating the senses, caused by the sense deity (*karaṇābhimānī*), have not manifested because of lack of a gross body. This means that before the creation of the body, the senses and the devatās exist along with the jīva. Since they do not function, they are included in the jīva. When the body appears, they differentiate from the jīva (with their functions). That is described.

### Text 60

*ekam ekatarābhāva ity eṣām anyonya-sāpekṣa-siddhatve nānāśrayatvaṁ darśayati \| tathā hi dṛśyaṁ vinā tat-pratīty-anumeyaṁ karaṇaṁ na sidhyati \| nāpi draṣṭā na ca tad vinā karaṇa-pravṛtty-anumeyas tad-adhiṣṭhātā sūryādiḥ \| na ca taṁ vinā karaṇaṁ pravartate \| na ca tad vinā dṛyam ity ekatarasyābhāva ekaṁ nopalabhāmahe \| tatra tadā tat-tritayam ālocanātmakena pratyayena \| yo veda sākṣitayā paśyati sa paramātmā āśrayaḥ \| teṣām api parasparam āśrayatvam astīti tad-vyavacchedārthaṁ viśeṣaṇaṁ svāśrayo’nanyāśrayaḥ \| sa cāsāv anyeṣām āśrayaś ceti \| tatrāṁśāṁśinoḥ śuddha-jīva-paramātmanor abhedāṁśa-svīkāreṇaivāśraya uktaḥ \| ataḥ paro’pi manute’nartham iti \|*  
*jāgrat-svapna-suṣuptaṁ ca guṇato buddhi-vṛttayaḥ \|*  
*tāsāṁ vilakṣaṇo jīvaḥ sākṣitvena vivakṣitaḥ \|\|iti \|*  
*śuddho vicaṣṭe hy aviśuddha-kartuḥ ity ādy uktasya sākṣi-saṁjñinaḥ śuddha-jīvasyāśrayatvaṁ na śaṅkanīyam \| athavā nanv ādhyātmikādīnām apy āśrayatvam asty eva \| satyam \| tathāpi parasparāśrayatvān na tatrāśrayatākaivalyam iti te tv āśraya-śabdena mukhyatayā nocyanta ity āha ekam iti \| tarhi sākṣiṇa evāstām āśrayatvam \| tatrāha tritayam iti \| sa ātmā sākṣī jīvas tu yaḥ svāśrayo’nanyāśrayaḥ paramātmā sa evāśrayo yasya tathābhūta iti \| vakṣyate ca haṁsa-guhya-stave sarvaṁ pumān veda guṇāṁś ca taj-jño na veda sarvajñam anantam īḍe iti \| tasmāt ābhāsaś ca ity ādinoktaḥ paramātmaivāśraya iti \|\|2.10\|\| śrī-śukaḥ \|\|60\|\|*

**Перевод:**

***ekam ekatarābhāve yadā nopalabhāmahe \|***  
***tritayaṁ tatra yo veda sa ātmā svāśrayāśrayaḥ \|\|***

**Since we cannot perceive one item in absence of one of the other items (subtle sense, sense deity and gross form), he who is independent of these three, is the ātmā. But the shelter of the ātmā is the Paramātmā. SB 2.10.8-9**

**One depends on the other. In order for each to function, they depend on each other. Without the object to be seen (adhibhautika, the gross organ or body), the sense (adhyātmika) which observes it cannot function. Without the sense, the deity (adhidaiva) which should produce the action of the sense cannot function. Without the deity, the sense does not function. Without the sense, the object to be seen has no function. If any one of these is absent, we cannot perceive the other. He who knows these three by ascertainment, who sees as the witness, is the shelter. That is Paramātmā. The three take shelter of each other. In order to distinguish Paramātmā he is described as taking shelter of himself (*****svāśrayaḥ*****). He is thus the shelter of all three. The jīva is also described as a shelter by accepting non-difference of the pure jīva and Paramātmā in the sense of the aṁśa and aṁśī.**

**With that in mind, there are verses like the following showing jīva as the shelter:**

***yayā sammohito jīva ātmānaṁ tri-guṇātmakam \|***  
***paro ’pi manute ’narthaṁ tat-kṛtaṁ cābhipadyate \|\|***

**Bewildered by that māyā, the jīva, though separate from the three guṇas, considers himself made of the three guṇas and takes on material existence created by the guṇas. SB 1.7.5**

***jāgrat svapnaḥ suṣuptaṁ ca guṇato buddhi-vṛttayaḥ***  
***tāsāṁ vilakṣaṇo jīvaḥ sākṣitvena viniścitaḥ***

**Waking, sleeping and deep sleep, the three functions of the intelligence, are caused by the three guṇas. The jīva is ascertained to be different from these three states since it is only the witness of them. SB 11.13.27**

**It is also said:**

***kṣetrajña etā manaso vibhūtīr***  
***jīvasya māyā-racitasya nityāḥ***  
***āvirhitāḥ kvāpi tirohitāś ca***  
***śuddho vicaṣṭe hy aviśuddha-kartuḥ***

**The liberated jīva, free of saṁsāra*****,*** **impartially sees the transformations of the covering on the jīva known as the mind, which performs actions opposed to the Lord and which is made of māyā. These transformations are without beginning, sometimes visible and sometimes invisible. SB 5.11.12**

**(In view of SB 2.10.9) from the verse above (SB 5.11.12) one should not think that the pure jīva is the ultimate witness and the shelter.**

**Or an alternative meaning is this. “But the jīva is the shelter of the senses, devatās and objects.” That is true. Since the three mutually take shelter of each other, they are not taking shelter in the real sense of the term. They cannot be called “shelter” by the chief meaning of the word. Thus the next verse says that one element does not function in the absence of the others. One is not really a shelter for the other two. Therefore the witness of these three (the jīva) is the real shelter. He who knows these three is the ātmā, the witness, the jīva, who has no other shelter** ***(svāsrayaḥ*****). This means he must take shelter of Paramātmā.**

***deho 'savo 'kṣā manavo bhūta-mātrām***  
***ātmānam anyaṁ ca viduḥ paraṁ yat***  
***sarvaṁ pumān veda guṇāṁś ca taj-jño***  
***na veda sarva-jñam anantam īḍe***

**The body, life airs, senses, internal senses, gross elements and sense objects do not know themselves or other things or the jīva. The jīva knows all of these items and the guṇas which cause them. He also can know Paramātmā, but does not really know the omniscient Lord. I worship that Lord with infinite qualities. SB 6.4.25**

**Therefore Paramātmā is the shelter as was already stated:**

***ābhāsaś ca nirodhaś ca yato ’sty adhyavasīyate \|***  
***sa āśrayaḥ paraṁ brahma paramātmeti śabdyate \|\|***

**The** ***āśray*****a, from whom arise creation, sub-creation, protection, mercy,** ***karma, dharma*** **of the kings, the appearance of avatāras*****,*** **destruction and liberation is also called Paramātmā and Brahman.** **SB 2.10.7**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

Because the three--sense, sense deity and gross organ--perform their functions with mutual dependence, they cannot be the shelter of each other. That is explained. He who knows the three (sense, sense deity and gross organ) is the shelter. “Since the pure jīva is called the witness of the senses and body, it functions independently. Why do you not say the jīva is the shelter of the three?” The jīva is the aṁśa and Paramātmā is the aṁśī. Both are accepted as the shelter.

Not satisfied, another meaning is given. Let the jīva be the shelter. In SB 6.4.25, *pumān* means the jīva. The jīva knows all these and the guṇas (*sarvaṁ pumān veda guṇāṁś ca taj-jñaḥ*).

### Text 61

*asya śrī-bhāgavatasya mahā-purāṇatva-vyañjaka-lakṣaṇaṁ prakārāntareṇa ca vadann api tasyaivāśrayatvam āha dvayena –*  
*sargo’syātha visargaś ca vṛttī rakṣāntarāṇi ca \|*  
*vaṁśo vaṁśānucaritaṁ saṁsthā hetur apāśrayaḥ \|\|*  
*daśabhir lakṣaṇair yuktaṁ purāṇaṁ tad-vido viduḥ \|*  
*kecit pañca-vidhaṁ brahman mahad-alpa-vyavasthayā \|\|*  
*antarāṇi manvantarāṇi \| pañca-vidham –*  
*sargaś ca pratisargaś ca vaṁśo manvantarāṇi ca \|*  
*vaṁśānucaritaṁ ceti purāṇaṁ pañca-lakṣaṇam \|\| iti kecid vadanti \|*  
*sa ca matabhedo mahad-alpa-vyavasthayā mahā-purāṇam alpa-purāṇam iti bhinnādhikaraṇatvena \| yadyapi viṣṇu-purāṇādāv api daśāpi tāni lakṣyante \| tathāpi pañcānām eva prādhānyenoktatvād alpatvam \| atra daśānām arthānāṁ skandheṣu yathākramaṁ praveśo na vivakṣitaḥ \| teṣām dvādaśa-saṅkhyatvāt \| dvitīya-skandhoktānāṁ teṣāṁ tṛtīyādiṣ yathā-saṅkhyaṁ na samāveśaḥ \| nirodhādīnāṁ daśamādiṣv aṣṭama-varjam \| anyeṣām apy anyeṣu yathokta-lakṣaṇatayā samāveśanāśakyatvād eva \| tad uktaṁ śrī-svāmibhir eva –*  
*daśame kṛṣṇa-sat-kīrti-vitānāyopavarṇyate \|*  
*dharma-glāni-nimittas tu nirodho duṣṭa-bhūbhujām \|\| iti \|*  
*prākṛtādi-caturdhā yo nirodhaḥ sa tu varṇitaḥ \| iti \|*  
*ato’tra skandhe śrī-kṛṣṇa-rūpasyāśrayasyaiva varṇana-prādhānyaṁ tair vivakṣitam \| uktaṁ ca svayam eva – daśame daśamaṁ lakṣyam āśritāśraya-vigraham iti \| evam anyatrāpy unneyam \| ataḥ prāyaśaḥ sarve’rthāḥ sarveṣv eva skandheṣu guṇatvena vā mukhyatvena vā nirūpyanta ity eva teṣām abhimatam \| śrutenārthena cāñjasā ity atra ca tathaiva pratipannaṁ sarvatra tat-tat-sambhavāt \| tataś ca parthama-dvitīyayor api mahā-purāṇatāyāṁ praveśaḥ syāt \| tasmāt kramo na gṛhītaḥ \|\|61\|\|*

**Перевод:**

**Speaking of the Bhāgavatam’s characteristics as a Mahāpurāṇa in another way, A slightly different list of the ten characteristics of a Mahā-purāṇa is given in the Twelfth Canto.30 it is stated that the Lord (not the jīva) is the shelter or āśraya:**

***sargo 'syātha visargaś ca vṛtti-rakṣāntarāṇi ca***  
***vaṁśo vaṁśānucarītaṁ saṁsthā hetur apāśrayaḥ***  
***daśabhir lakṣaṇair yuktaṁ purāṇaṁ tad-vido viduḥ***  
***kecit pañca-vidhaṁ brahman mahad-alpa-vyavasthayā***

**O brāhmaṇa! Authorities on the matter understand a Purāṇa to contain ten characteristic topics: the creation of this universe, the subsequent creation of worlds and beings, the maintenance of all living beings, their sustenance, the rule of various Manus, the dynasties of great kings, the activities of such kings, annihilation, the jīva and the supreme shelter. Other scholars state that the great Purāṇas deal with these ten topics, while lesser Purāṇas may deal with five. SB 12.7.9-10**

***Antarāṇi*** **means Manvantaras. Also there are five characteristics according to some:**

***sargaś ca pratisargaś ca vaṁśo manvantarāṇi ca \|***  
***vaṁśānucaritaṁ ceti purāṇaṁ pañca-lakṣaṇam \|\|***

**The five characteristics of a Purāṇa are sarga, pratisarga, vaṁśa, manvantaras and vaṁśanucaritam.**

**The different opinion (five characteristic instead of ten) is based on the distinction between major and minor Purāṇas, whether they are voluminous or small. Though Viṣṇu Purāṇa and others have ten characteristics, some are said to be minor because of the prominence of the five characteristics.**

**This does not mean that the ten subjects are discussed in order in the different cantos of Bhāgavatam since Bhāgavatam has Twelve Cantos. Since the ten topics are mentioned in the Second Canto, even if one counts from the Third Canto to the Twelfth Canto to make ten topics, the topics does not match the contents. For instance destruction** ***(nirodha*****) is discussed in the Tenth, Eleventh and Twelfth Cantos but not in the Eighth Canto (*****nirodha*** **is the eighth topic). It is also not possible to match other topics with other cantos successively. Śrīdhara Svāmī says:**

***daśame kṛṣṇa-sat-kīrti-vitānāyopavarṇyate \|***  
***dharma-glāni-nimittas tu nirodho duṣṭa-bhūbhujām \|\|***  
***prākṛtādi-caturdhā yo nirodhaḥ sa tu varṇitaḥ \|***

**In the Tenth Canto in order to spread the pure glory of Kṛṣṇa, destruction (*****nirodha*****) of the evil kings because they caused a decline in dharma is described. The four types of destruction starting with prakṛti were described previously.**

**He states that in the Tenth Canto the main description is that of Kṛṣṇa, the** ***āśraya*****.** ***Daśame daśamaṁ lakṣyam āśritāśraya-vigraham*****: in the Tenth Canto there is a description of the āśraya-vigraha, qualified as the tenth topic. One may find the other topics in many cantos as well.**

**In general, all topics are described in all cantos either secondarily or primarily. That is Śrīdhara Svāmī’s opinion. It has already been stated**

***daśamasya viśuddhy-arthaṁ navānām iha lakṣaṇam \|***  
***varṇayanti mahātmānaḥ śrutenārthena cāñjasā \|\|***

**The great devotees such as Vidura and Maitreya describe properly the nine topics in order to impart the highest knowledge of the tenth topic, through the words of the scripture and stories to illustrate their meaning. SB 2.10.1-2**

**Thus all topics are described in all places. Therefore the First and Second Cantos are included as part of the Bhāgavatam. (They are not excluded in order to match the ten topics). It is concluded there is no sequence in narrating the ten topics.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Generally all topics are in all cantos. *Sarga i*s chiefly in the Second and Third Cantos. *Visarga* is in the Second, Third and Fourth Cantos. *Vṛtti* from kāma is in the Third Canto.

*visasarjātmanaḥ kāyaṁ nābhinandaṁs tamomayam*  
*jagṛhur yakṣa-rakṣāṁsi rātriṁ kṣut-tṛṭ-samudbhavām*

**Not satisfied with his body which became full of ignorance, Brahmā gave up that body. This body transformed into night, which gives rise to hunger and thirst. Yakṣas and Rākṣasas respected the night. SB 3.10.19**

*Vṛtti* according to rules is discussed in the Seventh and Eleventh Cantos when describing varṇāśrama. Rakṣā is discussed in all cantos. *Manvantara* is described in the Eighth Canto. *Vaṁśa* and *vaṁśānucaraitam* are described in the Fourth and Ninth Cantos. *Saṁsthā* is described in the Eleventh and Twelfth Cantos.  *Hetu* is described in the Third and Eleventh Cantos by Kapila and the Lord. *Apāśraya* is discussed primarily in the Tenth Canto.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The ten topics of Bhāgavatam are described in another way: with different names and different meanings to some degree. Some foolish people associate these ten topics of the Purāṇas sequentially with the cantos starting with the Third Canto. The author refutes this view. This disturbs the nature of the Bhāgavatam with Twelve Cantos and eighteen thousand verses (since it omits two cantos). It would not be the complete Bhāgavatam. If one claims that Śukadeva spoke only ten cantos, the first and twelfth would not have any value. The eighteen thousand verses were studied by Śukadeva from his father and recited by him. That is a fact. The information is eternally accomplished. That is also a fact.

### Text 62

*atha sargādīnāṁ lakṣaṇam āha –*  
*avyākṛta-guṇa-kṣobhān mahatas trivṛto’hamaḥ \|*  
*bhūta-mātrendriyārthānāṁ sambhavaḥ sarga ucyate \|\|*  
*pradhāna-guṇa-kṣobhān mahān, tasmāt triguṇo’haṅkāraḥ \| tasmād bhūta-mātrāṇāṁ bhūta-sūkṣmāṇām indriyāṇāṁ ca \| sthūla-bhūtānāṁ ca \| tad-upalakṣita-tad-devatānāṁ ca sambhavaḥ sargaḥ \| kāraṇa-sṛṣṭiḥ sarga ity arthaḥ \|*  
*puruṣānugṛhītānām eteṣāṁ vāsanāmayaḥ \|*  
*visargo’yaṁ samāhāro bījād bījaṁ carācaram \|\|*  
*puruṣaḥ paramātmā \| eteṣāṁ mahad-ādīnāṁ, jīvasya pūrvakarma-vāsanā-pradhāno’yaṁ samāhāraḥ \| kārya-bhūtaś carācara-prāṇi-rūpo bījād bījam iva pravāhāpanno visarga ucyate \| vyaṣṭi-sṛṣṭi-visarga ity arthaḥ \| anenotir ay uktā –*  
*vṛttir bhūtāni bhūtānāṁ carāṇām acarāṇi ca \|*  
*kṛtā svena nṝṇāṁ tatra kāmāc codanayāpi vā \|\|*  
*carāṇāṁ bhūtānāṁ sāmānyato’carāṇi, cakārāc carāṇi ca kāmād vṛttiḥ \| tatra tu nṝṇāṁ svena svabhāvena kāmāc codanayāpi vā yā niyatā vṛttir jīvikākṛtā, sā vṛttir ucyata ity arthaḥ \|*  
*rakṣācyutāvatārehā viśvasyānuyuge yuge \|*  
*tiryaṅ-martyarṣideveṣu hanyante yais trayī-dviṣaḥ \|\|*  
*yair avatāraiḥ \| aneneśakathā \| sthānaṁ poṣaṇaṁ ceti trayam uktam \|*  
*manvantaraṁ manur devā manu-putrāḥ sureśvarāḥ \|*  
*ṛṣayo’ṁśāvatārāś ca hareḥ ṣaḍ-vidham ucyate \|\|*  
*manvādyācaraṇa-kathanena sad-dharma evātra vivakṣita ity arthaḥ \| tataś ca prāktana-granthenaikārthyam \|*  
*rājñāṁ brahma-prasūtānāṁ vaṁśas traikāliko’nvayaḥ \|*  
*vaṁśyānucaritaṁ teṣāṁ vṛttaṁ vaṁśadharāś ca ye \|\|*  
*teṣāṁ rājñāṁ ye ca vaṁśa-dharās teṣāṁ vṛttaṁ vaṁśyānucaritam \|\| 62 \|\|*

**Перевод:**

**The ten topics are described:**

***avyākṛta-guṇa-kṣobhān mahatas tri-vṛto 'hamaḥ***  
***bhūta-sūkṣmendriyārthānāṁ sambhavaḥ sarga ucyate***

**From the agitation of the original modes within the unmanifest material nature, the mahat-tattva arises. From the mahat-tattva comes the element false ego, which divides into three aspects. This threefold false ego further manifests as the tan-mātras, senses and gross elements*****.*** **The generation of all these is called primary creation. SB 12.7.11**

**From the agitation of the guṇas of pradhāna, mahat-tattva arises. From that, the three types of ahaṅkāra arise. From that arise the sense objects or tanmātras (*****bhūta-mātra*****), the senses** ***(indriya)*** **and the elements (*****ārthānām*****). The devatās of the senses are also indicated by this. This creation (*****sambhavaḥ*****) is called sarga or kāraṇa-sṛṣṭi.**

***puruṣānugṛhītānām eteṣāṁ vāsanā-mayaḥ***  
***visargo 'yaṁ samāhāro bījād bījaṁ carācaram***

**The aggregate, the total and individual jīvas, which is effect of the all previous mentioned elements which have been favored by the Lord, which are predominated by impressions of sin and piety, is called visarga. It consists of all moving and non-moving beings who continue like a seed producing a seed. SB 12.7.12**

**The aggregate (*****saṁhāraḥ*****) of mahat-tattva and other elements (*****eteṣām*****), objects of mercy of Paramātmā (*****puruṣānugṛhītānām),*** **filled with impressions of previous karmas of the jīva (*****vāsanā- mayaḥ*****), is called visarga. It is continuous flow of living beings moving and nonmoving which are like seeds which produce more seeds.**

***vṛttir bhūtāni bhūtānāṁ carāṇām acarāṇi ca***  
***kṛtā svena nṛṇāṁ tatra kāmāc codanayāpi vā***

***Vṛtti*** **means the process of sustenance, by which the moving beings live upon the nonmoving beings. For a human,** ***vṛtti*** **specifically means acting for one's livelihood in a manner suited to his personal nature, either by desire (kāma) or according to regulation (codanā). SB 12.7.13**

**Generally nonmoving entities (*****acarāṇi bhūtāni*****) are sustenance for the moving beings (*****carānām bhūtānām*****). The word** ***ca*** **indicates that the moving beings also eat other moving beings out of lust. For humans there is regulated livelihood according to one’s nature** ***(svena*****) out of desire or rules. That is called** ***vṛtti*** **or sustenance. (This is substituted for** ***sthāna*** **or** ***sthiti*** **in the first list.)**

***rakṣācyutāvatārehā viśvasyānu yuge yuge***  
***tiryaṅ-martyarṣi-deveṣu hanyante yais trayī-dviṣaḥ***

**In each age, the infallible Lord’s appearance in this world among the animals, human beings, sages and devatās is called protection of the universe*****.*** **By these incarnations he kills the enemies of Vedic culture. SB 12.7.14**

**The enemies of the Vedas are killed by the avatāras (*****yaiḥ*****). This is the topic** ***īśānukatha.*** **The first line describes** ***poṣaṇam*** **or protection. The previous verse explained** ***sthāna.***

***manvantaraṁ manur devā manu-putrāḥ sureśvarāḥ***  
***rṣayo 'ṁśāvatārāś ca hareḥ ṣaḍ-vidham ucyate***

***Manvantaram*** **means the reign of Manu, during which six types of persons carry out specific duties: the ruling Manu, the devatās, the sons of Manu, Indra, the great sages and the incarnations of the Supreme Lord. SB 12.7.15**

**Previously** ***manvantara*** **was defined as** ***sad-dharma*****. The present verse explains** ***sad-dharma*** **by describing the conduct of Manu and the others listed above. Thus this verse is in agreement with the previous definition.**

***rājñāṁ brahma-prasūtānāṁ vaṁśas trai-kāliko 'nvayaḥ***  
***vaṁśānucaritaṁ teṣām vṛttaṁ vaṁśa-dharās ca ye***

**Dynasties are lines of kings originating with Lord Brahmā and extending continuously through past, present and future. The activities of the prominent members constitute the subject of dynastic history. SB 12.7.16**

**The activities of the kings in the dynastic lines are called** ***vaṁśyānucaritam. Ūti*** **and** ***mukti*** **of the first list are replaced with** ***vaṁśānucaritām*** **and** ***hetu.*****31**

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The characteristics of sarga are described in order. *Trivṛtaḥ* modifies *mahataḥ.* (SB 12.7.11)

*Sāttvikī rājasaś caiva tāmasaś ca tridhā mahān*: mahat-tattva has three types: sattva, rajas and tamas. (Viṣṇu Purānā 1.2.34) Puruṣa is Paramātmā, situated within Brahmā. (SB 12.7.12) The meaning of the other verses is clear.

### Text 63

*naimittikaḥ prākṛtiko nitya ātyantiko layaḥ \|*  
*saṁstheti kavibhiḥ proktaś caturdhāsya svabhāvataḥ \|\|*  
*asya parameśvarasya \| svabhāvataḥ śaktitaḥ \| ātyantika ity anena muktir apy atra praveśitā \|*  
*hetur jīvo’sya sargāder avidyā-karma-kārakaḥ \|*  
*yaṁ cānuśayinaṁ prāhur avyākṛtam utāpare \|\|*  
*hetur nimittam \| asya viśvasya \| yato’yam avidyayā karma-kārakaḥ \| yam eva hetuṁ kecic caitanya-prādhānenānuśayinaṁ prāhuḥ \| apara upādhi-prādhānyenāvyākṛtam iti \|*  
*vyatirekānvayo yasya jāgrat-svapna-suṣuptiṣu \|*  
*māyāmayeṣu tad brahma jīva-vṛttiṣv apāśrayaḥ \|\|*  
*śrī-bādarāyaṇa-samādhi-labdhārtha-virodhād atra ca jīva-śuddha-svarūpam evāśrayatvena na vyākhyāyate kintv ayam evārthaḥ \| jāgrad-ādiṣv avasthāsu, māyāmayeṣu māyā-śakti-kalpiteṣu mahad-ādi-dravyeṣu ca \| kevala-svarūpeṇa vyatirekaḥ parama-sākṣitayānvayaś ca yasya tad brahma jīvānāṁ vṛttiṣu śudda-svarūpatayā sopādhitayā ca vartaneṣu sthitiṣv apāśrayaḥ \| sarvam aty atikramyāśraya ity arthaḥ \| apa ity etat kahlu varjane, varjanaṁ cātikrame paryavasyatīti \| tad evam apāśrayābhivyakti-dvāra-bhūtaṁ hetu-śabda-vyapadiṣṭasya jīvasya śuddha-svarūpa-jñānam āha dvābhyām \|*  
*padārtheṣu yathā dravyaṁ tan-mātraṁ rūpa-nāmasu \|*  
*bījādi-pañcatāṁ tāsu hy avasthāsu yutāyutam \|\|*  
*virameta yadā cittaṁ hitvā vṛtti-trayaṁ svayam \|*  
*yogena vā tad-ātmānaṁ vedehāyā nivartate \|\|*  
*rūpa-nāmātmakeṣu padārtheṣu ghaṭādiṣu yathā dravyaṁ pṛthivyādi yutam ayutaṁ ca bhavati \| kārya-dṛṣṭiṁ vināpy upalambhāt \| tathā tan-mātraṁ śuddhaṁ jīva-caitanya-mātraṁ vastu garbhādhānādi-pañcatāntāsu navasv apy avasthāsv avidyayā yutaṁ svatas tv ayutam iti śuddham ātmānam itthaṁ jñātvā nirviṇṇaḥ sann apāśrayānusandhāna-yogyo bhavatīty āha virameteti \| vṛtti-trayaṁ jāgrat-svapna-suṣupti-rūpam \| ātmānaṁ paramātmānam \| svayaṁ vāsudevāder iva māyāmayatvānusandhānena deva tyāder ivāniṣṭhitena yogena vā \| tataś cehāyās tad-anuśīlana-vyatirikta-ceṣṭāyāḥ \|\| 1.7 \|\| śrī-sūtaḥ \|\| uddiṣṭaḥ sambandhaḥ \|\|63\|\|*

**Перевод:**

***naimittikaḥ prākṛtiko nitya ātyantiko layaḥ***  
***saṁstheti kavibhiḥ proktaś caturdhāsya svabhāvataḥ***

**The learned define** ***saṁsthā*** **as destruction on four levels: occasional, elemental, continuous and ultimate. SB 12.7.17**

**Destruction is of four types from the śakti (*****svabhāvataḥ)*** **of the Lord (*****asy*****a).** ***Ātyantika*** **or ultimate destruction is the same as liberation (the ninth topic in the first list).**

***hetur jīvo 'sya sargāder avidyā-karma-kārakaḥ***  
***yaṁ cānuśāyinaṁ prāhur avyākṛtam utāpare***

**The cause of** ***sarga, visarga*** **of the universe is the jīva whom some call sleeping and some call covered. SB 12.7.18**

**The cause of** ***sarga*** **of the universe** ***(asya*****) is the jīva because he performs actions out of ignorance. Some call this cause** ***(yam*****) sleeping (in Mahāviṣṇu) because prominence of consciousness and others say jīva is invisible or covered because of prominence of upādhis.**

***vyatirekānvayo yasya jāgrat-svapna-suṣuptiṣu***  
***māyā-mayeṣu tad brahma jīva-vṛttiṣv apāśrayaḥ***

**That Brahman which is present in all objects as its witness, in states of waking, dreaming and deep sleep made of māyā, but which is separate from them, is called** ***apāśraya.***

**Because it would be contrary to what Vyāsa realized in samādhi, the pure jīva cannot be described as the shelter in this verse (by taking Brahman to mean the jīva). The meaning is this. In conditions of waking, dreaming and deep sleep produced by māyā-śakti, and in all conditions of māyā, composed of material elements like mahat-tattva** ***(māyā-mayeṣu),*** **Brahman, functioning separately (*****vyatireka*****) as a pure svarūpa or in relationships** ***(anvaya)*** **as the supreme witness, is the shelter for all situations of the jīva functioning either in his pure svarūpa or with upādhis*****. Apāśrayaḥ*** **means the Lord is the shelter surpassing everything else.**  ***Apa*** **means “excluding” and thus means “surpassing.”**

**Then in two verses, the knowledge of the pure svarūpa of the jīva (*****hetu)*****, which arises by the manifestation of** ***apāśraya*** **is explained.**

***padārtheṣu yathā dravyaṁ tan-mātraṁ rūpa-nāmasu***  
***bījādi-pañcatāntāsu hy avasthāsu yutāyutam***

**Just as earth is present in a pot and also exists outside the pot, the pure jīva exists in all states of the body from conception to death or exists without connection to the body. SB 12.7.20**

**Just as materials like earth** ***(dravyam*****) exist whether present or not present (*****yutāyutam*****) in pots or other objects (*****padārtheṣu*****) with names and forms since these can be perceived without the effect (pot), so the pure consciousness of the jīva (*****tan-mātram*****) is joined to the body between birth and death in nine states by ignorance and is disunited from the body without effort. Understanding this nature of the pure jīva, a person becomes detached from the body and becomes qualified for searching out the** ***apāśraya.*** **That is explained in the next verse.**

***virameta yadā cittaṁ hitvā vṛtti-trayaṁ svayam***  
***yogena vā tadātmānaṁ vedehāyā nivartate***

**When the mind, gives up the three states of waking, dreaming and deep sleep by bhakti-yoga*****,*** **and spontaneously enjoys the lotus feet of the Lord, at that time he realizes the Lord and gives up all material desires.**

**The mind, giving up the three states of waking, dreaming and deep sleep (*****vṛtti-trayam*****), becomes detached from matter (*****virameta).*** **It then realizes Paramātmā (*****ātmānam*****) either spontaneously (*****svayam*****) like Vāmadeva, seeing the Lord in various objects in the world, or by performance of bhakti-yoga (*****yogena*****) like Devahūti. The person gives up all actions** ***(ihāyāḥ*****) other than cultivating love of the Lord. Sūta speaks.**

**Thus sambandha has been indicated.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

Characteristics of destruction are discussed in SB 12.7.17. These are described in the fourth chapter of the Twelfth Canto. (SB 12.14.3-38) There is destruction after each Manvantara also.

*vajra uvāca—*  
*manvantare parikṣīṇe yādṛśī dvija jāyate \|*  
*samavasthā mahābhāga tādṛśī vaktumarhasi \|\|*

**Vajra said: O brāhmaṇa filled with good qualities! You should describe the situation when the Manvantara ends.**

*mārkaṇḍeya uvāca—*  
*manvantare parikṣīṇe devā manvantareśvarāḥ \|*  
*maharlokamathāsādya tiṣṭhanti gatakalmaṣāḥ \|\|*  
*manuś ca saha śakreṇa devāś ca yadunandana \|*  
*brahma-lokaṁ prapadyante punar-āvṛtti-durlabham \|\|*  
*ṛṣayaś ca tathā sapta tatra tiṣṭhanti te sadā \|*  
*adhikāraṁ vinā sarve sadṛśāḥ parameṣṭhinaḥ \|\|*  
*bhū-talaṁ sakalaṁ vajra toya-rūpī maheśvaraḥ \|*  
*ūrmi-mālī mahāvegaḥ sarvam āvṛtya tiṣṭhati \|\|*  
*bhūr-lokam āśritaṁ sarvaṁ tadā naśyati yādava \|*  
*na vinaśyanti rājendra viśrutāḥ kula-parvatāḥ \|\|*

**When the Manvantara ends, the devatās, free of sin, who rule the Manvantara, approach Maharloka and stay there. O son of the Yadus! Manu, the devatās along with Indra, go to Brahmaloka which is rare for those who take repeated birth. The seven sages reside there at all times. All of them do not have the qualification of Brahmā. O Vajra! The great Lord in the form of water with great currents covers the whole earth. Everything on earth is destroyed. O Yādava! Only the great famous mountains are not destroyed.**

**Kula-parvata means mountains like Mahendra and Malaya.**

*śeṣaṁ vinaśyati jagat sthāvaraṁ jaṅgamaṁ ca yat \|*  
*naur bhūtvā tu mahī devī tadā yadu-kulodbhava \|\|*  
*dhārayaty atha bījāni sarvāṇy evāviśeṣataḥ \|*  
*ākarṣati tu tāṁ nāvaṁ sthānāt sthānaṁ tu līlayā \|\|*  
*karṣamāṇaṁ tu tāṁ nāvaṁ deva-devaṁ jagat-patim \|*  
*stuvanti ṛṣayaḥ sarva divyaiḥ karmabhir acyutam \|\|*  
*ghūrṇamānas tadā matsyo jala-vegormi-saṅkule \|*  
*ghūrṇamānāṁ tu tāṁ nāvaṁ nayaty amita-vikramaḥ \|\|*  
*himādri-śikhare nāvaṁ baddhvā devo jagat-patiḥ \|*  
*matsyas tv adṛśyo bhavati te ca tiṣṭhanti tatragāḥ \|\|*  
*kṛta-tulyaṁ tadā kālaṁ tāvat prakṣālanaṁ smṛtam \|*  
*āpaḥ śamam atho yānti yathā-pūrvaṁ narādhipa \|*  
*ṛṣayaḥ manuścaiva sarvaṁ kurvanti te tadā \|\|*  
*manvantarānte jagatām avasthā*  
*mayeritā te yadu-vṛnda-nātha \|*  
*ataḥ paraṁ kiṁ tava kīrtanīyaṁ*  
*samāsatas tad vada bhūmi-pāla \|\|*

**All the moving and nonmoving creatures of the universe are destroyed. O descendent of Yadu! The earth becomes a boat and holds all the seeds. The Lord as a pastime pulls the boat from place to place. All the sages praise the Lord of the universe, who never fails to perform attractive actions as he pulls the boat. Matsya circles around in the rough currents, pulling the whirling boat with unlimited prowess to a peak in the Himalayas. He binds the boat to the peak and disappears. The inhabitants of the boat remain there. The purification continues for a period equivalent to Satya-yuga. Then the water recedes and everything returns to the previous state. O king! The sages and Manu then perform all their duties. I have described to you the situation of the universe at the end of the Manvantara. O lord of the Yadus! O king ! Now please say what else should be told to you in brief. \`**

The topic of destruction after every Manvantara is made clear in the Hari-vaṁśa commentary. Thus, in Bhāgavatam, destruction is described after the fifth and sixth Manvantaras.

*cākṣuṣe tv antare prāpte prāk-sarge kāla-vidrute*  
*yaḥ sasarja prajā iṣṭāḥ sa dakṣo daiva-coditaḥ*

**He who, inspired by the Lord, created the desired progeny at the time of Cākṣuṣa Manvantara when the previous creation had been destroyed by time, is known as Dakṣa. SB 4.30.49**

*rūpaṁ sa jagṛhe mātsyaṁ cākṣuṣodadhi-samplave \|*  
*nāvy āropya mahī-mayyām apād vaivasvataṁ manum \|\|*

**When there was an inundation after the period of the Cākṣuṣa Manu the Lord accepted the form of a fish and protected Vaivasvata Manu, placing him on a boat formed from the earth. SB 1.3.15**

In Bhārata-tātparya, Madhvācārya says *manvantara-pralaye matsya-rūpeṇa vidyām adān manave deva-deva:* at the destruction of the Manvatara, the Lord in the form of Matsya gave knowledge.

*sa vā asmat-kulotpannaḥ kalpe 'smin bhārgavarṣabhaḥ*  
*naivādhunāpi bhūtānāṁ samplavaḥ ko 'pi jāyate*

**But this same Mārkaṇḍeya, the foremost descendant of Bhṛgu, took birth in my own family during the current day of Brahmā, and we have not yet seen any total annihilation in this day of Brahmā. SB 12.8.3**

This statement does not accept destruction during Brahmā’s day, and speaks of destruction at the end of the day in the previous verse (SB 12.8.2), since the future Manus remain even during the destruction at the end of the Manvantara.

After the sixth Manvantara, the destruction was different from other destructions since the three worlds were inundated. In the Eighth Canto Matsya says:

*tri-lokyāṁ līyamānāyāṁ saṁvartāmbhasi vai tadā*  
*upasthāsyati nauḥ kācid viśālā tvāṁ mayeritā*

**When all the three worlds merge into the water, a large boat sent by me for you will appear. SB 8.24.33**

*yo 'sāv asmin mahā-kalpe tanayaḥ sa vivasvataḥ*  
*śrāddhadeva iti khyāto manutve hariṇārpitaḥ*

**In this particular destruction, King Satyavrata later became the son of Vivasvān, the king of the sun planet, and was known as Śrāddhadeva. By the mercy of the Supreme Lord, he was given the post of Manu. SB 8.24.11**

*Asmin mahā-kalpa* means “in this great destruction” since it refers to the special destruction at this time by the word *mahā.* According to Amara-koṣa, *kalpa* can mean *pralaya.* This destruction was like the destruction at the end of Brahmā’s day since it covered the three worlds. At that time Brahmā rests in the navel of Viṣṇu for the length of Satya-yuga for it is said that it is the night of Brahma:

*ahaṁ tvām ṛṣibhiḥ sārdhaṁ saha-nāvam udanvati*  
*vikarṣan vicariṣyāmi yāvad brāhmī niśā prabho*

**Pulling the boat, with you and all the sages in it, O King, I shall travel in the water of devastation until the night of Brahmā is over. SB 8.24.37**

When the three worlds are inundated, deva and asuras who have not concluded their period of enjoyment enter the boat. Matsya says to Satyavrata:

*tvaṁ tāvad oṣadhīḥ sarvā bījāny uccāvacāni ca*  
*saptarṣibhiḥ parivṛtaḥ sarva-sattvopabṛṁhitaḥ*

**Thereafter, you should collect all types of herbs and seeds of higher and lower species, and accompanied by the seven sages and surrounded by all kinds of living entities, you should get aboard that huge boat. SB 8.24.34**

Since it is proved that there is destruction at the end of the Manvantara, it is classed as one of the four types of destruction. It is daily or naimittika (like the destruction at the end of Brahmā’s day). There are other sudden destructions also described such as that at the beginning of creation during Svāyambhuva Manvantara and during the sixth Manvantara when Hiraṇyaksa was killed. Both those inundations are merged in one story in the Third Canto since the pastime is similar. Sometimes the events of the Pādma-kalpa and Brāhma-kalpa are also mixed. Brāhma-kalpa is the first day in Brahmā’s life. Pādma-kalpa is the last day in the first half of Brahmā’s life. 32

*nirodho ’syānuśayanam ātmanaḥ saha śaktibhiḥ*

***Nirodha*** **refers to the merging of the jīva along with his material identity into the Lord at the time of final devastation of the universe. SB 2.10.6**

This description from of destruction from the Second Canto is general since it does not include the nitya pralaya, or continual destruction of living entities’ bodies.

The work is concluded. Sambandha has been indicated. This means that a general indication of the supreme entity has been given. Bhagavān who is the topic of discussion in the scriptures (*sambandhi*) is discussed in six ways, but these have not been described.  *Upakrama* and *upasaṁhāra* (introduction and conclusion) are seen in *vedyaṁ vāstavam atra vastu* (SB 1.1.2) *Sarva-vedānta-sāram* (SB 12. 13.12) indicates *abhyāsa* (repetition*). Atra sargaḥ* (SB 2.10.1) indicates *apūrvatā* (uniqueness). *Vadanti tat tattva-vidaḥ* (SB 1.2.11) indicates *arthavāda* (praise) since this topic is not seen in other scriptures. *Śivadaṁ tāpa-trayomūlanam* (SB 1.1.2) indicates *phala* (result)*. Daśamasya viśuddhyartham* (SB 2.10.20) indicates *upapatti* (reasoning).

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

In the prevous list from the Second Canto, liberation or mukti was one of the items. Mukti, called *ātyantika-laya,* is included in the four types of destruction in this list. The sleeping jīva refers to the jīva endoewed with the remainder of unexperienced karmas. Earth becomes a pot endowed with name and shape (*rūpa*). The elements can be perceived without the presence of the effect, the pot. Understanding ātmā and becoming detached, the person becomes qualified for meditating on the Lord. Vāmadeva saw Paramātmā when he was in the womb. Devahūti performed bhakti-yoga.

*Iti śrī-kali-yuga-pāvaṇa-sva-bhajana-vibhājana-prayojanāvatāra-śrī-śrī-bhagavat-krṣṇa-caitanya-deva-caraṇānucara-viśva-vaiṣṇava-rāja-sabhājana-bhājana-śrī-rūpa-sanātanānuśāsana-bhāratī-garbhe śrī-bhagavata-sandarbhe tattva-sandarbho nāma prathamaḥ sandarbhaḥ*

**Here ends Tattva Sandarbha, the first among the Sandarbhas which use Bhāgavatam as their source, and which arise from the expert instructions of Rūpa and Sanātana Gosvāmīs, most revered members of the assembly of the best Vaiṣṇavas in the universe, following the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa Caitanya-deva, who descended to distribute his worship in order to purify Kali-yuga.**

**Комментарий. Автор Jīva Gosvāmī:**

*Iti* indicates the conclusion. *Vibhajana* means giving. Rūpa and Sanātana are the objects *(bhājana*) of respect (*sabhājana*) in the assembly *(sabhā*) of the best (*raja*) of Vaiṣṇavas in the universe (*viśva*). The Sandarbhas arise from the expert words of instruction or orders (*anuśāsana-bhāratī-garbhe*) of Rūpa and Sanātana.

**Комментарий. Автор Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa:**

The Sandarbhas contain words (*bhāratī*) of instruction (*anuśāsana*) of Rūpa and Sanātana, who are the objects of worship (*sa-bhājana*) in the royal assembly of Vaiṣṇavas in the universe and who followed the feet of Kṛṣṇa Caitanya, purifier of Kali-yuga, who appared as avatāra with the goal of spreading *(vibhajana*) his worship.

Vidyā-bhūṣaṇa has written this commentary on Tattva Sandarbha, concerning with text written by Jīva Gosvāmī. May it be purified by the devotees.
